CONTEXT.  The thesis ("Semantic Space Abstractions for Partially Observable
Deterministic Task and Motion Planning", 76 pp, submitted 2026-06-10) was graded
1.3.  Supervisors (Till Hofmann, Daniel Swoboda) did not run the code; they judged
the writing good but noted the work is honest about missing pieces.  Plan: turn it
into an ICAPS 2027 paper (conference June-July 2027, USA; SUBMISSION DEADLINE
DECEMBER 2026).  Division of labour: the supervisors write the paper text, the
author (Hani) does the code.  The missing pieces below come from the supervisor
debrief (2026-08), roughly in execution order.

NUMBERING.  Workstreams are #P1-#P10.  Old issues are cited as CB#N (archived
CODEBASE_AUDIT_FINAL.txt) and TH#N (archived THESIS_AUDIT_FINAL.md).  Work through
this file with the /workflow skill: one issue per turn, before/after preview,
explicit approval, individual commits.  STATUS: no marker = OPEN; [DONE]/[REJECTED]
on the header line when resolved.  [REMARK] items are recorded decisions, not tasks.

POLICY (carried over from CODEBASE_AUDIT.txt, still binding)
================================================================================

SHELL   Windows PowerShell only.  No bash heredocs for commits; plain
        `git commit -m "msg"`.  One-line commit subjects, no body.

REMOTES Push meaningful work to BOTH hosts: `git push github <branch>` (github.com
        hanihabobo-dot/pybullet) and `git push origin <branch>`
        (git.rwth-aachen.de hani.alassiri.alhabboub/pybullet).

CODE    Commented-out code is intentional documentation/toggles: never delete
        without explicit approval, move to archive/ instead.  Dead methods/imports
        are kept on purpose (some are needed for exactly this paper's perception
        work).  Pure-narration comments stay.

WIKI    Project wiki (pybullet.wiki.git, clone at ../pybullet.wiki/) must be
        updated after any change to architecture, data structures, algorithms,
        PDDL domain, execution pipeline, or design decisions.

ARCHIVE archive/ is tracked and pushed; no personal information in it.
        "emails and pdfs/" is private: never git add it; stage paths explicitly.

ENV     wsl_env/ is the WSL venv (gitignored): do not delete.  PDDLStream is a
        sibling checkout ../pddlstream_lib.  Launch wrappers (run_boxels,
        run_eval, plot_eval) live in the user's PowerShell $PROFILE, not the repo.
        Always give the user the --vis 1 GUI command alongside any headless run.

PLANNER PDDLStream plans against a separate DIRECT PyBullet client
        (boxel_env.plan_client_id); the planner is stateless, (boxel_fits ?o ?b)
        init facts are rebuilt from the BoxelRegistry every replan.

PLOTS   New plots add, never replace; old plots must stay regenerable; read new
        PNGs visually before committing.

GIT     Multi-commit refactors go on a branch off main; single-commit fixes on
        main.  No worktrees.  Pre-staged files in the index ride along with the
        next commit; do not unbundle.

================================================================================
PROJECT PRIMER (distilled from the archived audits; read this first)
================================================================================

WHAT THE SYSTEM IS.  A tabletop TAMP pipeline (PDDLStream + Fast Downward +
PyBullet, Franka Panda) for partially observable deterministic (POD) planning.
The contribution is the representation: an adaptive semantic partition of the
workspace into "Boxels" (object AABBs, occlusion-shadow volumes cast from a fixed
camera, octree-partitioned free space with face-merge), which makes occlusion
explicit symbolic state (Know-If fluents, blocks_view_at) so a classical planner
can search for hidden objects via optimistic determinization + replanning.
Baseline comparison: uniform voxel grid (~10x more cells/facts, worse success);
architectural comparison: TAMPURA (Curtis et al. 2024, arXiv:2403.10454).

KEY FILES (root):  boxel_env.py (env, scenes, camera, oracle, boxelization),
shadow_calculator.py, free_space.py, cell_merger.py, uniform_grid.py (baseline),
pddlstream_planner.py (+ pddl/), streams.py (grasp/kin/motion streams),
execution.py (pick/place/stack/sense execution), robot_utils.py, belief.py,
reboxelize.py, test_full_pipeline.py (main loop + eval verifiers), eval_runner.py,
eval_plotter.py, run_logger.py.  tampura_bridge/ runs OUR pipeline inside
TAMPURA's find_dice env (see #P4).  A src/ package refactor is planned but gated
(multi-commit branch, do only when the tree is otherwise quiet).

EVAL DATA.  Canonical headline dataset: eval_results/sweep_full_2026-05-28
(post CB#107 replan-cap removal; 810 cells = 3 goals x 3 variants x 30 seeds x 3
difficulty; 1800 s budget).  Thesis headline numbers (tab:headline): success
find-and-tray-stack 75.6/72.2/34.4 %, holding 65.6/66.7/47.8 %, stack
64.4/64.4/35.6 % (semantic / +mbs0.05 / uniform); stack-by-height semantic
100/80/13.3 % at h=2/3/4 (uniform 90/16.7/0).  NOTE: EVAL_SUMMARY.md documents
the OLDER sweep_anytime dataset (4500-row aggregated.csv, pre-cap-removal
numbers 42.3 % holding etc.) and is stale relative to the thesis; do not quote
it for the paper without saying which sweep it is.  A fresh full canonical sweep
(CB#113) is planned and should only run AFTER #P1-#P3 land, since those change
the system under test.

TAMPURA COMPARISON STATE.  TAMPURA source lives in WSL:
\\wsl.localhost\Ubuntu\root\tampura-work  (tampura/ core lib + tampura_environments/
incl. find_dice/ and panda_utils/; their .venv is python 3.11 / pybullet 3.2.7).
Local 20-seed TAMPURA find_dice re-run (runs/sweep_2026-05-26.json in WSL):
11/20 = 55 % success, 166 +/- 85 s per successful episode (published-implied
>= 63 %; published per-step 57 +/- 38 s, ours ~11 s/step).  The thesis frames this
comparison as INDIRECT and architectural (different task, codebase; local re-run
possibly disadvantages TAMPURA, TH#306).  Their hiding mechanic is CONTAINMENT
(die pose-aliased under a cup) vs our LATERAL shadows; their spawns are
hand-tuned reachable (x in [0.3,0.7], y in [-0.4,0.4], camera-visibility checked,
scene regenerated until the die is genuinely hidden); their controllers are
kinematic (motion via bundled BiRRT; grasp library: saved / antipodal / top).

KNOWN FAILURE TAXONOMY (thesis §5): timed out 24 %, no plan found (dominant on
stack-uniform), search exhausted (holding-only; 13/18 from axis-aligned shadow
under-coverage, TH#335), false success (physics check catches toppled tower /
fake grasp; holding-only, which is itself suspicious, CB#122).

BRANCHES THAT MATTER:  audit-14-dense-lattice-attempt (64-ray visibility lattice,
works, ~3x planning cost), audit-51-shadow-blocker-fix (principled blocker
re-grounding, reverted), audit-55-pose-aware-stack (destination-aware stack
kinematics, works, ~7x planning cost), audit-64-tampura-binary-grid.

NOTES FOLDER.  notes/*.md (THESIS_NOTES.md, TAMPURA_PLAN.md, ...) were removed
from the working tree in 2026-08 cleanup; recover via git history if a reference
below points there.

================================================================================
#P1. [OPEN T0]  REMOVE THE CONSTRAINT-WELD GRASP AND ALL ORACLE READS; BUILD A
     REAL PERCEPTION SYSTEM AND A REAL POSE SAMPLER
================================================================================
Supervisor wording: no workarounds and cuts any more.  The ONLY thing that may
stay is the hardcoded lift after a pick or place action (collision-avoidance aid).

WHAT MUST GO (full inventory, from the 2026-08-15 code scout):

  Grasp / release cheats:
  (a) execute_pick weld: p.createConstraint(JOINT_FIXED) attaches the object to
      the EE after gripper close (execution.py:775-780; doc at :655 calls it an
      accepted simplification).  The archived audits are explicit that this is
      "a telekinetic attachment that bypasses contact physics" (deferred #59) and
      that close_gripper is "purely cosmetic", the constraint provides ALL grip
      stability (deferred #81, commit 8774e6f).
  (b) release teleport: open_gripper uses p.resetJointState straight to max
      finger opening because POSITION_CONTROL could not physically free objects
      (deferred #81, commit 65146ad).  A real release must work through the motor.
  (c) bridge mirror of the weld: tampura_bridge/execution_boxel.py:94-107 (_weld),
      plus _held_offset ground-truth read (:82-91).  Whatever replaces (a) must be
      mirrored there (see #P4).
  (d) legacy tampura_bridge/execution_bridge.py: full pose-teleport pick/place
      (resetBasePositionAndOrientation at :34, :152).  Dead code (boxel_policy
      imports execution_boxel, not this).  Move to archive/ per policy.
      [(c)+(d) OBE 2026-08-20: the whole tampura_bridge/ tree was discarded to
      archive/tampura_bridge_v1/ — see the #P4 decision note.  No bridge mirror
      exists to maintain until the bridge restart.]

  Oracle / ground-truth reads feeding planning or perception:
  (e) boxel_env.oracle_detect_objects (boxel_env.py:1620-1682): reads exact poses
      via p.getBasePositionAndOrientation (:1650) and decides visibility by
      8-AABB-corner rayTestBatch matched against true body ids (:1659-1675).
      Called unconditionally from get_camera_observation (:1542-1589, both GUI
      and DIRECT paths); its output feeds the BoxelRegistry that planner and
      executor consume (test_full_pipeline.py:586-599).  THE central target.
  (f) RGB-D is already rendered but discarded: getCameraImage at :1560-1572;
      _depth_to_point_cloud exists (:1597-1618) but is commented out at
      :1573-1578 ("Re-enable if a real perception pipeline replaces the oracle").
  (g) sense_shadow_raycasting (execution.py:63-162): the PDDL sense action casts
      147 rays and classifies hits by comparing hit_obj_id against the KNOWN
      target/occluder body ids (:142-149).  compute_shadow_blockers (:165) same
      pattern.  A real sense must classify from rendered observation, not ids.
  (h) boxelization from privileged geometry: generate_boxels reads p.getAABB
      (boxel_env.py:1361); shadow_calculator.py raycasts true collision geometry
      (:127); free_space.py partitions from those AABBs.  Once perception
      supplies estimated poses/extents, these consume estimates instead.
  (i) planning-time ground truth: streams.py reads live p.getAABB at :384, :456,
      :1169, :1178-1193 (compute_stack_kin reads the support's true top surface).
      Execution-time ground truth: execute_place/execute_stack read live AABBs
      and poses to compute drop heights (execution.py:850-865, :1023-1037).
      Precedent from deferred #57: URDF-known SIZE is legitimate design-time
      knowledge (put it in an object_geometry dict on the registry); sensed
      POSITION is not.
  (j) sync_to_plan_client (boxel_env.py:669-702) mirrors exact GUI-world poses
      into the planning client every replan; the planner literally plans against
      ground truth.  Post-place/stack snap syncs of the plan-client shadow copy
      (execution.py:950-956, :1144-1150).  With perception, the plan client must
      be built from the estimated state.  (The plan-client MECHANISM stays; what
      changes is the source of poses.)
  (k) bridge perception adapter: segment_visible (perception_adapter.py:104-119)
      is already real segmentation-based VISIBILITY, but poses/extents still come
      from pbu.get_pose/get_aabb ground truth (:121-146, :170-175) and
      oracle_detect_objects is reused verbatim (:177-188); boxelize.py:43 reads
      true AABBs.

  Pose sampler:
  (l) sample_grasp yields exactly one grasp: fixed top-down orientation at a
      single hardcoded z-offset (_GRASP_Z_OFFSETS=[0.10], streams.py:532-577).
      No shape awareness, no side/angled grasps, no width reasoning.  "A good
      pose sampler" replaces this (TAMPURA's get_grasp library, saved/antipodal/
      top modes, is a working reference implementation; see #P5).

  ALLOWED TO STAY:  _apply_post_action_lift (execution.py:615-635; ~5 cm after
  pick :792, ~10 cm after place :941 and stack :1136).  Explicitly exempted.

  SCOPE DECISIONS RECORDED (2026-08-15):
  - The separate pre-release 15 mm lift in execute_place (execution.py:890-911,
    audit #85 wedge fix) is NOT obviously covered by the exemption ("lift AFTER a
    pick or place").  Expectation: a real friction grasp removes the tilt wedge
    that motivated it; try removing it with the weld, keep only if physically
    justified, and disclose either way.
  - Eval-harness ground-truth VERIFIERS stay: _verify_cube_on / _verify_holding /
    _verify_goal_physics and the Phase-4 "ORACLE ONLY" hidden-target setup block
    (test_full_pipeline.py:144, :221, :277, :634-696).  Scoring/logging ground
    truth in simulation is legitimate; the robot must not read it.

  KNOWN BLOCKER FOR A REAL GRASP (deferred #77) — ERRATUM 2026-08-20: the
  deferred-#77 size inventory (0.15 m occluders / 0.08 m targets) is STALE;
  fix #17 had already halved the preset scenes before the thesis eval.
  Code-verified today: default_scene occluders 0.075 m cubes / targets 0.04 m;
  stack_scene 0.05 m; mixed_shapes all < 0.08 m; ObjectSpec.lateral_friction
  already defaults to 1.0.  The remaining ungraspable geometry is
  scalability_scene (and random_pairs_scene on top of it — the eval sweep's
  scenes): occluder half-extents draw uniform(0.030, 0.045) -> 0.06-0.09 m
  full width, the upper half of the range over the 0.08 m finger opening;
  default_scene's 0.075 m occluders leave only 2.5 mm descent clearance per
  side, too tight for a friction grasp under position-control error.  The #77
  proposal still applies, narrowed: occluders become tall narrow blocks
  (~0.05-0.06 m wide, 0.10-0.15 m tall — occlusion improves with height),
  targets shrink slightly so the hidden-placement jitter window (occ_half -
  target_half) stays positive.  [LANDED 2026-08-20, branch
  p1-real-grasp-perception: scalability xy_half uniform(0.025,0.030) /
  z_half uniform(0.050,0.075), targets uniform(0.015,0.020); default_scene
  occluders 0.06 m wide x 0.075 m tall; finger-pad lateralFriction 1.2.]
  This changes every seeded scene and therefore every number: plan the
  canonical re-sweep (CB#113) AFTER this lands.

  STARTING POINTS.  Perception: segment_visible (was the bridge's; now at
  archive/tampura_bridge_v1/perception_adapter.py, reference only) for
  visibility; the
  commented-out depth/point-cloud path (f) for geometry; PyBullet's segmentation
  buffer gives object masks, from which per-object point clouds give estimated
  AABBs/poses (sim-grade "real perception": pixels in, poses out, no body-id
  reads).  Visibility density tradeoff is measured on branch
  audit-14-dense-lattice-attempt (64-ray lattice, ~3x planning cost).  Grasping:
  friction-grasp design sketch in deferred #59; belief-update honesty gaps to
  keep in mind: deferred #51 (stale blocker fallback; principled fix on branch
  audit-51-shadow-blocker-fix, reverted), #47 (blocks_view_at re-grounding,
  deferred out of thesis scope: reconsider here).

  STEP-1 FIELD BUGS F1-F4 (2026-08-20, GUI verification after step (1) landed;
  one diagnostic agent per bug; full reports with log-line evidence in
  archive/p1_field_reports_2026-08-20/; all four FIXED 2026-08-20 on this
  branch — see the [DONE] markers and the F4 fix note):
  F1. [DONE 2026-08-20] PHANTOM FIRST PICK (grip_loss.md): the Panda's fingers SPAWN CLOSED
      (panda.urdf finger lower=0; loadURDF zeroes joints; nothing initialises
      them — the defensive open_gripper was removed in audit #37/#38 on the
      false "init = open" assumption).  The first descent grinds the closed
      fingertips into the object top (+6.8 mm arrival stall), grip
      verification passes on link identity alone (both tips pressing DOWN
      count as "both pads in contact"; the 500-800 N readings are servo
      grind, NOT close transients — genuine grasps read 2x40 N), the object
      never moves, and the phantom hold survives to a place/stack that
      releases nothing (table-height places even pass drop-verify with
      err=0).  First pick only: any open_gripper leaves fingers at 0.04 for
      the rest of the episode.  FIX: (a) resetJointState fingers to 0.04 in
      _setup_scene after robot load (root cause, one line); (b) re-verify pad
      contact after the post-pick lift, abort to replan on loss; (c) held-
      contact entry assert in execute_place/execute_stack with its OWN
      failure path (the dispatcher's fingers_open classifier would misfile
      it); (d) aperture plausibility in grip verification
      (|finger_pos - cube_hw| <= ~6 mm; phantom reads ~0.00).
  F2. [DONE 2026-08-20] MOVED OCCLUDER NEVER RE-BLOCKS + PICK HAS NO GIVE-UP (pick_giveup.md):
      _build_init emits blocks_view_at hardcoded to the occluder's ORIGINAL
      boxel id (pddlstream_planner.py:439-444) and stops emitting
      obj_at_boxel for moved occluders, so the blocks_view derived predicate
      can never re-fire after a relocation — the planner believes the shadow
      clear, sense hits the real occluder identically each time, and the
      audit-#21 3-strike giveup burns the shadow.  This is the concrete
      #47/#51 mechanism.  Separately, pick failures have NO strike counter: a
      toppled block (unpickable by the single no-yaw grasp) loops
      byte-identical retries until the process is killed.  FIX: key
      blocks_view_at to the CURRENT boxel (moved_occluders.get(id, id), ~one
      line); add a pick strike counter mirroring blocked_counts; optionally
      gate on cube_tilt_deg in _release_and_verify_drop (already computed,
      never checked).  Tier 2 = step-(4) sampler: yaw 0/90 candidates from
      the live AABB, finger-width-validated before yield.
  F3. [DONE 2026-08-20] DEGENERATE/INVERTED SHADOWS for tall occluders near the camera
      (missing_shadow.md): back-corner and dominant-axis selection use the
      full 3D camera->object direction (shadow_calculator.py:81-110,
      :184-194); for a tall close occluder the downward component dominates,
      the shadow AABB collapses to the occluder footprint and the
      object-subtraction INVERTS it (observed shadow_of_red_object extent.z
      = -8.7e-6 m; wireframe drawn 17 um tall, label unaffected — hence
      "label without box" in the GUI).  test_boxel_fits is False on negative
      extents, so a target hidden behind such an occluder is STRUCTURALLY
      UNFINDABLE — a planner gap introduced by the tall-block resize, not
      cosmetics.  FIX: compute shadow direction/back corners in the
      horizontal plane only; add a degenerate-extent guard on the
      single-AABB path (shadow_calculator.py:248-255) mirroring the
      two-slab-carve guard.
  F4. [DONE 2026-08-20] STACK h=4 IK-INFEASIBLE AT THE FK GATE + SHADOWS NEVER RETIRED
      (stale_shadow_drop.md): the level-4 approach (z ~0.60 at 0.61 m
      horizontal, single top-down grasp at +0.10) sits at the Panda's reach
      margin where 100-iteration IK converges no closer than ~18 mm, so the
      #P1 10 mm FK gate rejects all 8 seeds on every replan -> "no plan
      found".  The observed "drop onto the old shadow" was the audit-#58
      release-in-place fallback hovering over the STALE plan-time stack
      config (coincidence; init-fact deltas prove the shadow poisoned
      nothing).  Underlying hygiene gap is real though: shadows are retired
      ONLY by sense actions — no path removes a shadow when its caster is
      picked/stacked (refresh_object_aabbs skips shadows by design;
      reboxelize treats them as permanent), so stack runs accumulate stale
      shadows in registry AND viz.  FIX: add a lower approach offset to
      _GRASP_Z_OFFSETS (e.g. [0.10, 0.06]); IK refinement (re-seed from own
      output / more iterations) before BOTH FK gates; execute_stack retry
      seeded from REST_POSES when the plan-config seed fails; retire the
      stacked/placed cube's shadow boxels after the OBJECT refresh
      (mirror the discovery-cleanup pattern), same in the place handler and
      release_held_object_in_place.
      [FIX NOTE 2026-08-20: retirement landed in all three sites but GATED
      on "no active search" (goal_kind == stack, or target already found).
      Ungated retirement in the place handler would delete the very shadow
      the plan senses right after relocating its occluder — the F2
      mechanism requires unsensed shadows to survive relocation, else a
      hidden target becomes structurally unfindable.  Deviation from the
      blanket proposal above, reason stated here.]
      [POST-FIX h=4 STATUS 2026-08-20 (run_2026-08-20_21-29-11, headless
      seed 0): the FK-gate dead-end is GONE — plan #1 certifies all three
      stack kins and the level-4 release lands at bottom_z err 0.1 mm.
      NEW FINDING for #P2(b): at the level-4 release the opened finger
      pad stays in light contact with the stacked cube (link 10, ~1.9-2.4
      N, penetration -0.12 mm) because the wrist is at its reach margin —
      drop-verify gate (iv) zero-robot-contact fails 3x while gates
      (i)-(iii)+(v) all pass, the retries nudge the cube (tilt 2.3->8.8
      deg), it topples when the arm departs, and the re-pick of the
      toppled cube fails (F2 strike counter fires, honest no-plan exit in

      concrete numbers: the h=4 residual is a PHYSICAL manipulation
      failure at the reach margin, not planning.  Candidate remedy for
      #P2: run the (allowed) post-stack lift before the verify gate, or

      [REVIEW PASS 2026-08-20: 5-lens adversarial review of the four fix

      [POST-FIX SMOKE NOTES 2026-08-21: (1) F3's corrected (larger)

      orange_object casts 7), inflating grounding and sampling —

      [SIBLING BATCH-SENSE 2026-08-21 (user-directed): after F3 one
      caster casts several shadow fragments, so a clear_but_empty sense
      removed only ITS fragment and the GUI kept showing the caster's
      other shadows ("shadow still there after sensing" report; the
      per-fragment removal itself was verified intact — is_shadow 6->5
      across the sense in run_2026-08-21_09-09-27).  handle_sense_action
      now re-casts the caster's remaining fragments after a

      [2026-08-21 ROOT CAUSE + FINAL USER DECISION (supersedes the
      retirement notes above): the persistent "sense doesn't remove the
      shadow" reports were NOT a removal bug — the sense classifier
      (byte-identical to main) flags still_blocked on ANY occluder or
      robot ray hit, and P1's tall occluders + F3's full-size fragments
      make 1-3 % marginal ray clips routine (runs 09-09-27 / 10-19-02:
      senses stuck at 1 % / 3 % blocked, so the clear_but_empty removal
      path never fired at all).  FIX: up to 5 % blocked rays are now
      tolerated and the sense classifies by the remaining rays (genuine
      blockages measure 12-100 %); disclosed cost: a target hiding
      exactly behind the tolerated rays is missed by that sense.
      retire_cast_shadows REMOVED WHOLESALE per explicit user
      instruction (both hygiene rounds reverted, incl. the belief
      'retired' status; stack-goal runs accumulate their spawn-time
      shadows again — accepted; the code survives in git history,
      commits 27a7fda..f66b3a9).  The sibling batch-sense stays (user
      choice) and now actually fires, since senses reach
      clear_but_empty again.]
      [PICK RELIABILITY + PARTIAL SHRINK 2026-08-21 (5-agent
      investigation synthesis, wf_f736bb62: removal machinery was
      "starved, not broken" — ~55 % pick failures from stale-position
      descents after transit shoves, ~25 % the already-fixed marginal
      veto, rest stale geometry + wall-clock plan divergence; overlay
      and physics exonerated, physics bit-identical GUI vs headless):
      (1) execute_pick re-aims the final descent at the object's LIVE
      pose (the dispatcher position was 10-28 mm stale in the GUI field
      runs — 7 of 9 picks failed single-pad); (2) pre-grasp aperture
      sized to the object (max horizontal half-extent + 8 mm) instead

  CB#113 LAUNCHED 2026-09-20 15:34 on commit 430a214, from its own
  immutable snapshot (Semantic_Boxels_sweep_cb113), 2160 cells,
  --timeout 1800 --skip-existing, output eval_results/sweep_cb113 in
  the main repo with the commit recorded in
  eval_results/sweep_cb113.commit.  It runs for a day or more and is
  resumable: re-running the same command skips banked cells.  WHILE IT
  RUNS, any other pipeline run on this machine contends for CPU, and
  pddlstream's budgets are wall-clock, so a GUI run alongside it will
  change both its own outcome and the sweep's — serialise, or accept
  that those cells are noisier than the rest.
  HOST SLEEP IS A HAZARD, found the hard way on the first evening: the
  machine idle-slept about four minutes after the sweep started and the
  sweep made no progress for five hours.  Two clocks disagree across a
  suspend and that is what makes it confusing to diagnose — the
  runner's per-cell timeout is a MONOTONIC deadline, which does not
  advance while the host is asleep, so the cell was not killed; but
  pddlstream's own "Time:" counter is wall-clock, so its log read
  18550 s while the process had used only 766 s of CPU.  A cell caught
  by a sleep therefore either runs far past its wall-clock budget or,
  if the runner does time it out, banks a "timeout" row that is not
  data.  Hold the machine awake for the duration, and afterwards run
  tools/_sweep_drop_stalled_cells.py <sweep dir> --apply, which deletes
  any cell whose wall clock exceeds 1.5x the timeout so that a resumed
  sweep re-runs it.
  EXPECTED DURATION, measured on the first 16 cells: an ordinary cell
  takes 5 to 26 s, so 2160 of them is about 11 hours of real work, but
  every cell that hits the 1800 s cap adds half an hour.  Budget two to
  three days unless the cap is lowered.  Lowering --timeout is a data
  decision, not a convenience one: the design note above says to run
  generous and post-process aggregated.csv at any shorter bound.]  (2) all "deterministic re-roll to 343879192"-era
  expectations (F5 notes, smoke lists) are historical; seed 999 now
  = loud rejection, full stop; (3) the /eval skill and
  tools/_eval_ab.py defaults updated — --seed-retry only ever
  existed 2026-08-21..23 and old-ref eval arms inside that window
  are the only place it may still be passed.  NEW COMPANION FLAG
  --allow-unwinnable (same day, user request): inspection mode that
  downgrades the CLASSIFICATION rejections (F5 sensable, role
  split) to loud warnings and runs the episode anyway, so a
  rejected seed can be confirmed unwinnable by eye in the GUI;
  construction failures still abort (no scene to show); never for
  eval sweeps.]

  F7. [ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED 2026-09-19 — analyst over four
      binding-death logs (10-29-51 #4, 22-12-06 #4, 17-14-10 #8,
      10-06-42 #4): the dead stream is ALWAYS compute-kin(obj, free_XXX,
      g) where obj was relocated in an EARLIER plan and free_XXX is the
      destination recorded in belief.occluders_moved.  That cell no
      longer exists: update_after_place removes the consumed free cell
      (boxel_data.py) and re-poses the object's OWN boxel to the placed
      pose, and the next reboxelize would drop it anyway — yet
      _build_init asserted (Boxel free_XXX) (obj_at_boxel obj free_XXX)
      from the moved record, FastDownward grounded pick(obj, free_XXX,
      g, q), and compute_kin_solution returned SILENTLY on
      registry.get_boxel(free_XXX) is None (every other early exit logs).
      Re-picks inside one plan (place then pick from the same cell in
      the same skeleton) DID bind — "pick from a cell placed in an
      earlier plan" was the trigger, not re-picking as such, and not the
      one-output grasp generator.  The 2x-per-iteration wall-clock
      blow-up is a BUSY-WAIT in pddlstream, not FastDownward: with every
      remaining binding STANDBY (check_downstream False, skeleton.py
      process_root re-pushes STANDBY bindings; the "TODO: if readd ==
      STANDBY" at :346), timed_process spins the whole
      search_sample_ratio-sized budget with zero stream calls — 10-29-51
      #4 spent ~100 of its 164 s spinning; 10-06-42 #4 90 %.  FIX:
      _build_init locates every registered object at its own boxel
      (obj_at_boxel o o) — the moved record is a belief annotation
      only — and compute_kin prints "[F7] ... boxel not in the registry"
      instead of returning silently; the blocks_view_at facts are keyed
      to the occluder's own boxel too (they had switched to the same
      dead cell, which the derived blocks_view could never match against
      obj_at_boxel).  REMEDY 3 ADOPTED the same evening: solve() now
      passes search_sample_ratio=0.25 (SEARCH_SAMPLE_RATIO; pddlstream's
      default 1) after the tray-stack smoke of the round-2 code hit a
      900 s cap in plan 6 on a genuinely unbindable skeleton (blue had
      been gripped rotated and stacked on its side; the observation
      rejected the stack, and the next skeleton could not bind): the
      STANDBY spin is the pddlstream busy-wait, and a quarter of the
      budget keeps every binding's one call per iteration while cutting
      the spin fourfold.  ISOLATED A/B 2026-09-20 (commit 7ec8574, the
      ONLY difference between the arms is --search-sample-ratio, so this
      measures the parameter and nothing else):
        seeds   library 1.0        pipeline 0.25
        0-19    15/20  8.3 s mean  16/20  5.1 s mean
        100-119 15/20  5.5 s mean  15/20  7.5 s mean
        total   30/40              31/40
      Success is identical within the measured +/-1-2 noise floor, and
      the mean planning time is a wash across the two blocks, so the
      fear that a quarter budget starves bindable skeletons is NOT
      borne out: it costs no seed either block.  What it does buy is the
      worst case — seed 13, the crowded-table dead end, spends 72 s
      spinning at 1.0 and 29 s at 0.25 for the same honest no-plan (and
      4 s in an earlier run of the same config, so the spin's cost is
      itself highly variable).  DECISION: 0.25 stays the default, the
      flag stays for anyone who wants the library behaviour.  Caveat
      worth carrying into the paper: the ratio also changes which
      FAILURE LABEL a dead end gets, since the binding-death classifier
      keys on whether the summary reports skeletons, so failure-mode
      tables are only comparable within one ratio.
      STRONGER EVIDENCE than the A/B, from the round-3 review's reading
      of the vendored library and of the accused logs: the parameter is
      consumed at exactly one place (focused.py's allocated_sample_time
      = ratio * search_time - sample_time) and bounds exactly one call,
      skeleton.py's timed_process, which IS the STANDBY busy-wait.  The
      guaranteed one-call-per-binding pass is greedily_process plus
      process_complexity and NEITHER takes a time budget, so shrinking
      the ratio cannot starve a bindable skeleton; all three claims in
      the call-site comment check out against the library source.  And
      the three seeds that looked like a regression never reached that
      code at all: every failing plan() ends "sample_time: 0.000,
      skeletons: 0, solved: False, timeout: False" against max_time
      1800 s — FastDownward returned no skeleton and the queue exited
      before timed_process was called.  The 17->14 gap on seeds 100-119
      is NOT a sampling regression; those episodes are F32 dead ends.
      Re-smoke on the same seed (physics diverged:
      blue slipped out at the stack entry, fell off the table and was
      retired LOST, and the remaining fragments sat behind the toppled
      11.6 cm orange box that no yaw can pinch): honest no-plan in plan 7
      after 2.1 s, cumulative planning 17.5 s for the episode; seed 999
      SUCCESS in 4 plans.  Whether the smaller budget costs bindable
      skeletons is what the f8 arms measure against f7 (same seeds).  SMOKE: seed 13 — the binding death of every run
      since 2026-08-21 — SUCCESS in 6 plans, every search under 2.4 s
      (green, the target's blocker, relocated in plan 6); seed 5 SUCCESS
      in 1 plan.  The analyst's other remedy, pddlstream's
      search_sample_ratio (1 -> e.g. 0.2 caps the STANDBY spin at the
      cost of one resample pass per iteration for streams that need
      several calls), is left at the default for this checkpoint: with
      the dead facts gone the spin only follows a genuinely unbindable
      skeleton; revisit if such cases dominate the sweep's wall clock.
      Analysis: scratchpad f7_analysis.md.  Previous entry:]

  [STEP (3) SENSE OVER THE RENDERED OBSERVATION — 2026-08-22.

  the render).  Off-image endpoints are never claimed observed

  (3b) GREP-GATE CLEANUP: the two remaining p.getAABB writes feeding
  the registry — the post-place free-space carve

  [STEP (4) GRASP SAMPLER OVER THE ESTIMATE + F24 — 2026-09-19.
  (4a) SAMPLER (streams.sample_grasp): reads the object's ESTIMATED
  box (registry boxel; the class prior NOMINAL_HIDDEN_EXTENTS for a
  pre-sense hidden object) and offers every top-down grasp whose
  pinch fits: yaw 0 pinches along world y, yaw pi/2 along world x
  (robot_utils.pinch_axis; measured with tools/_probe_f24_xy.py: the
  finger separation vector at yaw 0 is |dy| = 0.100 m, at yaw pi/2
  |dx| = 0.100 m); a span above GRASP_MAX_SPAN 0.076 m is not
  offered, the shorter span comes first; approach altitudes 0.10 /
  0.06 (altitude gate unchanged) plus one GRASP_TALL_CLEARANCE 0.08 m
  above a tall object's half-height, so a 13 cm occluder no longer
  has exactly one altitude (the F7 generator-exhaustion lead).
  Deterministic best-first order, no shuffle.  Grasp.position stays
  [0, 0, z], so the world-frame offset convention of the collision
  checker (held-cargo pose), place and stack is untouched.  Both IK
  gates (streams._pybullet_ik, robot_utils.solve_ik) now also require
  the FK ORIENTATION within IK_ORN_TOL_DEG 10 deg: they were
  position-only, so a yawed grasp satisfied positionally but not
  rotationally would have passed planning and failed on the descent.
  Limitation, stated: the box is axis-aligned, so an object that
  yawed on the table grows on both axes and may get no grasp (the F2
  giveup covers it; a principal-axis yaw from the segmentation is the
  follow-up if it ever matters).
  (4b) F24, THE PICK AIMS AT THE ESTIMATE: the dispatcher resolves the
  pick from registry.get_boxel(obj) (centre + box) and execute_pick
  takes obj_aabb=; without a boxel the pick is refused with a log and
  the loop replans (a pick is grounded on obj_at_boxel_KIF, which
  comes from a registered boxel).  execute_pick re-observes the scene
  once before the descent (env.detect_objects; the arm is parked at
  the approach altitude) and re-poses the box on the fresh detection
  when it is consistent with the known size (>= DETECTION_MIN_PIXELS,
  top within 1 cm), logging "[F24] pick re-aim" above 2 mm; an
  occluded or inconsistent detection keeps the registry box, logged.
  This replaces the 2026-08-21 live re-aim that read
  p.getBasePositionAndOrientation.  contact_z, the pre-grasp aperture
  and the width plausibility come from the estimate (top - 5 mm; the
  pinch-axis half-extent + 8 mm).
  (4c) TACTILE CLOSE (robot_utils.close_gripper_until_contact): the
  fingers advance 0.5 mm per step under the 60 N close force until
  BOTH pad links report a contact with a body other than the robot,
  the table and the ground; the finger position at that moment is the
  MEASURED half-width along the pinch axis, and the motors then hold
  3 mm inside it, the same squeeze close_gripper used to place from a
  p.getAABB width.  Nothing between the fingers -> None -> the
  existing pad-contact failure path.  The F1(d) plausibility check
  becomes a width window [5 mm, estimate pinch half-extent + 8 mm] (a
  standing-on-top phantom reads ~0; wider means the pads closed on
  something else or across the wrong axis).
  ACCURACY OF THE AIM (tools/_probe_f24_xy.py, seeds 0-24 + 999, 66
  detections): estimate centre error |cx| mean 1.0 max 2.3 mm, |cy|
  mean 0.2 max 0.8 mm, extents within -2.1..+0.1 mm, with the square-
  footprint completion ON (the raw cloud under-estimates the y extent
  by up to 9 mm: the completion is right for this camera).  The
  "small pick-success cost" F24 expected is therefore not visible at
  spawn; post-manipulation staleness is what the pre-descent
  observation and the F2 strikes absorb.
  (4d) WP2b, PLACE / STACK / RELEASE WITHOUT THE ORACLE (2026-09-19):
  execute_place and execute_stack used to read p.getAABB of the held
  body and of the support and p.getBasePositionAndOrientation of the
  held body for the drop heights, and _release_and_verify_drop judged
  the release from the simulator's AABB / velocity / orientation of
  the released body.  Now: the dispatcher passes the held object's
  registry box (held_aabb=; it survives the pick, rigid size) and the
  support's registry box (support_aabb=; re-posed by every observation
  and by the post-stack refresh), the EE-to-object offset is the one
  the pick established (_grasp_ee_to_obj_z: execute_pick's own contact
  rule applied to the rigid box), and the destination is the support
  box's centre and top.  Verification is split: _release_and_verify_drop
  keeps the TACTILE gates (fingers reached max open; no robot-link
  contact with the released body; retries for snags) and
  _observe_release, after the post-action lift, OBSERVES the released
  object with two renders 20 steps apart: top within 2 cm of support
  top + rigid height (skipped for the tray, a container), footprint
  within the rigid size + 1.5 cm and top not above expected + 1.5 cm
  (upright), centre moved <= 3 mm (still).  An object the render does
  not show (hidden by the arm) is accepted on the tactile checks and
  the log says so.  The former simulator quantities remain as
  print-only [gt-diag] fields.  The dead place fallback that read
  boxel_to_pybullet's spawn-time position now uses the registry.
  Smokes: seed 999 holding SUCCESS (3 plans; release-obs top 0.4522 vs
  expected 0.4525), tray-stack 343879192 SUCCESS (4 plans; stack on
  cyan: top 0.3971 vs 0.3982), stack h=2 seed 0 and h=3 seed 3 SUCCESS
  (tops within 1.3 mm of expected).
  (4e) WP2c, THE COLLISION WORLD IS THE BELIEF (2026-09-19): #P1
  inventory item (j).  sync_to_plan_client(registry=) now poses the
  motion planner's plan client from the registry — the robot's joints
  (proprioception); the plane, the table and the tray at their true
  poses (static bodies known at design time, like sizes); every other
  body at its registry boxel's centre, upright; a body the belief does
  not hold (a hidden target never seen, a retired lost object) parked
  at _PLAN_CLIENT_PARK [5, 5, -5] — the planner cannot avoid what the
  robot does not know is there, and a motion that hits it is a
  physical failure the #P2 monitor reports; the held body at the end
  effector.  The post-place / post-stack plan-client mirrors use the
  release observation's centre (else the intended destination), the
  audit-#58 emergency drop re-poses the dropped boxel with the rigid-
  size refresh (under the hand when the arm hides it), and
  boxel_to_pybullet carries names and body ids only.  Without a
  registry (probes) the old simulator mirror remains.  Smokes: seed
  999 holding SUCCESS (5 plans), seed 13 F7 as always, tray-stack
  SUCCESS (3 plans), stack h=3 SUCCESS.
  (4f) SAMPLER CORRECTION (2026-09-19, same day): the first A/B arm of

  every such cell, and with three grasps per occluder instead of one
  each FastDownward search took > 30 s until the 900 s cap.  Withdrawn:
  offsets are 0.10 / 0.06 (gated) again, and a square footprint
  (extents within GRASP_SQUARE_TOL 5 mm) gets yaw 0 only, so the
  planner's grasp count is unchanged for every current scene object
  (all square); a rectangular footprint gets both yaws, shorter span
  first.  The "more grasps = harder to exhaust" F7 lead is therefore
  NOT pursued through grasp multiplicity: it costs search time
  linearly at every doomed cell.  The arm was stopped and re-run on
  the corrected code (wp2 arms).
  Smokes: seed 999 holding SUCCESS in 5 plans (measured half-widths

  F12. [STILL OPEN; soft form TRIED AND WITHDRAWN 2026-09-19] VIEW_BLOCKED
      GATES SENSE ABSOLUTELY WHILE THE EXECUTED SENSE MAKES PROGRESS ON

      (BoxelData.blocked_fraction; KEPT, diagnostic), _build_init emits
      (marginally_blocked ?region) for a blocked fragment with fraction

      (cost 3, view_blocked + marginally_blocked in place of view_clear).
      Result on seed 13: twelve one-second plans, every one a
      sense_partial — at cost 3 it beats any relocation (pick + move +
      place + move >= 4) — every one still_blocked: a fragment grazed
      along its length by a body at its edge shrinks to (almost) itself

      parked all four fragments, and the run ended all_searched with
      the target unfound.  Worse than the honest no-plan.  A usable
      soft form needs a PROGRESS criterion (the blocked rays' bounding
      box strictly smaller than the fragment, or the blocked fraction
      confined to one side) that only the census can compute, and a
      cost that does not undercut relocation; both are design work for
      the F6 round, not a same-day patch.  Original:]
      VIEW_BLOCKED GATES SENSE ABSOLUTELY WHILE THE EXECUTED
      SENSE MAKES PROGRESS ON PARTIALLY BLOCKED FRAGMENTS (2026-08-22,
      runs 15-25-35 / 15-58-09): the domain derives view_clear by
      stratified negation over blocks_view, so ANY census-listed
      blocker (> 5 % of some slice) makes a shadow un-sensable in
      planning — the only plannable route is relocating the blocker,
      which is F7 territory when that means a re-pick.  But the
      EXECUTED sense handles partial blockage productively:
      still_blocked shrinks the fragment to the blocked remainder
      (partial-reveal, 2026-08-21) and the clear rays observe the rest
      empty.  In the repro, shadow_of_red was 12 % blocked by the
      marginally placed orange (see the F11 residual): a sense would
      have revealed 88 % of it or found the target outright, but the
      planner could not ground one and the episode died in the F7
      binding death instead.  Direction (F6-ADJACENT, SO PARKED WITH
      IT — it changes what sense means in the domain): either derive
      view_clear from a "usefully observable" criterion (every
      slice's blocked fraction under a threshold well above the
      census margin), or let sense ground on marginally blocked
      shadows at a cost penalty.  Evaluate together with the parked
      F6 criterion work on its A/B harness.
      [FIELD EVIDENCE 2026-08-22, NO THRESHOLD CHANGED
      (tools/swarm_2026-08-22/D_marginal_band_evidence.md +
      D_marginal_band_sweep.py, parsing all 159 of today's run logs =
      258 place actions; the 1478-cell pre-F11 sweep_full corpus was
      parsed separately and never mixed in).  Signature counted: a
      "*** X PLACED at Y! ***" whose immediately preceding audit-#78
      census already lists X as a blocker — i.e. a placement that
      cleared the prospective gate and was re-flagged at the 0.05 census
      margin by the very next census.
        * 57 incidents over 258 placements; 23 ended the episode, 34
          were absorbed by replanning.
        * 17 re-blocked the oracle's TRUE hiding shadow, and 15 of those
          17 (88 %) ended the episode, against 8 of 40 (20 %) for
          non-target shadows — re-blocking the target's own shadow is
          the lethal case, because sensing it is then unavoidable and
          relocating its blocker is exactly F7's machinery.
        * The 0.15 egregious gate (c0fb6ec, 15:40) barely moved the
          rate: 22.5 % of placements before, 19.4 % after.  The seed-0
          signature (orange@free_005 re-blocking shadow_of_red)

          the gate went live — and ends the episode every time.  F11's
          own "RESIDUAL (accepted, by design)" note predicted exactly
          this.

          prospective margin only for shadows whose blockers the current
          plan RELOCATES) is measured NEGATIVE on this corpus: it would
          have fired on 0 of the 6 post-gate incidents, because every
          one is COLLATERAL — a placement that frees one shadow occludes
          a different, untouched one — not a relocation of the blocked
          shadow's own occupant.  3 x 0.05 is also numerically today's
          0.15, so it is a scoping change, not a threshold change.
      CONCLUSION: the band is real and unchanged by F11, but this
      evidence can rule a candidate OUT and cannot size a replacement —
      the post-gate sample is 6 incidents over 31 placements dominated
      by one repeated seed, and the incident counts are repeated
      observations of ~a dozen distinct scene/plan interactions, not
      independent trials.  THRESHOLDS LEFT ALONE, as F11's revert
      history warrants (335ddc1's 0.05 attempt doubled planning time and
      collapsed the gated arm 20/20 -> 11/20).  Any replacement must be
      defined first and then measured on the 20-seed A/B harness.  This
      corroborates F11's own verdict that the actual lever is F7: 23 of
      57 primary and 4 of 6 post-gate incidents are absorbed only once
      the binding death is fixed, which no margin threshold touches.]

  F14. [DONE] THE EXECUTOR SPENDS A FIXED STEP COUNT PER WAYPOINT PAIR,
      SO A SMOOTHED TRANSIT THROWS THE CARGO (2026-08-22, user GUI run
      17-14-10, holding seed 0): orange was picked with
      pad_normal_force=112.25 N (:133), the transit to free_005 needed
      RRT ("direct path blocked", :139) and smoothed "8 wps -> 3 wps"
      (:140), the arm slung the block off the table, and the place entry
      found pad_normal_force=0.00 N (:145).  Every later refresh render
      then logged "1 object(s) not visible — keeping last estimate:
      ['orange_object']" while the planner kept believing orange sat in
      its original boxel — the episode searched a world that no longer
      existed and died in the F7 binding death (:635).
      MECHANISM (measured, tools/swarm_2026-08-22/C_motion_deltas.md +
      probe_C_motion.py, 7 RNG realizations of the same transit): the
      RRT bounds every tree edge to RRT_STEP_SIZE = 0.2 rad
      (streams.py:952), but _smooth_path (SMOOTH_ATTEMPTS=75,
      streams.py:1101) splices several such edges into ONE straight edge
      and certifies it with is_path_collision_free, which checks 8
      samples for collisions and NEVER bounds the edge's joint-space
      length.  The dispatcher then ran that edge through
      move_robot_smooth with a hardcoded steps=30
      (test_full_pipeline.py:1348) — the same schedule it gives a
      0.2 rad edge.  Worst measured pair: 1.146 rad in one hop =
      0.0382 rad/step = 9.16 rad/s, 4.2x joint 2's 2.175 rad/s URDF
      limit; median 8.0 rad/s.  POSITION_CONTROL chases that target with
      a 240 N-m budget and no maxVelocity anywhere in the path, so the
      arm swings hard enough to break a friction grasp.  The F10 fix
      (held cargo checked at its true pose) correctly forces RRT more
      often, so exposure to this went UP — F14 is the pre-existing
      defect F10 surfaced, not a regression in F10.
      SCOPE IS WIDER THAN SMOOTHED RRT (F17 telemetry, run 17-50-31):
      ordinary 10-waypoint LINEAR moves showed the same class on their
      first hop — the arm's real pose to the trajectory's first executed
      waypoint measured 2.29 rad in one pair = 17.7 rad/s with the EE at
      4.4 m/s.  The bound therefore belongs in the executor, where every
      caller inherits it, not in the smoother.
      [FIXED 2026-08-22: move_robot_smooth's step count is now a FLOOR,
      not a schedule.  It measures the joint-space delta and stretches
      the interpolation until no joint is commanded past its datasheet
      velocity (JOINT_VELOCITY_LIMITS, the panda.urdf/datasheet 2.175
      rad/s for joints 1-4 and 2.610 for the wrist, against the default
      1/240 s timestep), clamped at MAX_INTERP_STEPS=1200 so a
      pathological pair slows the arm instead of stalling the episode.
      Short hops keep exactly the caller's nominal count, so the
      contact descents and every other call site are byte-identical;
      only hops that would exceed a limit are stretched, and each one is
      disclosed at DEBUG ([F14]).  Deliberately NOT fixed in the
      smoother: bounding shortcut length would change planning geometry
      and risk the F11-class regression, while the executor bound is
      exact, local and covers the linear-move case too.
      VERIFIED (3 consecutive holding seed 0 runs, F17 telemetry
      reading the physics): peak |qd| fell from 17.7 rad/s to <= 2.55
      rad/s — inside the limits — worst per-step delta from 0.0737 to
      0.0106 rad, and ZERO GRIP_LOST and ZERO OFF_SUPPORT events across
      all three runs (pre-fix the same seed threw orange 838 mm to
      [0.480,-1.237,0.027], i.e. onto the floor).  Honest cost: an
      episode's simulated motion time roughly doubles (a real Panda
      needs 1.05 s for a 2.29 rad joint move; the old schedule did it in
      0.125 s).  Planning time — the metric CB#113 reports — is
      untouched.  The two of three runs that still failed failed in the
      F7 binding-death class, disclosed, not by a throw.]

  F15. [DONE] "CONTAINS SOMETHING THE RENDER CANNOT LOCALIZE" RETIRES
      THE FRAGMENT THE TARGET IS SITTING IN, AND NEVER SAYS WHAT IT SAW
      (2026-08-22, run 17-14-10, step-(3) regression): shadow_of_green
      (:243,:303,:357) and then shadow_of_red — blue's ACTUAL hiding
      place per the oracle (:53) — each burned 3 strikes of the new
      sense_contains_unlocalizable guard and were marked not_here
      (:358, :584); the episode then died in the F7 binding death with
      the target still physically inside a fragment the planner had
      eliminated.
      MECHANISM (tools/swarm_2026-08-22/B_sense_code_path.md): the guard
      compares two DIFFERENT tests.  sense_shadow_from_render classifies
      each grid endpoint by a single-pixel seg lookup with no pixel-count
      floor (execution.py:189-201), while sense_detections comes from
      detect_objects_from_render, which drops any body whose WHOLE-FRAME
      footprint is under DETECTION_MIN_PIXELS=6 (perception.py:404).  A
      body can therefore trip an endpoint while being absent from the
      detection set, and the branch then refuses to act.  Note also that
      occluder_pybullet_ids passed to sense is only THIS shadow's
      current blockers (execution.py:1850), so a relocated occluder is
      no longer in the ignore set and any body it now intercepts is
      treated as newly discovered "content".
      THE 3-STRIKE BUDGET IS SHARED BY TWO DIFFERENT FAILURE MODES
      (found by the swarm code-path pass, B_sense_code_path.md §2, and
      it is what actually killed the target's fragment in this run):
      blocked_counts is ONE dict keyed by shadow id
      (test_full_pipeline.py:1013, never reset per plan) and is
      incremented from BOTH execution.py:1967 (contains_nontarget-
      unlocalizable) and execution.py:2277 (still_blocked), with both
      giveup tests reading >= 3.  shadow_of_red_object therefore died on
      a mixed budget: attempt 1 was still_blocked (:466-468, which
      legitimately shrank the fragment), attempts 2 and 3 were
      unlocalizable (:529, :583) — three strikes, but never three of the
      SAME observation, and not one of them evidence of absence.  Split
      the counters (or at minimum stop the two modes from feeding one
      budget) as part of (c).
      WHY THE not_here IS A BELIEF LIE: the branch's own comment says
      "the content stays unmodelled" — the observation is "this volume
      contains SOMETHING I could not resolve", which is evidence of
      OCCUPANCY, not of the target's absence.  Site B in B_sense_code_
      path.md §5 confirms it writes belief.mark_sensed(found=False)
      without removing the boxel and without any geometric proof of
      absence, and that the F5 grid_would_hit contract is only ever
      checked once at spawn against the ORIGINAL fragment — never
      re-checked after a partial-reveal shrink.
      [DONE (a) 2026-08-22: the refusal now NAMES what it saw.
      sense_shadow_from_render returns a fifth value, interceptor_counts
      {seg id -> endpoints it stood in front of}, covering every
      intercepting body; it is diagnostic only and no branch reads it.
      The guard prints [F15-diag] with, per discovery, its name, its
      endpoint count, its whole-frame seg pixel count, whether it
      cleared the detection minimum, and the full set of bodies the
      render did localize.  Without this the field log could not say
      which body cost the target its fragment.]
      [DONE (b) 2026-08-22: LOCALIZE FROM WHAT IS THERE.  Before burning
      a strike, the branch re-runs the SAME unprojection
      (detect_objects_from_render) at min_pixels=1 restricted to the
      intercepting bodies.  The endpoints they intercepted prove they
      have pixels, and those pixels carry depth — DETECTION_MIN_PIXELS
      is a whole-frame CONFIDENCE gate, not a statement that the body
      cannot be located.  Because a 1-5 pixel cloud measures far smaller
      than the object, the estimate is GROWN to the
      NOMINAL_HIDDEN_EXTENTS class box centred on the cloud (support-
      snapped when it sits on the table), mirroring the hidden-object
      class prior the streams already use: the box only ever
      over-estimates, so "could something hide here / does this fit"
      stays conservative.  The discovery then flows through the normal
      contains_nontarget bookkeeping instead of being refused.  A body
      with genuinely ZERO pixels still cannot be localized and still
      burns a strike — the bound stays finite.]
      [DONE (c) 2026-08-22: NO MORE FALSE not_here, AND SPLIT BUDGETS.
      belief gains a third status, 'unresolved' (mark_unresolved /
      get_parked_shadows).  Both giveup sites now park instead of
      eliminating: the unlocalizable path (whose observation is evidence
      of OCCUPANCY) and the still_blocked path (whose own message always
      said "Shadow is NOT observed empty" while writing not_here
      anyway).  The planner behaviour is unchanged — the replan loop
      passes known_empty + parked as known_empty_shadows, so a parked
      fragment is withheld exactly as before and the episode still
      terminates — but the BELIEF no longer asserts an absence nobody
      observed, and run_logger discloses parked fragments in the outcome
      ("parked UNRESOLVED, not eliminated ... the target may be
      inside"), excluding them from the "Shadows searched" count so a
      success can never imply the robot cleared a region it gave up on.
      The unlocalizable strikes also get their own key namespace
      ("<sid>::unlocalizable") so the two failure modes stop sharing one
      budget.]
      NOTE the guard is RARE headless: 0 trips in 4 consecutive seed-0
      runs, and probe_A_interceptor.py could not find any pose of a
      thrown orange that renders under 6 px while intercepting a sense
      endpoint (347 poses swept, disclosed negative).  F14 is the likely
      upstream cause — a block flung to the floor at the frame edge is
      exactly the sliver body this branch cannot resolve — so with F14
      landed the (b) path should fire rarely; (c) is the guarantee that
      when it does, the target's fragment is not written off.
      [CORRECTED 2026-08-23 by F19: the field interceptor was neither
      thrown orange nor any scene body — it was the GUI's own overlay
      phantom boxes rendering into the observation (run 11-25-45's
      [F15-diag] named seg ids 10/12, 348-1197 px, outside the 0-6
      scene-body range).  The headless-rare / probe-negative pattern
      above was the fingerprint of a GUI-only cause: the visualizer
      only exists under the GUI.  (b) remains correct for genuine
      slivers, (c) remains the guarantee; F19 removes the overlay
      class entirely.]
      SUB-FINDING (F15.1): THE THROWN OBJECT WAS NEVER RETIRED.
      Across all 8 plans the run logged "1 object(s) not visible in the
      refresh render — keeping last estimate: ['orange_object']" 7 times
      (:148,:244,:304,:359,:470,:530,:585) and NEVER once reported the
      step-(3c) LOST verdict, so the planner kept orange in its original
      boxel for the whole episode while the object was on the floor.
      Reconstructing the same scene offline WITHOUT the arm renders
      orange's believed region fully observed-empty (probe_A_
      interceptor.py PART 3), which points at the retirement check being
      defeated by transient ARM occlusion at exactly the refresh
      moments: retire_lost_objects only fires when EVERY slice of the
      believed region is visible-through, and an arm parked over that
      region during the post-sense refresh makes it "occluded, therefore
      not lost" — correct in isolation, but it means a stale belief can
      persist indefinitely as long as the arm keeps happening to stand
      in front of it.  Evaluate with (b)/(c): the honest fix is probably
      to remember the un-observed verdict and retry the region when the
      arm is elsewhere, not to relax the occlusion rule.  Note the
      audit-#78 census DID track reality correctly over the same period
      (shadow_of_orange's blocker flips orange -> red at :455), so the
      census and the object registry disagreed for the whole episode.
      [RESOLVED 2026-08-23 BY F19: the "something not modeled by
      teleport reconstruction" that kept the region occluded was the
      OVERLAY, not the arm — orange's believed-region slices rendered
      overlay-intercepted at every GUI refresh, gating off the 3c
      retirement, while the no-viz probe (and every headless run)
      retired the object cleanly.  With F19 concealing the overlay for
      every observation render, GUI runs see exactly what the probe
      saw; no separate retry-when-arm-elsewhere machinery is needed for
      this class.  The narrower arm-parked-over-the-region case remains
      theoretically possible but has no observed instance.]

  F16. [DONE] SENSE RE-CHECKS ONLY THE SAME CASTER'S SIBLING FRAGMENTS,
      AND ONLY ON clear_but_empty (2026-08-22): the sibling batch-sense
      (execution.py:2052) re-runs sense_shadow_from_render only over

      clear_but_empty.  Nothing regressed here — the user's broader

      loops that F15 exposes and reduces mission actions.  Step counts
      change, so CB#113 re-baselines regardless.
      [FIXED 2026-08-22: the caster-scoped loop is replaced by
      execution.sweep_all_fragments, which classifies EVERY registry
      SHADOW fragment against the sense render and is called from THREE
      sites, not one — the clear_but_empty/contains_nontarget branch (as
      before), the still_blocked branch, and the F15 unlocalizable
      branch.  The last two are the point: a sense that could not settle
      ITS fragment still rendered the whole table, and that observation
      used to be discarded — exactly the loop where the field run burned
      six senses learning nothing.  The removal criterion is byte-
      identical to the sibling pass (delete only on clear_but_empty,
      i.e. every grid endpoint of that fragment was seen; each removal
      observation-backed via belief.mark_sensed(found=False)), and
      still_blocked fragments get the same partial-reveal shrink a
      planned sense would give them.  contains_nontarget fragments are
      deliberately left untouched for a planned sense that can run the
      discovery bookkeeping.  A found_target on some OTHER fragment is
      reported loudly ([F16]) but NOT acted on — promoting it would mean
      running the audit-#76 registration chain from inside a sweep, and
      that chain is the found-path's contract; surfacing it lets the
      next plan target it.  Promoting that surface to a belief update is
      the natural follow-up.]

  F18. [ROOT CAUSE FOUND AND FIXED 2026-09-20; honest-reporting half
      landed with #P2] A TRANSIT KNOCKS A BYSTANDER FLAT AND THE EPISODE
      STILL REPORTS SUCCESS (2026-08-22, found BY the new F17 telemetry on the
      first tray run after F15/F16 landed, run 19-07-38, seed
      343879192, find-and-tray-stack): during
      "plan#1 action 3/9: move(tray)" — carrying cyan_object — the arm
      hit orange_object, which the arm was never holding: DISTURBED x44,
      TOPPLE, displaced 75.9 mm and ending at tilt 90.0 deg (flat on its
      side) at [-0.018,0.197,0.354].  It stayed toppled for the rest of
      the episode, and the run reported SUCCESS because the goal
      (cyan and blue on the tray) did not happen to need orange.
      This is the F10 family surviving F10's fix: F10 corrected the
      held-cargo POSE used in collision checking, and this transit's
      cargo (cyan) was checked — so either the executed motion diverges
      from the validated one, or it was the ARM's own links rather than
      the cargo that swept orange, in which case the arm's own check
      should have rejected the edge.  Establish which before proposing a
      fix; the telemetry frame stream for that action has the per-step
      poses of both bodies and the arm.
      This is exactly #P2's thesis (prove the failures are EXECUTION,
      detect physical failure, fail honestly) with a concrete, cheap
      detector now available: the world-integrity monitor #P2 asks for is
      largely already computed — telemetry's DISTURBED event is "a body
      the arm never grasped moved", which is the definition #P2 needs.
      Wiring that event into the run outcome (a success that toppled a
      bystander is not a clean success) is the natural next step and
      would give the paper an honest per-episode integrity flag.

      [REPORTING HALF DONE with #P2 (37c811a): the world-integrity
      monitor runs after every move/pick/place/stack, the same topple is
      now detected and replanned against, report_run_outcome prints
      "NOTE: success with N world-integrity event(s) ... not a clean
      success" and timing_summary.json persists the event list, so the
      per-episode integrity flag the entry asks for exists.]

      [ROOT CAUSE 2026-09-20, from the telemetry frames of the 09-19
      16-04-57 reproduction (same seed, same action) — the entry's two
      candidates were "the executed motion diverges from the validated
      one" or "the ARM's own links swept it".  BOTH ARE WRONG.  What
      touched orange_object is in its own contact list, frame by frame:
        step 1432 t=5.967  tilt  4.6 deg  contacts=['table','cyan_object']
        step 1434 t=5.975  tilt  8.1 deg  contacts=['cyan_object']
        step 1436 t=5.983  tilt 11.5 deg  contacts=['table','cyan_object']
      cyan_object is the CARGO, and the arm's own links never appear.
      So F10's fix was right and the cargo WAS in the collision world at
      the correct pose — the check simply never looked where it mattered.
      is_path_collision_free sampled a FIXED 8 configurations per edge,
      whatever the edge's length, and this transit was the direct-path
      shortcut: "Moving to tray (2 waypoints)" is a SINGLE straight-line
      joint-space edge, 63.4 cm of end-effector travel (measured from the
      telemetry frames of that action), so consecutive collision checks
      sat 9.1 cm apart at the end effector while the cargo is 3.3 cm
      wide.  A body standing between two samples was never tested.  The
      RRT was never the problem: _steer caps an edge at RRT_STEP_SIZE
      0.2 rad, and 8 samples over 0.2 rad is 0.029 rad apart.  It is the
      two places that hand the function an arbitrarily long edge — the
      direct-path shortcut and the smoother's replacement edges, which
      are exactly the edges that become executed transits.
      SECOND MECHANISM, found the same day and the sharper of the two:
      that transit did NOT come from the direct-path shortcut at all.
      The log of the same action reads
        plan_motion: direct path blocked — running RRT-Connect
        plan_motion: RRT path 12 wps -> smoothed 2 wps
      A smoothed path of 2 waypoints is the single edge start->goal —
      the exact straight line the direct check had just REJECTED.  The
      two gates disagreed because they were not the same gate:
      plan_motion passes strict_gripper_interior=is_pick_place to the
      direct check (the 2026-08-21 fix, so a transit's interior is
      checked with the fingers STRICT), while _smooth_path passed only
      allow_gripper_collisions and never strict_gripper_interior.  So
      every shortcut was certified with the gripper relaxed along its
      whole length, and the smoother could hand back any edge the
      direct check had refused.  The 2026-08-21 fix was live but a
      second, more permissive gate stood behind it.
      PREVALENCE, and which mechanism dominates (55 recorded runs of
      the 09-20 arms, tools/_f18_prevalence in the scratchpad): 13 runs
      (24 %) contain at least one bystander event, 32 events in all
      (22 disturbed, 8 off_support, 2 toppled).  Attributing each event
      to the move that preceded it, by that move's waypoint count —
      10 waypoints is the direct-path linear trajectory
      (_linear_trajectory's default), anything smaller is a smoothed
      RRT path —
        10 waypoints (direct path)        : 25 events
         3 waypoints (smoothed RRT path)  :  7 events
         2 waypoints (smoothed to one edge):  0 events
      so in THESE arms mechanism (1), the fixed sample count on a
      full-length direct edge, accounts for most of what is observed,
      and mechanism (2) for the rest.  The seed-343879192 tray
      reproduction is a 2-waypoint smoothed path, i.e. mechanism (2);
      it does not appear in this table because these arms run holding
      and stack goals, which do far fewer long loaded transits than a
      tray-stack does.  Both mechanisms are real and both are fixed;
      do not quote one without the other.
      FIX (2026-09-20), both halves:
      (1) the check is RESOLUTION-based, not count-based.
      robot_utils.PATH_CHECK_MAX_STEP_RAD = 0.2/7 is the resolution an
      RRT edge already gets; is_path_collision_free raises its sample
      count until no two samples are further apart than that in any
      joint (floor still n_checks, ceiling PATH_CHECK_MAX_SAMPLES 128),
      testing the coarse spread first so a plainly blocked edge still
      rejects in 8 evaluations.  An RRT edge is unchanged at 8 samples
      by construction; a 1.5 rad shortcut edge goes from 8 to 54.
      (2) _smooth_path certifies a shortcut under EXACTLY the direct
      check's rule, strict_gripper_interior included.  A shortcut that
      cannot pass the direct check's rule is not a shortcut.
      Lever --path-check-step (0 restores the fixed count) for the A/B.
      RESIDUAL, made countable rather than fixed: the tree itself is
      grown with the relaxed rule applied PER EDGE, and "the first and
      last 20 % of the path" is meaningless inside a 0.2 rad step, so
      an unsmoothed RRT path's interior waypoints are still certified
      more permissively than the direct path ever was.  Rejecting them
      would strand the planner at pick and place poses where the
      fingers genuinely sit in clutter, so plan_motion now LOGS
      "[F18] n of m interior segment(s) of the executed path pass only
      with the gripper relaxed" for the path it is about to return.
      That check decides relaxation by where a segment sits along the
      WHOLE path (first/last fifth of the joint-space distance is
      endpoint clutter, the rest is transit) rather than within the
      segment, which is the very confusion that created the residual —
      a per-segment test would relax the ends of every segment and
      measure nothing.  It sits behind --path-check-diag and is OFF by
      default: it costs a strict check per interior segment of every
      trajectory the STREAM yields, and most yielded trajectories are
      never executed, so leaving it on would put diagnostic work in the
      planning-time number it is meant to explain.  Turn it on for a
      measurement run and count the line.
      Cost: rejected shortcuts keep the RRT detour, so paths get longer
      and both planning and execution time rise; the A/B measures the
      price against the bystander and transit-loss counts.
      [A/B 2026-09-20 (commit 54f982d, chain 9; BOTH arms carry the
      smoother fix, the flag switches only the RESOLUTION half, so this
      isolates (1) and not (2)):
        holding seeds  resolution on        resolution off
        0-19           16/20  7.0 s mean    15/20  5.1 s mean
        100-119        17/20  5.3 s mean    16/20  6.9 s mean
        total          33/40                31/40
      Success and planning time are a wash — the smoke's 2.5x slowdown
      on the stack scene does NOT generalise to the holding scenes, and
      the direct-path rejection rate only moves from 5.0 % (416 clear /
      22 blocked) to 7.5 % (392 / 32).
      WHAT THE SAFETY NUMBERS ACTUALLY SAY, stated against my own
      expectation: over 35 runs per arm, bystander events are 14 in the
      pre-F18 arm (f8), 3 with the smoother fix alone, and 8 with both
      halves.  So the SMOOTHER fix is what moves the number, and adding
      the resolution did not help — the three largest shoves in the
      whole comparison (106, 131 and 187 mm) are all in the resolution
      arm, against a 37 mm maximum without it.
        tray-stack seeds 0-7   resolution on     resolution off
        success                4/8               3/8
        mean planning          23.6 s            23.5 s
        bystander events       4 (2 topples,     2 (both 26 mm)
                                 114 and 123 mm)
        cargo lost in transit  2                 4
      Both goal types agree, so this is not one arm's luck: over 43 runs
      per configuration, resolution OFF gives 5 bystander events and
      NONE above 50 mm, resolution ON gives 12 events including five
      shoves of 106 to 187 mm, at the same success and the same
      planning time.  Normalising by plans executed does not remove it
      (0.029 vs 0.075 events per plan on holding, 0.07 vs 0.15 on
      tray).  For reference the pre-F18 arm sits at 14 events over 35
      runs, so the SMOOTHER half improved the record and the resolution
      half gave part of it back.
      MECHANISM NOT ESTABLISHED, and I will not pretend otherwise.  The
      natural explanation is that more rejected direct paths push motion
      onto RRT detours whose surviving edges are certified with the
      fingers relaxed (the residual above), but the rejection rate only
      moves from 5.0 % to 7.5 %, which looks too small to carry it.  An
      attempt to attribute each event to the path kind that preceded it,
      by reading the planner's own log lines, was DISCARDED as invalid:
      plan_motion logs every SAMPLING call, not just the trajectory that
      gets executed, so "the last path line before the event" is usually
      some rejected candidate.  The only reliable provenance in the log
      is the executed move's waypoint count, and a direct path (always
      10, by _linear_trajectory's default) is not distinguishable there
      from a smoothed RRT path that happens to have 10.  Settling it
      needs the executed trajectory's provenance logged at the move,
      which is a one-line addition worth making before the next attempt.
      DECISION 2026-09-20: the resolution default is OFF
      (streams.path_check_step = 0.0, i.e. exactly the configuration the
      pcold arms measured), the smoother fix is unconditional, and the
      finer check stays one flag away at --path-check-step 0.0286.
      Shipping the coarse check as the default is a measured choice, not
      an oversight: it IS unsound on long edges, that is stated here and
      in the wiki, and if the residual is ever fixed the resolution must
      be re-A/B'd, because the soundness argument for it has not gone
      away.]

  F17. [DONE] HEADLESS RUNS RECORDED DECISIONS BUT NOT PHYSICS
      (2026-08-22, user-directed): a GUI run let the user WATCH the
      episode; a headless run logged plans, senses and census verdicts
      and almost nothing about the world, so F14 — a block visibly slung
