      [FIXED 2026-08-22: new telemetry.py, hooked at all seven
      p.stepSimulation sites (robot_utils x4, execution x1, boxel_env
      x2).  Per FRAME (default every 0.05 s of sim time, plus a forced
      frame on every event): each body's pose, rpy, tilt, linear and
      angular velocity, resting height, contact partners with normal
      forces, displacement this action, drift since spawn and offset
      RELATIVE to the end effector, plus the arm's joint positions,
      velocities, per-step deltas, EE pose and EE speed — to
      telemetry.log and telemetry.jsonl in the run directory.  Per
      HEARTBEAT (default 0.5 s of sim time): a compact where-is-
      everything line into the MAIN run log.  EVENTS fire on physical
      anomalies — FLING (an unheld body moving fast, or held cargo
      OUTRUNNING the gripper), GRIP_LOST (cargo leaving the pads in
      mid-air with the fingers still closed, confirmed on the next
      check so a normal release does not trip it), TOPPLE, OFF_SUPPORT,
      AIRBORNE, JOINT_JUMP, JOINT_OVERSPEED, DISTURBED (a body the arm
      never grasped moved) — with the state-like ones LATCHED to their
      transition so a block lying on the floor does not re-fire forever.
      Per ACTION: peak EE speed, peak joint velocity, worst per-step
      joint delta and every body's net displacement, peak speed and end
      state.  SCOPE: a pure observer — log text is its only output, it
      never feeds the registry, the belief or the planner, and it reads
      the simulator directly (the same accepted category as the eval
      verifiers and the execution servo, NOT the perception path).
      CONSOLE: everything goes through logger.debug or straight to the
      telemetry files, so no telemetry line ever reaches the console.
      DEFAULT ON headless, OFF under the GUI (user direction: the GUI
      already shows this); --telemetry / --no-telemetry override, and
      eval_runner passes --no-telemetry because the sweep MEASURES
      wall-clock and an observer that costs time would shift the numbers
      it reports.  This is what caught F14's mechanism headlessly on the
      first try, and its numbers are the F14 verification evidence.]

  F20. [DONE 2026-09-18] THE PIPELINE NEVER RE-DETECTS A NEWLY VISIBLE
      OBJECT: DISCOVERY IS SENSE-ONLY, SO A TARGET IN PLAIN VIEW STAYS
      UNKNOWN (2026-08-23, user GUI report on seed 999 + headless run
      13-25-19).  Objects enter the registry in exactly two places: the
      Phase-2 initial observation, and handle_sense_action when a SENSE
      action discovers them inside a shadow fragment.  Nothing re-runs
      detection as the world changes, so an object that becomes plainly
      camera-visible mid-episode — because the robot just moved the
      occluder that was hiding it — is never noticed.  The robot walks
      past its own target.
      EVIDENCE (seed 999, holding): blue spawns behind red as seen from
      the camera (user screenshot: blue clearly seated behind the red
      occluder).  Run 13-25-19 relocated red, orange AND green, sensed
      every shadow fragment, reported "target not here" on each, and
      ended "All 0 shadows searched — target not found" — with blue
      sitting unoccluded in the open, in full view of the camera, for
      the last three plans.  Every individual step was correct; the
      belief simply has no path from "the camera can see it now" to
      "the planner knows where it is".
      WHY IT WAS INVISIBLE UNTIL NOW: the F5 pre-flight guardrail
      REJECTED exactly the scenes that expose it (target outside every
      sense-hittable fragment), so the failure could never be observed
      in a run.  The guardrail was removed on the same day (user
      directive, see the note below); this finding is what came out
      from under it.
      FIX DIRECTION (this is #P1 step (3)'s natural completion, and it
      is what makes "everything is winnable" true): refresh detection
      from the observation on every replan — get_camera_observation /
      env.detect_objects() already returns per-object estimates — and
      register any DETECTED object that has no registry boxel as an
      OBJECT boxel with obj_at_boxel / obj_at_boxel_KIF /
      obj_pose_known, exactly as the sense-discovery branch does.  A
      target found this way is directly pickable with no sense action
      at all.  Watch three interactions: (i) it must not resurrect
      objects the F19/step-3c lost-object logic just retired — the
      same observation drives both, so run them off ONE render;
      (ii) the shadow fragments of a newly detected object have to be
      cast, or it becomes an occluder nobody accounts for;
      (iii) re-detection while HOLDING an object must not register the
      held object as table-resting.
      RELATED, now root-caused and filed separately as F21: on seed 999
      blue's centre lies in a GAP between shadow fragments.  Fragment
      coverage is NOT a partition of the occluded space, which is
      independent motivation for re-detection — but on this seed the
      gap is itself a carve BUG, not geometry.
      [VERIFIED AND FIXED 2026-09-18 (commit c697233; independent
      adversarial review of the 08-23 write-up, four reviewers + a
      critic, plus telemetry replays of run 13-25-19).  The PHENOMENON
      and the registry analysis above hold; the MECHANISM sentence was
      wrong: detection DOES re-run on the whole workspace at every sense
      (execution.py detect_objects render, every body >= 6 px), after
      every place and after pick failures.  The defect was that
      handle_sense_action registered bodies only from the NAMED
      fragment's ray endpoints and refresh_object_aabbs re-posed only
      boxels that already existed, so whole-frame detections of
      unregistered bodies were discarded.  Corrected timeline: blue was
      in the sense detection dict at FIVE consecutive senses (plans 2-4:
      54 px half behind green, centre error 4.6 mm; plans 5-6: 267 px,
      1.5 mm), not "the last three plans"; plan 7 never rendered (the
      all_searched exit at test_full_pipeline.py:1109 precedes any
      observation).  FIX, per the thesis sensor model (methods.tex:102,
      :166: the percept rides on the sense action; alg. line 214
      "update belief: target found, region known-empty, or a new
      occluder revealed") and the user's statement that a sense
      observes the entire workspace: execution.register_new_detections,
      called at the end of EVERY sense outcome branch after that
      branch's own registration — any detected body with no registry
      boxel becomes an OBJECT boxel at the render estimate; the search
      target gets no shadows and is directly pickable (the generic
      OBJECT-boxel facts in _build_init satisfy pick; obj_pose_known is
      a DEAD predicate — declared, set by sense, emitted for visible
      targets, read by nothing); a non-target joins `occluders` (else
      compute_shadow_blockers never lists it), casts fragments against
      the current solids AND existing fragments (#68 carve) and every
      new fragment enters the belief as unknown (BeliefState.add_shadow
      — the pre-existing discovery branch never did this: its fragments
      were invisible to get_unknown_shadows(), F23 below).  Companion
      change the 08-23 fix direction omitted: the holding loop's
      all_searched exit now yields when the target has a registry boxel
      (belief.target_found_in stays None until the pick sets it — setting
      it on registration would end the loop before any pick and report
      physics_mismatch).  NOT adopted: the 08-23 "refresh detection on
      every replan" — an observation outside a sensing action, which
      the thesis model excludes; a target revealed by the very last
      place with no sense afterwards ends honestly as not found (with
      F21 fixed it lies in a fragment, so a sense is planned first).
      refresh_object_aabbs now prints "[perception] detected but
      UNREGISTERED" so a dropped detection can never again pass in
      silence.  EVIDENCE: seed 999 holding (run 16-35-40): SUCCESS in 3
      plans / 5.4 s — plan-2 sense registered blue at 54 px while still
      half behind green, plan 3 = move + pick; find-and-tray-stack seed

      refresh_object_aabbs' exclude_ids hook still has no caller (F26).
      REVIEW FOLLOW-UP (two independent read-only reviewers over the
      landed diff, fixes in ba924bd): (1) the discovery branch's
      rediscovery cleanup removed an object's superseded fragments from
      the registry but never from the belief — harmless while
      discovery fragments were not in the belief, but with add_shadow
      an orphaned 'unknown' id would have kept get_unknown_shadows()
      non-empty forever and blocked the all-searched exit (and the
      same applied to SPAWN fragments of a rediscovered spawn occluder
      all along, ending such runs planner_failed with phantom
      "unsearched shadows"): BeliefState.remove_shadow, called from the
      cleanup.  (2) A re-detected body standing between the camera and
      an EXISTING fragment was never listed as that fragment's blocker
      (the census runs only at spawn and after a place), so the planner
      derived view_clear, the next sense classified the body as CONTENT
      and deleted the fragment with the target possibly behind it:
      register_new_detections now re-runs compute_shadow_blockers for
      every fragment (one extra render) and updates the map in place.
      (3) A re-detected body's first in-fragment discovery no longer
      burns an F9 rediscovery strike (belief.redetected, one exemption).
      (4) Bodies seen below the table surface are logged, not
      registered (knocked-off-table is #P2's).  (5) Re-detected targets
      join the census body list like every spawn boxel does.  (6) The
      belief now COLLAPSES on localisation: _build_init emits
      obj_at_boxel_KIF(o, F) for every registered OBJECT boxel o and
      every fragment F — an object with a known pose is known to be in
      no fragment — so sense(t, F) is no longer groundable beside
      obj_at_boxel(t, t) (the reviewers' semantic objection to relying
      on the all-searched gate alone; the gate stays as the loop-level
      guard).  Smoke after the follow-up: holding 999 SUCCESS (4 plans,
      15.6 s), holding 0 and stack h=2 SUCCESS; tray-stack 343879192
      this time ended in the known intermittent #P2 class (see F28).
      PAPER-TEXT NOTES for #P10 (reviewer T1/T6): the thesis wording
      "a blocked shadow yields no observation" (methods.tex:166), the
      two-outcome sensor model (:102) and "moving an object cannot
      spawn new shadows" (:157) no longer describe the code — a sense
      is a frame-scoped observation whose belief update covers every
      fragment (F16) and every visible body (F20), and a relocation can
      reveal bodies whose fresh fragments extend the search; the
      post-place render also re-poses boxels and re-derives blockers.
      The paper must state the observation model once, consistently.]

  F27. [OPEN, MINOR] SHADOW AABBs MISS THE SIDE LOBES OF OFF-AXIS
      OCCLUDERS (theory review 2026-09-18, pre-existing, unchanged by
      F21): rays are cast from the BACK corners only and the AABB is
      clamped to start at the occluder's back face on the dominant
      horizontal axis (shadow_calculator.py Step 4), so the wedge
      occluded by a side face of an object off the camera azimuth is
      never claimed.  Line-of-sight probe: a single 5 cm cube at
      (0.50, 0.00) occludes 253 cm^3, the fragment claims 224, a 2 cm
      wide lobe of 29 cm^3 is unclaimed; a taller box in front of a
      shorter one leaves 114-120 cm^3 unclaimed (a 4.9 cm cube's worth).
      A target there is reachable only through F20 after the occluder
      moves, never through a planned sense.  Same review measured the
      over-approximation the other way: 54-69 % of every fragment's
      volume is camera-VISIBLE (height overestimate + F25 spread; F21's
      kept remainders add a little more), a cost the paper must state
      when it calls a shadow Boxel "the occluded space".  Fix: cast from
      all corners whose outward face is visible, or build the fragment
      from the frustum's table footprint instead of hit-point extremes.

  F30. [OPEN, PLANNING QUALITY] PLANS CARRY REDUNDANT RELOCATIONS: the
      user's GUI run 22-12-06 (seed 13) opened with a 10-action plan
      that placed red_object at free_003, picked it up again and placed
      it at free_005 before sensing — a 7-action plan without the
      detour was available (tools/eye.py --audit flags it: "red_object
      is put down 2 times in one plan").  Cause (corrected after
      reading pddlstream/algorithms/downward.py): the domain DOES have
      action costs (total-cost, stack=2, others=1) and the search DOES
      use them, but PDDLStream's default search is 'ff-astar2' — eager
      WEIGHTED A* with the FF heuristic doubled, f = g + 2h, plus
      preferred-operator alternation — so the path cost g is
      outweighed by the heuristic and a 10-action plan is accepted
      when h happens to look better along the detour; success_cost
      stays inf, so the first plan under the bound is returned.
      2026-09-18: (1) New CLI flag --planner (run_logger.py;
      ff-astar1 / ff-astar2 / ff-astar / ff-wastar1 / ff-wastar2 /
      dijkstra), plumbed to planner.plan(planner=) -> solve(planner=).
      Tried 'ff-astar1' (w = 1, f = g + h) as the default: seed 13's plan
      1 became the 7-action plan (detour gone) BUT its planning time rose
      12 -> 103 s over 4 plans, and seed 999 — 4-15 s at w = 2 — hit a
      900 s cap in its fourth plan; both still ended in their F7 binding
      deaths.  The unweighted search expands far more nodes in exactly
      the searches that end without a plan, so it is NOT the default;
      the flag keeps it available.  (2) A post-planning rewrite of the
      plan (commit c2867ac: planner.plan() dropped a place/stack of ?o
      that was immediately picked up again from the same boxel and let
      the dispatcher's audit-#60 runtime motion re-plan bridge the gap)
      was REVERTED the same day on the user's directive: "never do
      that. this is cheating".  The rule: the loop acts on the plan the
      planner returned; nothing edits a plan between the planner and
      the executor.  A plan-quality problem is fixed in the search or
      the model (weight, heuristic, anytime bound, domain) or it is
      reported as it is.  REGRESSION ARM f2021c_51fd444 (seeds 0-19,
      same night, machine otherwise idle) ran with the rewrite still in
      but ZERO of its events fired in any of the night's runs (the
      detour is rare), so it measured the flag-only code that is now
      HEAD: 14/20, mean 12.1 s, median 7.2 s vs f2021b_ba924bd 15/20,
      10.8 s, 7.4 s; flips: seed 0 won, seeds 1 (binding death) and 8
      (physics mismatch) lost; identical planning code, so the three
      flips are pure trajectory noise (the per-placement camera image
      adds ~0.2 s of wall clock, which is enough to move PDDLStream's
      wall-clock budgets).  Use this pair as the measured noise floor:
      ±1-2 seeds per 20 for the SAME code.
      Cost of the detours: wasted
      actions, an extra chance to disturb the scene, and every
      placement is a chance to hide something (the eye audit of the
      same run shows the later place of orange at free_017 cut the
      visible target-class body blue from 507 to 62 px, and red at
      free_005 stood on the camera ray to purple, which surfaced only
      at 113 px once orange moved).  Fix direction, all inside the
      planner: (a) REJECTED: any rewrite of the plan after solve()
      returns (user directive above); (b) give solve() a success_cost
      bound / a short anytime improvement window after the first plan
      so a cheaper plan wins when one exists, at a planning-time price
      to be measured per search configuration; (c) a search portfolio:
      w = 1 under a short per-search cap, falling back to w = 2, so the
      w = 1 price is paid only where it is bounded; (d) for the paper
      report plan length per episode as the satisficing search
      produces it.  Not a correctness defect; interacts with F11
      (placement corridor blocking) and #P3(c) (reach-bounded
      placements).

  F31. [DONE 2026-09-19; dedicated A/B NOT RUN, see the status note at
      the end of this entry] A PLACEMENT MAY HIDE A KNOWN BODY
      FROM THE CAMERA.  LANDED: pddlstream_planner._compute_placement_
      hides emits a static (placement_hides ?o ?b) for every (movable
      object, free cell) whose virtual box at the cell (footprint
      centred, bottom on the cell floor, the object's estimated
      extents; identical-extent objects share one test as in F11)
      intercepts at least PLACEMENT_HIDES_MIN_FRACTION 0.5 of the
      camera rays to a 3 x 3 x 3 lattice over another registered
      object's box (the tray excluded either way; pure belief
      geometry: registry boxes + camera pose).  Domain: :action place
      requires (not (placement_hides ?o ?b)); the SOFT form is a second
      action place_hiding with the positive fact and cost 3 (FastDownward
      action costs are static, so a conditional increase was not an
      option) — the executor treats it exactly as place.  The hard form
      alone starved placements: seed 13 plan 4 went from the usual F7
      binding death to "no plan at all"; with the soft form the
      skeletons are back (the no-plan search there now takes 33 s
      instead of 2.5 s: two place actions double the grounded
      placements; measured in the A/B).  Threshold rationale: the
      22-12-06 evidence cut 88 % of a body's pixels; from this camera
      the low lattice points are intercepted first, so at 50 % the top
      face — the surface the estimate is anchored to — is still seen;
      a grazed corner is not a hidden body.  Seed 13's own evidence
      (orange over blue, red over purple) is NOT reproduced by the
      fact: blue and purple were unregistered (hidden) bodies at the
      time — the fact protects KNOWN bodies only, by definition.  Smoke
      seed 13 (F31 code): red at free_005 still cut orange from 1708 to
      996 px (42 %) with its top in view, below the criterion; seed 999
      emitted 2 facts and succeeded.  [F31-diag] prints the per-object
      count each replan.  Original finding: the placement-blocking facts
      (audit #5 / F11, blocks_view_at over FREE cells) protect only the
      sense corridors to SHADOW fragments; nothing stops the planner
      from putting an occluder between the camera and a registered
      object.  Evidence (user GUI run 22-12-06, seed 13, eye --audit):
      place(orange -> free_017) cut blue_object from 507 to 62 visible
      px, and red at free_005 stood on the camera ray to purple, which
      surfaced at only 113 px once orange left.  Harm: a hidden known
      body cannot be re-posed by refresh_object_aabbs (its belief goes
      stale the moment a transit disturbs it), cannot be re-detected
      (F20 channel closed), and in find-and-tray-stack or a multi-
      target goal it may be the next target; every such placement also
      creates un-modelled occlusion behind the placed object (relocated
      objects cast no new shadow, methods.tex:157 — sound for the
      TARGET only because the cell behind was observed clear, i.e. a
      free cell, before the placement).  FIX DESIGN (domain surgery,
      hence its own audit round + A/B before landing, like F6): (1) in
      _build_init, next to the F11 egregious-triple test, evaluate for
      every (movable object o, free cell b) whether o's box placed at b
      intercepts the camera rays to any OTHER registered object's boxel
      (ray-vs-AABB slab test on the boxel's top-face corners + centre;
      ~6 objects x ~40 cells x 5 rays per replan, negligible) and emit
      a static (placement_hides ?o ?b) fact; (2) add (not
      (placement_hides ?o ?b)) to :action place's precondition
      (domain_pddlstream.pddl; stack onto an object is exempt — the
      support is the thing being covered); (3) prefer a SOFT form if
      the hard form starves placements on crowded tables: cost 3
      instead of 1 for a hiding placement via a conditional (increase
      (total-cost)) — check FastDownward's cost handling for
      conditional effects first; (4) A/B on seeds 0-19 + 100-119 for
      success, plans/episode and planning time; (5) the eye's --audit
      "placements that changed what the camera sees" is the acceptance
      test: zero entries on the smoke battery.  Interacts with #P3(c)
      (reach-bounded placement candidates: fewer cells, more pressure)
      and F11 (the corridor facts already remove many cells).
      [A/B STATUS 2026-09-20, stated honestly: the dedicated F31 arm was
      never completed — chain 2's arm was discarded when the F7 fix
      landed on top of it, and no arm on disk isolates F31.  The only

      [DEFECT FOUND AND FIXED 2026-09-20 by the round-3 adversarial
      review (two of seven readers found it independently, and it
      survived the refutation pass): _compute_placement_hides built its
      "known bodies that must not be occluded" set from every OBJECT
      boxel except the tray, with NO held-object filter.  On a replan
      while holding, the held object's boxel is the stale pick-time box
      — the same staleness that made this commit drop its location
      facts, and refresh_object_aabbs never re-poses a held body — so
      the planner protected a position the object had already left, and
      OTHER objects' placements at cells on that dead sight line were
      flagged placement_hides and cost 2 instead of 1.  Bounded, and
      conservative in direction: place and place_hiding have identical
      preconditions and effects and differ only in the polarity of the
      fact and in cost, so nothing became infeasible.  Under
      --unit-costs the fact is inert.  Fix: the function takes held_obj
      and skips it when collecting the bodies to protect.
      [CORRECTION 2026-09-20, round 4, on BOTH the fix and this note.
      (1) THE NOTE WAS WRONG where it said "the held object's own
      placement was already exempt (the emitter skips pairs naming the
      hidden body)".  The inner guard is `if obj_id != tid`, which
      exempts the held object only as the HIDDEN body, never as the
      PLACED one, so (held, cell) pairs were emitted before the fix and
      the disposal fallback WAS affected.  (2) THE FIRST FIX OVERSHOT
      and silently disabled F31 on the only action it constrains.  The
      loop feeds two different structures: `known`, the bodies whose
      sight lines are protected, which is POSITION data and where the
      held object's staleness genuinely disqualifies it; and
      `ext_classes`, keyed on EXTENTS alone, which is where the emitted
      pairs draw their placed object from.  Extents do not go stale
      when an object is carried.  Excluding the held object from both
      meant no placement_hides fact could ever name it — and since
      place and place_hiding both require (holding ?o), the placed
      object is ALWAYS the held one, so every placement became cost 1
      and F31 stopped discriminating.  Corrected: skip it in `known`
      only.  Log-unobserved (no F31-era log has a plan with a non-None
      held_obj), but live on the replan-while-holding and disposal
      paths, which August-and-later logs exercise in 84 % of holding
      replans.]  Provenance: the defect is a41cb12's, i.e. F31's
      own, not the round-2 commits'.  NOTE for anyone reproducing it:
      the [F31-diag] block keys on the PLACED object and never names
      the hidden body, so this case is invisible in that log, and every
      [F31-diag] line on disk comes from a plan with held_obj=None —
      there is no recorded instance, only the code path.]

  F32. [OPEN — PLANNING, found 2026-09-19 by the F7 fix] CROWDED-TABLE
      DEAD END: with every belief fact consistent (F7), seed 13 plan 4
      is an honest no-plan — two unknown fragments, each blocked (the
      target's blocker green plus orange on one, purple on the other),

      blocker's facts pointed at a dead cell and the derived
      blocks_view ignored it — a belief error, not a plan.  Levers, all
      inside the model: (a) the F11 PAIR criterion keys corridor facts
      to the CELL's AABB — a coarse merged cell blocks a corridor its
      centre placement would not; the per-object virtual-box test
      (what the census will see) is the exact prediction and would
      free such cells — the F11 note reverted an honest-MARGIN attempt,
      not this; (b) finer free-space cells near corridors; (c) a
      progress-aware partial sense (F12).  Lever (a) is behind
      --corridor-test {cell+object (default, F11), object} and gets its
      own A/B arm in the post-F7 checkpoint (arm f32); with the reach
      centre corrected the same day, seeds 13 and 119 end in this
      honest no-plan at plan 3 within seconds instead of the minutes-
      long binding deaths.

  guarantee is gone from test_full_pipeline, the Phase-4 oracle line no
  longer claims a scene is unwinnable (it states that discovery must
  come from re-detection instead), and the "pre-flight and real run

  pre-flight is scene CONSTRUCTIBILITY (placement exhaustion, the
  audit-#70 reach assert) plus the find-and-tray-stack role split,
  which is goal FORMATION (the goal builder needs one visible and one
  hidden target to build a tower), not a winnability judgement.
  RATIONALE: the guardrail encoded "fragment containment is the only
  route to discovery", which is false — clearing the occluders and
  LOOKING is another, and the render already supports it.  Its real

================================================================================
#P2. [OPEN T0]  STACKING-ACCURACY DROP: PROVE IT IS EXECUTION, DETECT PHYSICAL
     FAILURE (KNOCKED TOWERS, OBJECTS OFF THE TABLE) AND FAIL HONESTLY
================================================================================
The paper must address why stack success collapses with height (semantic
100/80/13.3 % at h=2/3/4).  Supervisor position (matches thesis §5, CB#116,
TH#281/#304/#332): the planner finds valid plans at every height; the SIMULATED
ARM rams the tower and knocks everything over: a simulation/manipulation failure,
not a planning failure.  The thesis discloses this but never fixed or fully
instrumented it (TH#332: "not an issue to resolve" for the thesis; it IS one for
the paper).  Also required: when an object is knocked OFF the table, recognise it
and fail the episode instead of retrying forever.  This detection layer is part
of the #P1 perception system.

WHAT THE CODE DOES TODAY (2026-08-15 scout):
  - detect_execution_collisions (robot_utils.py:307-381) runs after every move
    and pick, but BOTH call sites discard the return value
    (test_full_pipeline.py:1213-1218, :1298-1303).  Collisions print and change
    nothing (CB#42 deferred this; the deferral dies here).
  - No code checks mid-episode whether any object left the table or workspace.
    _verify_cube_on/_verify_on_table/_verify_holding run only at goal-check time.
    A cube knocked off the table mid-plan is invisible to the loop; deferred #83
    even states "cube-fell-off-the-workspace recovery is out of scope".  The
    empirical marker exists: the spawn window's x lower bound (-0.251) is where
    cubes used to tip off the table edge (boxel_env.py:823-826).
  - Stack toppling is likely SILENTLY UNCAUGHT: physics_mismatch (false success)
    occurs ONLY on holding, never on stack (36 vs 0 cells), which is backwards:
    a toppled tower should trigger it (CB#122, with author notes; repro seeds
    0,4,15,19,20,34,36,39,43,44,45,48,59,63,75,87,92 on sweep_full_2026-05-28).
  - CB#121: stack-uniform fails via fast "no plan found" (~48 no-plan vs ~7
    timeout) despite the huge grounded domain.  Author hypothesis: mid-execution
    knock-down, replan from scattered state finds no plan.  Same manipulation
    fragility, different exit reason.

  [KNOCKED-OFF-TABLE EVIDENCE 2026-08-21 (run 22-11-42, tray-stack seed
  343879192): a cyan transit slip (F10 mechanism) LAUNCHED the goal cube
  off the table — it landed on the floor at y=1.27 m, 1.276 m from the
  base.  The planner correctly could not reach it (pre-IK gate lines
  name the distance) and spent 107 s exhausting sense-based alternative
  skeletons before a GENERIC "no plan (9 unsearched shadows remaining)".
  Deliverable (a)'s monitor would classify this as knocked_off_table
  immediately after the failed transit — same episode outcome, honest
  label, two minutes sooner.]

      false-success physics checks; hooks where the discarded collision returns
      already flow.

      Root-cause the h=4 collapse with the monitor's data: confirm (or refute)

  [#P2 (a), (c), (d) + F28 + F26 LANDED 2026-09-19.
  (a) WORLD-INTEGRITY MONITOR: execution.world_integrity_check runs
  after every executed move, pick, place and stack (test_full_pipeline
  _integrity_after): ONE render (env.detect_objects, the sense
  action's own channel; the simulator is never read) reconciles every
  registered OBJECT boxel with what the camera shows — knocked_off_
  table (its detection lies wholly below the table surface; the F22
  support completion now leaves such a cloud alone instead of snapping
  it up), lost (no detection and its believed region renders empty on
  every slice: the step-(3c) check), disturbed (detected more than
  INTEGRITY_DISTURB_M 15 mm from its believed centre; the boxel is
  re-posed with the rigid-size fusion), toppled (raw detection far
  lower and wider than its rigid box — a box on its side; a cube's
  topple is not visible in an AABB, stated), off_support (believed
  stacked but its bottom is no longer at its support's top within
  INTEGRITY_SUPPORT_TOL_M 2 cm: the on-relation is dropped so the
  planner re-stacks).  Knocked-off and lost bodies are retired
  (retire_lost_objects); after any event the census is re-run.  The
  held body is excluded (F26's first while-holding site: exclude_ids
  is passed), the tray is fixed.  REACTION: a goal-critical object
  that left the belief ends the episode at once with exit_reason
  knocked_off_table (honest failure, no blind retry); any other
  belief change with an empty hand breaks the plan for a replan (the
  loop's reactive replanning on an unexpected observation); while
  holding, the plan continues and the release observation / next
  replan absorb it.  Telemetry's ground-truth DISTURBED / TOPPLE /
  OFF_SUPPORT events remain the validation labels for these verdicts
  in the run log; a "[integrity] after <action>: n/m believed bodies

  (c) CB#122 half: a stack goal whose symbolic success the end-of-run

  #P2(b) ROOT CAUSE, 2026-09-20.  READ THE CORRECTION: an earlier
  version of this block, written the same day, claimed "the arm-tower
  hypothesis is REFUTED" and "the tower was never touched".  That was
  WRONG, and the round-4 review caught it from the same run.  I had
  traced one window (t=22.5 s) and never looked at t=16.6 s, where the
  tower actually died.  The corrected account follows; the supervisor's
  long-standing position is essentially SUPPORTED, with one refinement
  that matters for the fix.
  Three h=4 runs with a dense telemetry cadence (--telemetry-period
  0.02; seeds 0, 3, 7; scratchpad p2b_h4.sh):
    seed 0  FAILED pick_giveup (3 failed picks of red_object, whose
            belief had drifted 45 mm)
    seed 3  SUCCESS — a clean four-level tower
    seed 7  FAILED stack_giveup
  So h=4 is not one failure mode: it is at least three, and one of the
  three seeds succeeds outright.
  SEED 7, THE WHOLE CHAIN (logs/run_2026-09-20_15-14-17;
  timing_summary.json: exit_reason stack_giveup, plan_count 9,
  n_integrity_events 9).  ONE initiating event with a long tail:
  (1) THE CARGO DESTROYS THE TOWER, at plan#1's own fourth-level
      placement, 6 s before anything else:
        step 3987 t=16.6125  stack(blue_object, green_object)
          orange tilt 0.04 z .350 | red tilt 0.04 z .400 |
          green tilt 0.03 z .450   (the three-level tower, upright)
          blue  tilt 0.45 speed 0.7046 contacts ['robot:9','robot:10']
        step 3990 t=16.6250  <- the held cube reaches the tower top
          green tilt 3.18 speed 0.5976 (was 0) contacts [red, blue]
        step 4032            tilts 48.93 / 48.94 / 48.96 — LOCKSTEP,
                             i.e. the stack topples as a rigid unit
        step 4054            tilts 92.96 / 85.69 / 92.79 — all flat
      Upright to flat in about 0.28 s, beginning in the exact frame the
      gripper-held cube, still descending at 0.67-0.70 m/s, touched the
      top of the tower.  The monitor's own first two integrity events
      are that collapse: off_support red_object on orange_object,
      gap -51.2 mm, and green_object on red_object, -51.1 mm.  The run
      log independently prints DISTURBED red 4.5 mm "though the arm
      never grasped it" and FLING green 0.60-0.62 m/s "untouched by the
      arm" — that phrase means no robot LINK is in the contact set, and
      it does not exclude the robot-held cargo, which is what hit it.
      The same signature recurs at plan#7 (t=42.26) and plan#9
      (t=58.90), with further off_support events at plan#7 (-21.7 mm)
      and plan#8 (-51.1 mm).
  (2) A FINGER THEN ROLLS A FALLEN CUBE INTO AN UPRIGHT ONE, during a
      later pick:
        step 5111 t=21.2958  orange tilt 90.00  contacts ['table']
        step 5116 t=21.3167  orange tilt 89.92  contacts ['table',
                                                          'robot:9']
        step 5400 t=22.5000  orange tilt 75.28  contacts ['table',
                             'robot:9','red_object','blue_object']
        step 5460 t=22.7500  orange tilt 69.07  contacts ['table',
                                                          'robot:9']
      Orange had ALREADY been flat for 4.4 s (214 frames, table as its
      only contact) before a finger ever touched it — it fell in the
      collapse at (1), being the tower's bottom cube — and under the
      finger its tilt DECREASES, 90 to 75 to 69.  The finger rolled an
      already-fallen cube; it did not topple it.  What the finger DID
      cause is the next link: the rolled orange drove the still-upright
      blue_object over (blue's only non-table contact through its whole
      topple is orange, never the robot), and blue reached 90.14 deg.
  (3) THE UNDETECTABLE TOPPLE THEN COSTS THE EPISODE.  A cube on its
      side is not distinguishable from an upright one in an AABB
      (stated in the monitor note above), so the belief kept treating
      blue as stackable, the robot picked it up sideways and the drop
      verification correctly rejected the result.  Strike accounting,
      corrected: strike 1 belongs to plan#1 at t=16.4-17.0, BEFORE the
      knock-down, with blue upright (pre-release tilt 0.69, released
      0.00); only strikes 2 (plan#7, pre-release 89.93) and 3 (plan#9,
      90.15) are the sideways-blue mechanism.
  WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE PAPER.  Deferred #84's surviving mechanism is
  CARGO-INTO-TOWER INTERFERENCE AT PLACEMENT — the rim hypothesis —
  not arm-link collision.  The supervisor's "the simulated arm rams the
  tower" is right in substance; the refinement is that the thing that
  rams it is the object being carried, not a link, which is why the
  arm's own collision checking never saw it: the executed descent is a
  straight drop to the release height at transit speed, and the cargo's
  swept volume during that descent is not what plan_motion certified.
  ACTIONABLE, and it is NOT the gripper exemption: the descent should
  approach the support slowly and stop on contact, the way
  close_gripper_until_contact already does for grasping, instead of
  arriving at 0.7 m/s.  A force- or contact-terminated placement would
  have prevented event (1) outright.  The blanket gripper-collision
  exemption (see F18's residual) explains only event (2), and the
  ignored-body fix proposed there would NOT have saved this episode.
  Both remain worth doing; this one first.
  Evidence tools: tools/_p2b_tilt.py, _p2b_chain.py.  METHOD NOTE for
  whoever reads telemetry next: trace the WHOLE episode before naming a
  mechanism.  The error above came from reading one window, and the
  phrase "untouched by the arm" in the run log is exactly the kind of
  line that invites it.

  (d) CB#124: timing_summary.json now carries integrity_events (per
  action, with kind / object / plan / measure), n_integrity_events,

  [REVIEW FOLLOW-UP 2026-09-19 (adversarial reviewer over 067630e..
  f6ae28e; findings acted on): (i) retire_lost_objects no longer
  declares a retired object's shadow fragments empty on the strength
  of "the caster is gone" — with the observation at hand (the sense

  [REVIEW FOLLOW-UP 2 (same day, the reviewer's final report, 14
  findings; the first three were the ones above).  Acted on: (2) the
  monitor's render is an observation of the whole workspace — its
  detections go through register_new_detections (held body skipped),
  so a target uncovered by a knocked-away occluder enters the belief
  there and a run cannot end "all searched" with the target in plain
  view; (3) "lost" (believed region observed empty) no longer ends the
  episode like knocked_off_table — the object may resurface through
  re-detection, the loop goes on and the all-searched exit is the
  honest end if it never does; (4) disturbances of one body force a
  replan at most three times (the belief is updated regardless, the
  plan runs on) so an arm-adjacent bystander cannot churn the loop —
  together with the arm-occlusion guard above; (6) a toppled body is
  re-initialised from the raw detection (its box IS a new rigid size
  until it stands again) instead of being unioned into a box no grasp
  spans; (7) a re-posing refresh sets the registry dirty flag so the
  next replan re-carves free space (a rejected release could leave a
  consumed cell reading free); (9) after events the plan client is re-
  synced from the belief at once (the runtime plan_motion re-solve
  runs before the next replan's sync); (13) _build_init's placement
  filter tests the same target compute_kin tests (cell centre + the
  vertical grasp offset).  Disclosed, not changed: (5) a release the
  camera cannot see after the lift is accepted on the tactile checks
  and logged "unobserved" — the next observation (monitor, sense)
  corrects the belief, and the eye records it; (8) stack_giveup now
  counts only the robot's own (observation) failures; (10) tactile
  contact queries are filtered by the target's body id — an identity
  the segmentation mask supplies for free, stated in the paper as the
  sim-grade identity assumption; (12) the reach-constrained
  scalability spawns change the benchmark distribution — every pre-
  range A/B is on random-pairs (already constrained), CB#113 is the
  re-sweep; (13) constants without a measurement behind them are
  named as such: INTEGRITY_DISTURB_M 15 mm (above the ~2 mm estimate
  noise and the 8 mm pre-grasp clearance), INTEGRITY_SUPPORT_TOL_M
  2 cm (the release observation's own tolerance), toppled 3 cm, the
  release observation's 1.5 cm upright margins, the 3 mm / 20-step
  stillness test, the 1 cm re-observation top test, the +8 mm width
  slack, the 0.5 mm/step tactile close, IK_ORN_TOL_DEG 10,
  SUPPORT_FOOTPRINT_MIN_FRACTION 0.25 — all conservative by
  construction, none tuned on a seed, all to be reported as design
  choices.]
  [REVIEW ROUND 2 (2026-09-19, over f6ae28e..adf5320; report
  scratchpad/review_cheats_round2.md; no new oracle read, no plan
  post-processing, no seed-specific code; the F7 fix and the reach
  centre verified against the URDF).  Acted on: (1) COST MODEL — with
  stack at 2 and place_hiding at 3 a held occluder was cheaper to
  stack on a 3 cm cube than to place where it would hide a body; now
  place 1 < place_hiding 2 < stack 3, a placement of any kind stays
  cheaper than stacking; preferences under the weighted search, not
  guarantees, and --unit-costs disables both (help says so).  (2) the
  monitor's registration of a newly seen body now counts as a belief
  change: the plan client is re-synced (the body was parked below the
  table until the next replan's sync) and the plan breaks for a replan
  with an empty hand.  (3) the disturbance cap is per PLAN and its
  suppression is printed.  (4) the initial problem export syncs the
  plan client from the belief first (under --sense-gate strict its
  raycast saw true spawn poses).  (5) a target that was seen, then

  exit_reason target_lost, not all_searched; the knocked_off_table
  message no longer says "or lost".  (7) the held object gets no
  obj_at_boxel / on_table fact while held (holding is its state; its
  stale box had re-pinned it as the blocker of its own fragment for a

  static per replan (a body placed earlier in the same plan is
  unprotected at its new pose) and stack onto the tray is exempt;
  (10) under --sense-gate strict the dense raycast also runs for
  registered objects whose Know-If facts already exclude sensing

  obj_at_boxel fact (unpickable only through GRASP_MAX_SPAN), and

  URDF shape at the lower centre; (14) init-fact order changed with
  the F6 fact set, so seed-for-seed comparisons against pre-F6
  references are not order-identical (the noise floor already covers
  this).]

================================================================================
#P3. [OPEN T1]  ALL OBJECTS MUST SPAWN WITHIN REACHABLE RANGE (TAMPURA-STYLE)
================================================================================
Supervisor doubt is JUSTIFIED: the reachability constraint exists but the eval's
own scene does not use it.  boxel_env defines a 0.65 m reach disk around the base
(-0.4, 0) and a _SAFE_TABLE_ window (x [-0.1,0.70], y [-0.40,0.40]) applied only
when cfg.constrain_to_reach=True (boxel_env.py:808-929).  stack_scene sets it
(:267); random_pairs_scene forces it (:394); but scalability_scene NEVER sets it
(SceneConfig default False, :148), so it samples the wider unconstrained window
(x [-0.20,0.60], y [-0.50,0.50], :830-831), and the eval sweep runs scalability
cells (eval_runner.py:416-421).  The thesis sentence "random reachable positions"
(results.tex:81) is therefore not guaranteed for part of the data.

TAMPURA'S REFERENCE PATTERN (find_dice/env_generator.py): spawn x in [0.3,0.7],
y in [-0.4,0.4] in front of the fixed base; every spawn camera-visibility
checked; whole scene regenerated (up to 20x) until the die is genuinely hidden;
PLACE destinations separately constrained to a shortened 0.75 x 1.22 m reachable
table band (get_shortened_table_dims).

DELIVERABLES: (a) constrain_to_reach=True for every scene used in evals (or
default True + explicit opt-out), keep the _assert_xys_in_reach post-spawn net;
(b) quantify the damage: count sweep cells whose spawns violated the reach disk
(recomputable from logged spawn poses), report whether any headline number moves;
(c) verify PLACEMENT sampling (streams place/stack targets) is also
reach-bounded, mirroring TAMPURA's shortened-table pattern; (d) numbers refresh
folds into the post-#P1 canonical sweep (CB#113).
  [EVIDENCE 2026-08-21 for (c): user GUI runs 16-23-24 / 16-24-28
  (random-pairs, post-spacing tree): PDDLStream repeatedly bound placements
  at unreachable free-space boxels — free_009 ee target [0.45, 0.25, 0.52]
  and free_001 [0.5, 0.0, 0.675] — and all 8 IK seeds were rejected at the
  FK gate (147-251 mm residuals) on every resample, burning the full
  sampling budget; both runs were killed mid-sampling.  Placement
  reach-bounding applies to random-pairs too, not only scalability spawns.
  MITIGATED 2026-08-21 (2129bf3): compute_kin fast-fails targets more
  than 0.80 m horizontal from the base BEFORE the IK loop — kills the
  wall-clock burn; candidate GENERATION is still unbounded, so the full
  reach-bounding of placement sampling remains this deliverable.
  EXTENDED SAME DAY (29ad499) after a user GUI field report (run
  22-01-06): the pre-IK gate alone rejects a placement only AFTER
  FastDownward commits a plan to it — the GUI run burned a 99 s search
  round on free_008 at 0.802 m, then started the next 110 s round and
  the user killed it.  _build_init now filters free boxels beyond the
  shared streams.REACH_LIMIT_M (0.80 m) out of the boxel_fits PLACE
  candidates entirely (shadow fits stay un-gated — hideability is not a
  reach property).  Side effect: the search space shrinks — holding
  seed 0 plan #1 dropped 31 s -> 2.1 s, stack h=2 -> 0.96 s.
  Remaining for (c): stack/tray destinations and the TAMPURA-style
  shortened-table PLACE band; spawns (a)/(b) unchanged.]
  [LANDED 2026-09-19.  (a) scalability_scene sets constrain_to_reach=
  True (random-pairs and stack already did; the fixed preset scenes
  keep their pre-baked positions) — every eval scene now spawns in the
  _SAFE_TABLE_* window inside the 0.65 m disk.  Changes every
  scalability seed's layout; CB#113 re-sweeps.  (c) HEIGHT-AWARE REACH:
  tools/_probe_reach_envelope.py measured the top-down IK envelope on
  an empty table with the real 8-seed FK-gated IK (342 XY targets x 6
  heights x 2 yaws): the boundary is a sphere of radius ~0.823 m
  centred at world z 0.066 m (1.6 cm above the Panda's base frame,
  which PyBullet reports at z 0.05; a first cut placed the centre
  0.066 above the base, 5 cm too high, and let a 0.83 m cell through
  that IK failed by 11-13 mm at every seed — the seed-119 binding
  death after the F7 fix) — reach 0.765 m at 4.5 cm above
  the table, 0.743 at 10 cm, 0.711 at 16 cm, 0.680 at 20 cm, 0.652 at
  24.5 cm, 0.604 at 30 cm (fit within 3 mm of every row; both yaws
  identical to 1 %).  streams.ee_reachable(target) = inside that
  sphere (R 0.815, 8 mm margin) AND the flat 0.80 m limit; used by
  compute_kin's pre-IK gate, by compute_stack_kin (which had no gate:
  a tower at the reach margin ate full 8-seed IK rounds per grasp) and
  by _build_init's placement filter per (object, cell) with the place
  EE target = cell + object half-height + the lowest admissible grasp
  offset — so a far cell is not offered for a tall object while a
  low one may still be.  Trigger: the first step-(4) A/B, seed 5
  plan 5, cells 0.69 m out at z 0.57 passed the flat gate and failed
  IK by 36 mm at every attempt.  (b) quantifying the damage in the
  old sweep is moot — the system under test changed — CB#113 on the
  constrained scenes is the number.  The TAMPURA shortened-table
  PLACE band is subsumed by the envelope (a geometric statement of the
  same thing, measured instead of copied).]

#P4. [OPEN T0]  RUN OUR PIPELINE INSIDE THE TAMPURA ENV FOR A VALID COMPARISON
================================================================================
The thesis comparison is honest but indirect (TH#306): local TAMPURA re-run on
find_dice vs our pipeline on OUR scenes.  The paper needs ours running in THEIR
env, seed-for-seed (reuse TAMPURA's exact 20 seeds; bridge solve stays
seed-generic).

DECISION 2026-08-20 (user): the existing bridge implementation is DISCARDED —
"remove all the work done in tampura bridge. that work was trash. we should
start over."  The whole tampura_bridge/ tree (genuine bridge, smoke probes,
captures) moved to archive/tampura_bridge_v1/ (also recoverable pre-move from
git history).  The CB#120/CB#123 state notes below describe that ARCHIVED
version — they stay as the requirements list for the restart.  The restart
must build on the post-#P1 weld-free pipeline from day one; #P1's bridge-
mirroring obligations are void until the new bridge exists.

The DISCARDED vehicle was: tampura_bridge/ (ran out-of-process inside
their WSL venv via PYTHONPATH injection; BoxelPolicy drove TAMPURA's own rollout
via no-op actions + belief_bridge writes into their SceneBelief; entry point
run_boxel_on_finddice.py).

STATE OF CB#120 (the fair-comparison blocker list), verified 2026-08-15:
  FIXED in the current GENUINE bridge: (a) real streams (planner_boxel.py +
  streams_boxel.py + pddl/domain_find_dice_streamful.pddl); (b) real motion
  planning (RRT-Connect via _plan_follow); (c) collision checking; (e) visibility
  via segment_visible (render + segmentation), not the AABB oracle.
  PARTIAL: (d) physics: grasp is contact-verified and objects made dynamic, but
  the hold is still the JOINT_FIXED weld, "consistent with the shared pipeline".
  Once #P1 removes the weld in the main pipeline, the bridge MUST follow, or the
  comparison is unfaithful again.
  STILL OPEN: (f) containment regions: find_dice hides the die by CO-LOCATION
  under a cup; our lateral shadows never contain it, so the semantic partition
  does not drive the search there: model cup-footprint containment regions.
  (g) the arm-park before each sense is bridge-internal, not a planned action.
  (h) full overlay redraw each refresh (perf only).

MUST-FIX BEFORE ANY QUOTED NUMBER (CB#123, verified still present verbatim):
boxel_policy.py:372-377 discards execute_go_home's status and unconditionally
marks at_home + success ("no_path" still scores reward 1.0); execute_pick:263
ignores the lift's status ("ok" without lifting).  Thread both statuses; on
go_home no_path record fail_reason go_home_no_path.

THEN: PHASE 4, the gated >= 20-seed seed-for-seed bridge eval on their exact
seeds, honest failures included: this replaces the thesis's indirect comparison
as the paper's headline TAMPURA result.  Also regenerate the wall-clock plot
(CB#111) and fix the stale eval_plotter docstring (CB#125) when touching it.
Architectural decision recorded: the no-op-policy harness is acceptable for the
paper (it drives their env, belief, and reward untouched); switching to native
action-schema execution via their effects_fn/execute_fn is optional extra
faithfulness, decide with supervisors if time allows.
