--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
P4.0  WHAT THE USER ASKED FOR (2026-10-25, binding)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
"Make little changes and just let our setup work on their find_dice problem.
We might need to change our model to allow the idea of looking underneath a
certain kind of object.  Every tweak is honestly reported.  Then run this on
TAMPURA's env vs the TAMPURA system on the same seeds and see who did better,
how quickly.  No welds, no oracles, no hardcoded solutions.  The planner stays
the same; a different planner is out of scope.  The point is to make the thesis
claims comparable, and as a result provable or at least justifiable.  Any
change that breaks that honesty is flagged and aborted.  All changes stay
faithful to the POD line of work.  Later: other tasks, possibly LW1 in a 3D
Wumpus world.  Keep a documented record of every decision and fix in the style
of the codebase audit."

THE FAILURE THAT MUST NOT REPEAT.  The first bridge (archive/tampura_bridge_v1,
discarded 2026-08-20) scored 20/20 because bridge code, not the planner, chose
which cup to move (boxel_policy.py:83-98 hand-built a one-cup init so exactly
one plan existed), where to place it (_next_place_xy, :160-176, justified in a
comment by where the hidden die was), deleted the planned move actions and
re-planned transit itself (:215-238), parked the arm before every sense without
a planned action (:295-296, :345-348), declared every shadow known-empty
without an observation once the die was visible (:248-258), swapped the domain
through a planner subclass (planner_boxel.py:28-49), welded the grasp
(execution_boxel.py:94-107), grasped at the object's live simulator AABB
(:191-197), kept the hidden die's body id in the streams from step 0
(_streams_smoke.py:33), and wrote at_home and success into TAMPURA's belief
whether or not the arm had moved (boxel_policy.py:372-381, CB#123).  The
planner only ever emitted [pick cup, place, sense, pick die, go_home].

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
P4.1  HONESTY CONTRACT H1-H6 AND THE GATES G1-G21 THAT ENFORCE IT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
H1  No welds or constraints, no teleports of task objects or of the robot, no
    ground-truth reads (true poses, body-id matching beyond the declared
    identity map, TAMPURA's belief or world internals) feeding any decision:
    planning, perception, belief update, success scoring.
H2  The planner is PDDLStream + Fast Downward exactly as in the main pipeline:
    no planner swap, no domain swap outside a declared and generated delta, no
    plan post-processing, no hardcoded action sequence, no scripted decision
    logic, no robot motion outside a planned action.  Every WHAT / WHERE /
    ORDER decision is the planner's, computed from the belief.
H3  Model tweaks are allowed only if small, expressible in the thesis's POD
    model (belief as literals over named regions, a sense action with a
    deterministic observation, hidden regions as belief volumes, discovery by
    relocation and sensing, optimistic determinisation, replanning), and each
    has its own audit entry with BEFORE / AFTER / CLAIM EFFECT lines.
H4  Seed for seed: TAMPURA's exact seeds 0-19, their scene files untouched,
    their success criterion, honest failures counted.  No seed- or scene-
    specific code; no decision rule fitted to the released layouts.  Never
    edit their tree.
H5  Anything that cannot satisfy H1-H4 is flagged and ABORTED in this record,
    never worked around.
H6  No adaptation is named "faithful"; neutral verbs (execute_pick).  Prose for
    the user and the paper carries no dashes.

GATES.  tools/bridge_gates.py (B0) implements every gate as a runnable check;
it runs before every bridge commit and before any quoted number.  Each gate
names the archived violation it would have caught.  Every runtime hook below
both raises AND increments a violation counter that is read after the episode
(G21), because main-pipeline diagnostics swallow broad exceptions
(world_eye.py:172-176, telemetry.py:201-205).
G1  Weld/teleport grep over the CAMPAIGN IMPORT GRAPH (every module imported
    from run_bridge_on_finddice.py or tools/run_tampura_timed.py, listed by
    an import-graph check; tampura_bridge/tests/ lies outside it and nothing
    on it imports tests/) for every pybullet call that writes world or robot
    state.  The grep list is the current instance of that rule:
    createConstraint, changeConstraint, removeBody, createMultiBody,
    loadURDF, loadSDF, loadMJCF, loadBullet, loadSoftBody, saveState,
    restoreState, resetSimulation, setRealTimeSimulation,
    resetBasePositionAndOrientation, resetBaseVelocity, resetJointState,
    resetJointStateMultiDof(s), applyExternalForce, applyExternalTorque,
    setCollisionFilterPair, setCollisionFilterGroupMask, set_group_positions,
    set_pose(, changeDynamics, setGravity, setTimeStep,
    setPhysicsEngineParameter.  Every hit is on the allowlist or the gate
    fails.  B0 generates the allowlist from a real grep into one reviewed
    file, tools/bridge_gates_allowlist.txt (file:line, reason, class:
    plan client, D9 set-up, G14 exemption, unreachable on the bridge path,
    or test control), fixed before review gate 1 and cited in its BF#.  It
    holds at least: the plan-twin mirror writes; D9's set-up; our own scene
    construction in boxel_env (_setup_scene, the spawn helpers and reset(),
    unreachable on the adopt-client path, asserted by B3(b); the adapter's
    reset() raises); the save/probe/restore writes of
    robot_utils.is_config_collision_free (robot_utils.py:235, 269, 324, 327;
    plan client at run time), of solve_ik (:566, 618, 671; the G14
    exemption) and the plan-client IK seed write (streams.py:359-361); the
    overlay bodies of visualization.py (:66, 83, 176, 254, 327, 344;
    unreachable because every bridge run passes draw_boxel_overlays=False);
    and the set-up calls of the G6 controls in tampura_bridge/tests/.
    (execution_bridge.py:34-35,57,62,101,114-116,152; execution_boxel.py:69,
    105,183,236-238; sense_config.py:34; boxelize.py:150-152.)
G2  Ground-truth read grep over decision code (policy, planner, streams,
    perception, executor): getBasePositionAndOrientation, get_pose(, get_aabb(,
    getAABB(, rayTest(, rayTestBatch(, world.poses, belief.object_poses,
    visibility_grid, reads of ObjectInfo.position.  Allowed only in the
    scoring adapter's contact and joint readback, the plan-twin mirror, ray
    casts whose client is the plan twin (B3(c); G8(b) checks the client at
    run time) and a declared WRITE-ONLY set, generated by B0's grep into the
    same reviewed allowlist file: world_eye, telemetry, the print-only
    diagnostics at execution.py:605-606, 657-658, 1132, 1260 and the
    execute_stack prints at :1567, 1571, 1630, the scoring-only verifiers at
    test_full_pipeline.py:179-180, 218 (recorded, not acted on, :2219-2225),
    and the D9 after_settle hook; the Phase-4 oracle read at :697 is
    unreachable under oracle_phases=False.  A leak test asserts that no value
    from the write-only set, and no body id from a tactile contact list
    (G8(b)), reaches the registry, the belief, the planner or the executor's
    choices.
    (perception_adapter.py:127-128; execution_boxel.py:88,102,191,285-287;
    streams_boxel.py:419,491.)
G3  Executed-vs-planned ledger plus a motion-layer hook.  Every executed
    primitive is step i of plan k, executed in order from step 1 with
    identical args, no skips, no insertions; a plan ends early only on an
    enumerated and logged trigger (sense outcome, integrity replan, IK
    failure, give-up, deadline); the runtime plan_motion re-solve before a
    move is logged with its goal configuration equal to the planned one.  The
    dispatcher sets and clears a context (plan id, step index); a hook on
    pybullet.setJointMotorControl2/Array, setJointMotorControlMultiDof(Array)
    and robot_utils.move_robot_smooth for the executing robot fails when no
    context is active, so motion commanded
    from outside the dispatcher (execute_fn before or after main(), the env
    adapter, the scoring adapter) cannot pass.  (Dropped moves boxel_policy.py:
    225-226; parks at :295-296, :346-347, which ran outside any dispatcher.)
G4  Place destination fidelity: |executed xy - registry.get_boxel(planned
    boxel).center.xy| below the executor's own tolerance.  (_next_place_xy.)
G5  Belief provenance.  Every SHADOW boxel (lateral or interior) that leaves
    the registry, leaves the belief, or changes status carries a provenance
    row written at the classifier call site, from an enumerated channel:
    planned sense render; full-registry sweep render (execution.py:2185-2201);
    lost-caster retirement render (execution.py:2105-2125); rediscovery
    retirement render (M7); strike park (unresolved, counted separately per
    episode); "observed before placement" (D6, with the free-space boxel and
    region ids it rests on).  A removal or status change with no render of
    that region's grid and no listed channel fails.  If the user rejects M7 (Q10), one more
    channel is enumerated, "superseded by recast, unrendered" (the rediscovery
    cleanup at execution.py:2871-2884), counted per episode with each dropped
    fragment's volume and disclosed as P4.5 correction (6); it is never
    silent.  (known_empty_shadows = all_shadows, boxel_policy.py:253-258;
    per-cup-name bookkeeping :352-353.)
G6  Scoring channel.  The ONLY writer of TAMPURA's belief inputs is
    tampura_bridge/scoring.py (D3).  POSITIVE controls run first and must
    pass: a verified two-pad hold of the die body with the arm at
    DEFAULT_ARM_POS yields get_reward 1.0; the same hold of a cup body yields
    0.0.  Then the fault injections: go_home no_path, a failed lift, a slip
    after the lift; each must end with rewards[-1] == 0.0.  The arm readback
    is logged at main() return (main() leaves the executing client
    connected, B3(b)) and at observation build, and the two must be
    bit-identical; stepSimulation calls on the executing client before
    main() starts and between the end of the last dispatched action and the
    observation build are counted and must be 0, in GUI and headless runs
    alike (bridge runs pass gui_hold_s=0, B3(c)), so no post-hoc settle can
    carry the arm toward the 1e-4 boundary.  The
    RolloutHistory pickle loads in their venv without tampura_bridge on
    PYTHONPATH (B1(j) tests the round trip; D12).  (CB#123.)
G7  Scene-literal grep: "cup_0", "die", cup_[0-9], problem file names, any
    value read from problems/*.json in a decision module.  Allowed only in
    task_spec.py (category table) and log strings.  (boxel_policy.py:43,
    74-99, 310.)
G8  Identity-map boundary.  (a) The env adapter's object table is the seg-id
    to name and category map built from their world.objects and
    world.categories, the same map their obs_from_camera_image applies
    (env.py:70-73); it carries no pose, orientation or AABB for any body
    without an OBJECT boxel.  (b) Runtime hook: pybullet getAABB,
    getBasePositionAndOrientation, getClosestPoints and getContactPoints
    raise when called on the executing client with the body id of a TASK
    body that has no OBJECT boxel (the robot, the plane and the table are
    outside this rule; the executor's tactile queries with bodyA = robot or
    bodyA = held body stay legal, execution.py:483, 639, 1169, 1255;
    robot_utils.py:468, 487; G2's leak test covers the body ids in their
    results), and rayTest and rayTestBatch
    raise on the executing client outright; exceptions: the plan-twin static
    branch, the scoring adapter and the G2 write-only set.  (c) After every
    sync, every
    boxel-less body in the plan twin sits at _PLAN_CLIENT_PARK.  (d) The paper
    states the identity map (seg id to category) as the perception assumption
    both systems share; the main pipeline already hands the target's id to the
    sense classifier as its recognition stand-in (execution.py:88-89, 2474).
G9  Constant provenance.  Every constant the bridge passes into perception,
    planning or execution is the main pipeline's value with a cited line, or
    derived ONCE from the Panda URDF, the YCB meshes, a constant in their
    source (cited) or a scene-independent probe on an empty table, recorded
    in a BF# entry.  Forbidden sources: instance poses, problems/*.json, the
    released layouts, the seed, any episode outcome.  (lift_height tuned to
    the hidden die, execution_boxel.py:210-215.)
G10 Planner and domain identity, unconditional.  At every plan() call:
    type(planner) is PDDLStreamPlanner and type(planner.streams) is
    streams.BoxelStreams exactly (no subclass); sha256 of planner.domain_pddl
    and planner.stream_pddl equals pddl/domain_pddlstream.pddl and
    pddl/stream.pddl, or, when M1 is adopted, the file tools/
    gen_bridge_domain.py regenerates from them plus the declared delta (P4.5
    M1); the regenerated file contains every precondition of the live domain.
    GOAL AND TARGET PIN, also at every plan() call: the goal argument equals
    exactly the mission goal ('and', ('holding', T), ('at_config', q_home))
    (or ('holding', T) when no goal configuration is declared) or exactly
    ('handempty',) for main()'s disposal plan (test_full_pipeline.py:
    1380-1383); T is the one symbol whose category in the identity table
    equals task_spec's target category, and the target_objects argument is
    [T].  main() builds the goal itself (D7 (i)); no caller passes one.  The
    goal is written to the ledger per plan id.  A B0 positive control adds
    one extra obj_at_boxel conjunct and must trip this check.  (Hand-built
    one-plan problem, boxel_policy.py:68-105.)
    (planner_boxel.py:28-49; domain_find_dice.pddl:219-282;
    domain_find_dice_streamful.pddl:101-116.)
G11 Planner call path.  (i) No pddlstream import in tampura_bridge/ or in any
    episode-path module other than pddlstream_planner.py and streams.py.
    (ii) A hook on the solve name bound in pddlstream_planner (imported at
    module load, :33) asserts that the caller is PDDLStreamPlanner.plan and
    that the number of solve calls equals the number of plan ids in the
    ledger.  (iii) The list object PDDLStreamPlanner.plan returned is the
    list the dispatcher iterates (id equality, loop variable logged per
    step).  (Hand-built init + direct solve, boxel_policy.py:68-105;
    _convert_plan :215-238.)
G12 Success parity: success for BOTH arms is rewards[-1] == 1.0 from each
    arm's RolloutHistory.  No bridge-side RESULT line is quoted anywhere.
    (run_boxel_on_finddice.py:69-74.)
G13 Their tree and their loop are untouched.  sha256 over `git ls-files` of
    tampura and tampura_environments before and after every run, and `git
    status` shows no modified tracked file (runs/, temp/, logs/, __pycache__
    are acknowledged untracked outputs; pddlstream's TEMP_DIR is cwd
    relative).  Every loaded tampura module's __file__ lies inside the hashed
    trees.  Exactly two runtime registrations exist and are asserted: the
    boxel_episode schema (arm O, D2) and the tampura_policy_timed policy (arm
    T, P4.6); BoxelBridgePolicy is instantiated directly, not registered.
    type(policy).rollout is Policy.rollout on both arms; the timed subclass's
    __dict__ holds only __init__ and get_action; the env has no instance
    attribute "step".  The T driver is run_planner.py's __main__ copied line
    for line plus one import, kept identical by a diff check.  No monkeypatch
    of their classes.  A pip freeze of arm T's venv is hashed before and
    after every run and must not change (D12).  (boxelize.py:120-131.)
G14 Runtime state trap on the pybullet MODULE (BulletClient forwards to it,
    so one wrap covers both call styles): every state-changing call G1 greps
    for dispatches on (physicsClientId, body id) and fails for any task body
    AND for the robot on the executing client (calls that take no body id,
    such as createMultiBody, the load calls, saveState, restoreState,
    resetSimulation, and setRealTimeSimulation with a nonzero value, fail on
    the executing client outright), except: D9's pre-registered
    set-up before the first physics step; the plan-twin mirror (plan client
    only); the save/probe/restore of the plan-client robot in IK and
    collision checks (streams.py:416-418, 470-473; robot_utils.py:235-327);
    and robot_utils.solve_ik on the EXECUTING robot's arm joints, called by
    execute_pick, execute_place, execute_stack and the post-action lift
    (execution.py:880, 1058-1091, 1380-1381, 1582-1594), valid only inside a
    dispatcher context and asserted net-zero by the hook: no stepSimulation
    during the call and the arm's joint positions restored bit-identically on
    exit.  solve_ik's resetJointState also zeroes the arm's joint velocities;
    that is a main-pipeline property shared with our scenes and is disclosed
    in its BF#.  Task-body poses in the executing world are asserted
    unchanged across every collision check.  (A resetJointState of the arm to
    DEFAULT_ARM_POS before scoring would be CB#123 moved from the belief into
    the simulator; it is not net-zero, so it fails.)
G15 Sense fidelity: a sense outcome is classified from the render of the
    PLANNED region's grid (execution.handle_sense_action).  A body that
    becomes visible elsewhere is registered by register_new_detections and
    logged as discovery; both paths are legitimate, and disabling or delaying
    discovery to make a sense "do it" is forbidden.  (Reveal by whole-scene
    re-segmentation credited to a sense, boxel_policy.py:348-349.)
G16 Oracle paths.  The env adapter (which inherits both from BoxelTestEnv)
    overrides oracle_detect_objects to raise, and its call counter reads 0;
    it overrides update_object_positions as a counted no-op that reads
    nothing, because main() calls it unconditionally (test_full_pipeline.py:
    564, 1734, 1845, 1906, 2085, 2153) and so does every release
    (execution.py:623), while ObjectInfo.position, the field it writes, is
    read only by spawn code and the Phase-4 oracle (boxel_env.py:1365;
    test_full_pipeline.py:696), so skipping the read is decision-neutral.
    G2's leak test asserts ObjectInfo.position is never read on the bridge
    path.
G17 Naming lint: "faithful" absent from bridge code, captures and prose.
G18 Clock: between env.initialize() returning and the first env.step the
    bridge performs only the pinned-map load and G19's assertion, the schema
    registration, the policy's construction (as run_planner.py) and G13's
    runtime check, each stamped in the ledger's driver_clock row (BF#21).
    D9's set-up, the settle,
    the env adapter, the first render, boxelization and shadow casting run
    inside execute_fn, so their RolloutHistory.time_deltas covers them, as it
    covers TAMPURA's own t=0 stream sampling.  Ledger timestamps prove it; the
    durations are logged.  Headless runs only during the campaign.
G19 Seed hygiene on BOTH drivers: random.seed and np.random.seed(global_seed)
    precede env.initialize exactly as run_planner.py does; no bridge or
    driver code consumes the global RNG before get_scene_data; the loaded
    problem file per seed equals the pinned map (P4.2; on the cluster,
    BF#8's map) and is asserted;
    PYTHONHASHSEED is pinned to one value for B1(g), both T repetitions and O.
    Arm O's driver records both RNGs' state before their code and
    PYTHONHASHSEED in the driver_clock row; the ledger reader's G19h checks
    them (BF#21).
G20 Camera: a module-level hook on pybullet.getCameraImage for every client
    checks that the view and projection matrices equal those their own
    pbu.get_image_at_pose path computes at DEFAULT_ARM_POS (D4); write-only
    captures (world_eye images, GUI panes) are declared by name.
    (segment_visible rendered with its own 60 deg projection,
    perception_adapter.py:110-115.)
G21 Gate record: every PER-EPISODE gate writes a count to the ledger: the
    runtime hooks of G3, G5, G6, G8, G10, G11, G14, G16 and G20, and the
    ledger checks of G4, G15, G18 and G19's seed hygiene.  Any count above 0
    fails the episode's gate check whatever happened to the exception.  The
    PER-RUN gates are G13 (tree, registrations, pip freeze) and G19's
    scene-map check; a violation of either stops the campaign.  P4.6 SUCCESS
    says how each outcome is counted.

REVIEW GATES (agents).  An adversarial pass with the lenses cheat (hardcoding,
oracle, weld, scope), POD faithfulness, comparison validity and feasibility
runs four times, each before the next package starts: (1) on the B2+B3
diffs, before B4; (2) on the B4+B5+B6 diffs (every model tweak including M7,
and the frozen constants with the D11 branch), before B7; (3) on B7's
episodes and diffs, before B8; (4) on B8's campaign report.  This is the only
list.  Reports go to archive/tampura_bridge_plan_2026-10-25/reviews/ with
durable per-agent files.  This continues the user's 2026-09-18 mandate.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
P4.2  GROUND TRUTH IN ONE PAGE (verified 2026-10-25/26 against mirrored source)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
THEIR TASK.  find_dice: a Franka Panda (models/srl/franka_panda/panda.urdf;
tool link panda_tool_tip; wrist camera link camera_frame, 640x480, fx=fy=525,
so a vertical field of view of 2*atan(240/525) = 49.1 deg; their renders use
near 0.02, far 5.0, up = world +z, target along the camera's +z axis,
pb_utils.py:1836-1844, 1895-1908; finger joint upper limit 0.04 m in the URDF
per the round-1 feasibility critique, while OPEN_GRIPPER_POS 0.045 is a code
constant position control cannot reach; B1 re-verifies), a table whose top is
z = 0, one YCB die plus 1 to 5 upside-down YCB cups (a..e_cups_ud) at x in
[0.3, 0.7], y in [-0.4, 0.4].  Every YCB body is created with baseMass = -1
(panda_env_utils.py:676-702), neither static nor a usable dynamic mass.  Their
world NEVER sets gravity (no setGravity in find_dice, setup_robot_pybullet or
the tampura package; PyBullet's default is zero) and never steps physics.
Cups start 1 cm above their resting height (env_generator.py:117); in layouts
1747173591 and 1747173593 the die sits at the hiding cup's centre point, about
4 cm above the table, for the whole TAMPURA episode.  Exactly THREE scene files
exist; get_scene_data picks one with random.choice after the global seed
(env.py:801-811).  The die sits inside a d or e cup in ALL THREE layouts: at
d_cups_ud's point in 1747173591, at e_cups_ud's point in 1747173593, 1.5 cm
from d_cups_ud in 1747173590.  The generator required the die to be absent from
the wrist camera's segmentation at DEFAULT_ARM_POS and each object's centre
point (only) to project into that image (env_generator.py:131-148, 164-180).
Pinned seed-to-scene map of the 2026-05-26 run (each console's "Loading scene
from" line; os.listdir order dependent):
  1747173590 (die + 4 cups) <- seeds 1,2,3,4,8,14,15,18   (TAMPURA 2/8)
  1747173591 (die + 1 cup)  <- seeds 0,7,9,11,12,13,16    (TAMPURA 6/7)
  1747173593 (die + 4 cups) <- seeds 5,6,10,17,19         (TAMPURA 3/5)
(BF#8, 2026-10-27: this is the laptop run's map.  The cluster campaign pins
the cluster's own map, where seeds 1,2,3,4,8,14,15,18 load 1747173593 and
seeds 5,6,10,17,19 load 1747173590: tools/bridge_gates.py SCENE_MAP.)
Actions: pick(o,g) [requires not moved(o)], place(o,g,p), look(o1,o2,q)
["look behind o2 for o1", depends moved(o2)], go-home [only while holding],
no-op.  Streams sampled ONCE at t=0 (5 grasps from a saved per-mesh grasp
library, 5 placements uniform on a shortened table x 0.1 to 0.85 with no
inter-object collision check, 5 look confs 20 to 30 cm above each object's t=0
pose).  Belief: known poses plus a 15 mm visibility voxel grid; voxels within
7.5 mm of ANY known object's concave collision mesh are cleared, so the cavity
of an inverted cup stays a die candidate without any per-class declaration.
Perception hands out the TRUE pose of every body whose id appears in any pixel
of the seg mask (env.py:57-75).  Execution is kinematic: headless trajectories
TELEPORT to the last waypoint, the carried object follows the tool by
resetBasePositionAndOrientation, picks cannot physically fail, nothing falls.
Reward = Exists o: is-target(o) and holding(o), and at-home, evaluated on the
abstract BELIEF over STORE SYMBOLS: abstract() emits holding(grasp_body) and
is-target over the symbols in world.objects (env.py:306-321), and their own
pick writes grasp_body = the object's store symbol (env.py:362, 394); at-home
is np.allclose(conf, DEFAULT_ARM_POS, rtol=1e-4, atol=1e-4); their go-home
reports DEFAULT_ARM_POS even when its motion plan failed (env.py:563-581).
Rollout: exactly max_steps = 20 steps, no early stop, no-op after the goal;
the store and env.state are deep-copied every step and the whole history is
pickled at the end (policy.py:66, 106, 159); loop_s = time_deltas[-1] covers
get_action (200 outcome samples per replan, each an IK, BiRRT or render; SymK
itself 0.04 to 1.5 s per episode) plus env.step.  Local run 2026-05-26: 11/20,
loop 240 +/- 151 s over all episodes, 166 +/- 85 s over successes; every
inspected failure is the same degeneracy (a look that reveals nothing is
re-selected to step 20 under the envelope rule, tampura_policy.py:83-99).  The
policy hook: a Policy subclass with get_action(belief, store) -> (Action(name,
store symbols), info, store); env.step resolves the schema by name on every
call and ProblemSpec.verify accepts a schema with no preconditions or effects
(environment.py:17-22, 62; spec.py:235-260); store.add_typed works after
initialize (store.py:59-63).

OUR MODEL.  Belief = BeliefState status per fragment (unknown / not_here /
found / unresolved) plus one Know-If fluent obj_at_boxel_KIF.  sense(?o
?region) needs handempty, view_clear(region), not KIF, boxel_fits; it
optimistically asserts the target in the region; view_clear is derived from
static blocks_view_at facts and the blockers' current boxels.  Sense takes no
configuration: the camera is fixed (methods.tex:170).  Shadows are 2.5-D
lateral volumes cast from the fixed camera from registered task bodies only,
by rayTestBatch on the default client: none of the three cast sites passes a
client (boxel_env.py:1512-1513; execution.py:2356-2357, 2979-2980), so in our
scenes a ray can stop at a body not yet registered (PAPER_AUDIT item (h));
an object's footprint is never inside a shadow (shadow_calculator.py:217-236);
the robot is excluded from detection (boxel_env.py:1749-1752) and casts
nothing.  Free space is the workspace box minus registered OBJECT and SHADOW
AABBs (free_space.py:47-48, 86-102), and the planner gets KIF for the target
on every OBJECT and free boxel on audit #67's premise that the camera covers
the whole table (pddlstream_planner.py:667-681).  After a place the moved
object's box is re-posed from the render and no new shadow is cast
(test_full_pipeline.py:1910-1975, matching methods.tex:157); new fragments are
cast only when a sense discovers or rediscovers a body (execution.py:2255-2384,
2948-3003; the rediscovery path first deletes the body's old fragments from the
registry and the belief without a render, :2871-2884, and carves the recast
only against OBJECT boxels, :2960-2980, while register_new_detections also
carves against existing fragments, :2278-2279; F9 bounds rediscovery to 3).
The deleted area becomes free space and receives the not-here literal without
an observation (pddlstream_planner.py:675-681): an unsound belief write in
today's pipeline (M7).  After a move the loop names the joint readback after
the target configuration whatever the residual (test_full_pipeline.py:
1591-1597), and RobotConfig equality is by name (streams.py:118-124).  The
goal is ('holding', target) (test_full_pipeline.py:762) and the loop ends as
soon as the target body is held (_loop_done, :1201-1209); init gives Config
and at_config only to current_config (pddlstream_planner.py:889-890); the
planner's home_config is REST_POSES under the symbol q_home (streams.py:
255-258).  The planner reads pddl/domain_pddlstream.pddl unconditionally
(pddlstream_planner.py:177).
Execution is physical since #P1: friction grasp with tactile verification,
motor release, integrity checks; the one exempt hardcoded motion is the
post-pick / post-place lift, 0.10 m in code (execution.py:869).  Execution-time
IK runs on the EXECUTING robot: solve_ik saves the arm, resets it to the seed
and to each candidate, and restores it (robot_utils.py:556-672); pick, place,
stack and the lift call it (execution.py:880, 1058-1091, 1380-1381,
1582-1594).  Our env sets gravity -9.81 right after resetSimulation
(boxel_env.py:510-511) and, in _setup_scene, finger-pad lateral friction 1.2
and fingers opened to 0.04 (boxel_env.py:810-812, 824-828); every scene object
gets lateral friction 1.0 (ObjectSpec default, boxel_env.py:91, applied at
:674-677), so our grasp is validated at pad 1.2 against object 1.0, while
their YCB bodies keep PyBullet's 0.5.  main() runs a 50-step settle and then
env.update_object_positions() before Phase 2 (test_full_pipeline.py:562-564).
Constants tied to our setup: Panda links
9/10/11 hardcoded in five files; external camera at (0.1,-0.8,0.7), near
0.01; table top 0.325; reach gate R 0.815 about a WORLD z of 0.066;
GRASP_MAX_SPAN 0.076 compared with the ESTIMATED box (streams.py:719, 819);
the pick contact floor table_z + 0.035 (execution.py:1034-1037), which lies
ABOVE the top of a die of about 2 cm; the endpoint settle of move_robot_smooth
stops at 2e-3 rad (robot_utils.py:780), twenty times their at-home tolerance;
class priors 0.04 / 0.03 and sense-grid offsets derived from 3 to 4 cm cubes.
execution.py and the robot_utils motion functions call the pybullet module on
the DEFAULT client; their executing world is the first BulletClient they open
(panda_env_utils.py:844-847).  Our pipeline is validated in wsl_env (Python
3.10, numpy 2.2.6); their venv is Python 3.11, numpy 1.24.4, trimesh 4.12.2,
scipy 1.15.3; pybullet is 3.2.7 in both.  Importing tampura pulls in torch,
gymnasium, stable_baselines3 and tensorboard (tampura/__init__.py:1-3,
policies/__init__.py:4, dqn_policy.py:11-16), none of which wsl_env has, and
a pickle written under numpy 2.x does not load under numpy 1.24.

THE GAP.  Their die is hidden by CONTAINMENT; our shadows are lateral and our
objects are solid.  Their camera moves; ours is fixed.  Their goal needs
at-home; ours stops at holding.  Their execution is kinematic and gravity-free;
ours physical.  Their 20 seeds are 20 stochastic trials over three layouts.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
P4.3  DESIGN DECISIONS D1-D12 (each becomes a BF# entry once implemented)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
D1  WHAT RUNS: our WHOLE pipeline (main loop, PDDLStream planner, streams,
    render-based perception, physical execution) inside TAMPURA's executing
    PyBullet world, on their seeds, through their rollout.  Not a planner-only
    port onto their kinematic controllers (that variant is B9, optional, needs
    a user decision, and would be labelled "planners under TAMPURA's
    executor").  The thesis claims concern the whole method.

D2  HARNESS: BoxelBridgePolicy (instantiated directly) returns ONE action per
    episode, a schema named boxel_episode appended at runtime to
    env.problem_spec.action_schemas (no preconditions, identity effects_fn,
    OUR execute_fn).  Their rollout, their env.step, their Belief.update and
    their get_reward run untouched: our execute_fn performs D9's set-up,
    builds the env adapter and calls main(), which settles (D9 step (5)),
    takes the first render, boxelizes and runs the whole plan-act-sense loop
    (bounded by the episode wall cap); execute_fn then returns the SceneState
    it received and the D3 observation; their update
    applies the observation; their reward judges it; steps 1..19 return no-op
    (their no-op branch deep-copies the belief, so a reward reached at step 0
    persists to rewards[-1] without any bridge write).  The info dict holds
    only JSON-able values; our ledger lives in our files.  REJECTED: the
    archived no-op policy that mutated their SceneBelief in place
    (belief_bridge.py:44-58); it bypassed their observation channel and is
    where the forced success lived.

D3  SCORING CHANNEL: tampura_bridge/scoring.py ObservationAdapter builds the
    terminal observation from physical readback only:
    SceneObservation(camera_image=None, poses={}, grasp=G, grasp_body=S,
    conf=Q).  S is the store symbol s in env.sim_world.objects with
    store.get(s) == the body id that both finger pads contact with squeeze
    force >= 0.5 N (the harness's _verify_holding rule) and no constraint on
    that body, resolved with the same lookup their obs_from_camera_image uses
    (env.py:70-73); never chosen by name; None when the pads hold nothing or a
    non-task body.  G is a store symbol of type grasp added once through
    store.add_typed(<plain tuple: position, quaternion, width>, 'grasp') when
    S is not None, so deep copies and the pickle stay plain data.  Q is the
    real readback of the 7 arm joints, never a constant.  camera_image and
    poses stay empty: the reward does not read them, and a render through
    their perception would pull true poses into scoring for no effect.  The
    symbol lookup is the ONLY store read in scoring.  TAMPURA's own go-home
    reports at-home on a failed motion plan; ours cannot, so our arm is scored
    stricter on this point, which is disclosed, not imitated.

D4  CAMERA: a FIXED EXTERNAL camera with exactly the view their own
    pbu.get_image_at_pose computes for camera_frame at DEFAULT_ARM_POS (eye,
    target along the camera's +z, up = world +z, near 0.02, far 5.0, 640x480,
    fx=fy=525), rendered with our TinyRenderer path, declared once in
    task_spec.py and used for every render (G20).  It is the thesis's sensor
    model unchanged (a fixed eye; sense calls no streams; methods.tex:170)
    placed at the viewpoint of their generator's hidden check, so "hidden at
    t=0" means the same thing for both systems.  It is a declared extra sensor
    with respect to their robot, not a ground-truth read.  When the arm is at
    home its hand is in frame exactly as in their check (B1(e) measures what
    it hides; D8 removes that area from the workspace); when the arm is
    elsewhere it can occlude the eye, which the sense classifier treats as a
    blocker.  REJECTED: (a) the physical wrist camera, because every non-sense
    render (census at planning time, pick re-observation, post-release checks,
    integrity checks; execution.py:343, 720, 723, 821, 998, 1799, 1954, 2102;
    test_full_pipeline.py:1936, 2169) would come from an unplanned moving
    viewpoint, and the planning-time census would need a phantom render from
    home or an unplanned move home (CB#120(g)); (b) an external camera at any
    other pose, because it changes what "hidden" means relative to their
    generator.  DISCLOSED in the headline itself (M5): our eye observes after
    every action without moving the arm; theirs observes only on look, from
    20 to 30 cm, and at initialize.

D5  ARM CLEARANCE BEFORE SENSING (model tweak M1).  With the eye where the
    hand rests at home, the arm away from home can block the eye's view of
    the region it just uncovered; three still_blocked senses then park the
    region.  The domain already handles the held object's self-occlusion with
    the handempty precondition (added 2026-08-21 for exactly that reason).
    M1 adds (at_config ?q) (sense_config ?q) to sense for a clearance
    configuration q_clear, a Config object of its own carrying DEFAULT_ARM_POS
    joints, reached by a planned move through move + plan-motion, with TODAY's
    name-on-arrival semantics (the 2e-3 rad settle is ample for a geometric
    clearance); it is NOT the goal symbol q_home and shares none of D7's
    tolerance, hold or bound.  At q_clear the robot occludes exactly what it
    occluded in the t=0 render the regions were cast from, and D8 removes that
    area from the workspace, so every region inside the window is unoccluded
    by the arm at q_clear by construction.  PRE-REGISTERED, SCENE-INDEPENDENT
    RULE (B1(h)): on an empty table, for each declared hollow class at every
    cell of the D8 window, render the class's interior footprint from the D4
    eye with the arm at the post-place lift pose over that cell; if the worst
    blocked fraction exceeds SENSE_MARGINAL_BLOCKED_FRACTION (0.05), M1 is
    adopted, otherwise dropped.  Adoption is the expected outcome, and the
    record says so.  LINEAGE: the discarded bridge gated sensing on
    DEFAULT_ARM_POS through is_at_sense_config(atol=1e-4) and an at-home teleport
    (archive sense_config.py:28-41; boxel_policy.py:54); M1 differs by being a
    planned move with a planned motion, decided on measured geometry, with no
    teleport and no tolerance shared with scoring; the M1 BF# cites this.

D6  CONTAINMENT = INTERIOR REGIONS OF HOLLOW CLASSES (model tweak M2).  A
    category declared hollow in task_spec.py, with cavity dimensions measured
    once in B1(b) from the YCB mesh (inner footprint = AABB minus wall
    thickness; cavity height), casts a SHADOW-typed belief region of kind
    interior over that cavity, created_by the hollow object, wired like a
    lateral fragment (belief.add_shadow, shadow_occluder_map, census, corridor
    facts, sense over its grid), and carved on EVERY registration path
    against all OBJECT boxels except its caster's and all existing SHADOW
    fragments, interiors included (B5's one carve set; today's discovery
    path carves laterals against OBJECT boxels only, P4.2, which M7 aligns).
    The planner, the streams and the sense action are unchanged.  Interior
    ids live in their own list (interior_boxel_ids), exempt from the
    rediscovery cleanup at execution.py:2871-2884; an interior region leaves
    the belief only through a render of its grid (G5).  An interior region is
    a fixed region of space: when its caster is moved, it stays where it was
    and its view-blocking fact releases.
    STATUS RULE (pre-registered; a query on the belief at registration time,
    never on the code path or on the object's history; no per-object flag
    anywhere): whenever a hollow body is registered from a render (initial
    boxelization, discovery, rediscovery, re-registration after loss, the
    post-place re-pose), its cavity footprint is tested against the belief
    as it stood just before that registration.  If every part of it lies in
    free-space boxels inside W (D8) or in regions an earlier sense, sweep or
    retirement render showed empty, the interior region is created not_here
    with provenance "observed before placement", and its G5 row lists those
    boxel and region ids; otherwise it is created unknown.  Tests: a
    footprint that overlaps any SHADOW or OBJECT boxel, or leaves W, gives
    unknown, and the result never depends on which object it is.  PREMISE,
    stated rather than hidden: free space counts as observed because W is
    imaged in full at t=0 by construction (D8) and the world changes only
    through actions that a render follows.  That is exactly the premise
    under which the thesis treats free space as observed space (audit #67)
    and under which a relocated object casts no new lateral shadow
    (methods.tex:157), so lateral and interior regions follow one rule.  Its
    known weakness, a body displaced into free space while the arm hides it
    from the eye, applies to both and is disclosed once (P4.8).
    REJECTED: a new region predicate (redundant with blocks_view_at +
    view_clear); lift-and-look or sense-while-holding (contradicts handempty,
    reopens scripted motion); interiors for every object (changes the thesis
    scenes); a "cups already moved are empty" flag (the archived per-cup
    bookkeeping, boxel_policy.py:352-353).  The generic variant (every object
    hollow) stays behind a task-spec switch and is not used.

D7  GOAL AND AT-HOME: goal ('and', ('holding', die), ('at_config', q_home)).
    Everything below is one B3(d) loop change, default off, under the B3
    acceptance; none of it lives in the bridge (no planner subclass and no
    hand-built init, G10 and G11).  (i) main() builds the goal itself:
    ('holding', T) as today, conjoined with ('at_config', q_home) when a
    goal configuration is declared; there is no goal argument, and G10 pins
    the goal at every plan() call.  (ii) A home-configuration parameter gives
    the planner's
    home_config the joints of task_spec's q_home (their DEFAULT_ARM_POS;
    default REST_POSES, streams.py:255-258), symbol q_home.  (iii) Whenever a
    goal configuration is declared, _build_init emits (Config q_home) (Boxel
    home) (config_for_boxel q_home home), so move (which needs (Config ?q2)
    and config_for_boxel, domain_pddlstream.pddl:207-216) can reach q_home
    from any current configuration through plan-motion with the die as
    cargo; the home pseudo-boxel is excluded from every init enumeration by a
    type check, asserted (no is_free_space, is_shadow or boxel_fits fact
    names it).  (iv) Moves made while holding are motion-planned with the
    held body as cargo.  Today they are not: the plan's post-pick
    configuration comes from compute-kin and carries only ignored_body_ids
    and grasp_ee_offset (streams.py:1434-1435), q_home ignores nothing, so
    plan-motion's held set is empty (:934-937); the runtime re-solve copies
    the plan's q1 metadata and runs only when the start pose drifts by more
    than 1e-2 rad (test_full_pipeline.py:1540-1547); the readback
    configuration carries joints and name only (:1594).  The change: every
    readback configuration made while holding carries the held body's
    held_body_ids, ignored_body_ids and grasp_ee_offset, and the move
    handler re-solves every move made while holding from the current
    configuration with the dispatcher's held body as cargo, whatever the
    drift, toward the planned goal configuration (logged as G3's runtime
    re-solve).  compute-kin configurations are NOT changed (that would make
    the approach to the pick treat the die as already held).  B3 tests: a
    post-pick move to q_home is re-solved with the die as cargo, and a
    corrective move to q_home is planned while holding (an injected at-home
    miss).  (v) The at-home evaluator applies THEIR exact test (np.allclose
    on the 7-joint readback, rtol = atol = 1e-4) and ONLY to a move whose
    target is q_home; every other move keeps today's name-on-arrival rule.
    When the test fails, current_config becomes a fresh Config symbol
    q_readback_k carrying the readback joints (never named q_home), so the
    planner's init is honest and the planner itself plans the corrective
    move.  (vi) For a declared goal configuration the loop ends on the goal
    test itself, the target held AND the last move to q_home passing the
    at-home test, not on _loop_done's holding-only test.  The only new exit
    is home_not_reached, after two consecutive PLANNED moves to q_home that
    leave the test false; the existing exits are unchanged.  (vii) A move to
    q_home ends with a FINAL HOLD (an executor mode, default off): position
    control toward q_home with move_robot_smooth's gains and forces, stepped
    until their allclose test on the 7-joint readback passes or
    HOME_HOLD_STEP_CAP steps have run (today's endpoint hold stops at 2e-3
    rad or 120 steps, robot_utils.py:775-788).  HOME_HOLD_STEP_CAP is a
    task_spec constant (D8), set once in B6 as twice the largest step count
    B1(i) needs to pass the test over its whole probe grid, empty-handed and
    with the die-class payload, and frozen before B7; the executor reads it,
    so B3 carries no number.  If B1(i) finds the test unreachable within its
    probe cap, the headline test stays strict, the record says the at-home
    conjunct fails by construction, and the user decides before B7 (Q12); no
    tolerance is relaxed.  The A/B on our scenes shows only that the change
    is inert when no goal configuration is declared.  The headline uses their
    strict test;
    secondary columns report "holding but not at home" and "at home within
    2e-3 rad", computed by the campaign report from the logged readback,
    never written into their belief.  B7 measures; it does not choose a
    rule.

D8  TARGET-CLASS, ROBOT AND WORKSPACE CONSTANTS ARE TASK-SPEC INPUTS, SET ONCE
    (B6), from G9's allowed sources only: NOMINAL_HIDDEN_EXTENTS and _MIN from
    the die mesh; SENSE_LOW_SLICE_OFFSET below the die's half height minus
    SENSE_RENDER_DEPTH_TOL; SENSE_DENSE_SPACING below half the die footprint;
    octree auto_cell from the largest declared class; the pick contact floor
    min_contact_z = table_z + (pad bottom offset below panda_grasptarget, from
    their finger mesh) + a declared clearance, with 0.035 kept as the
    thesis-scene default; the pre-grasp aperture cap and the grip width gate
    checked against the die class; GRASP_MAX_SPAN from the URDF finger limit
    (0.04 per finger gives 0.076 with today's clearance); the reach envelope
    from B1(f); HOME_HOLD_STEP_CAP from B1(i) by D7 (vii)'s rule.  WORKSPACE
    WINDOW W for free space, placement candidates and ShadowCalculator: their
    shortened-table region (a constant in their place sampler), intersected
    with the reach envelope, keeping only cells whose column up to the
    tallest declared class height projects entirely into the D4 frame (3-D
    corners, not the table-plane footprint) and is free of robot pixels in
    the empty-table home render (B1(e)).  REPRESENTATION: W is the largest
    axis-aligned rectangle inside that cell set (a maximal-rectangle search
    on the cell grid, stated in B6's BF#), passed as the existing table
    x and y ranges that free space, the shadow caster and placement already
    take (free_space.py:27-29, 47-48; boxel_env.py:548-567); no new mask
    feature.  Lateral shadows are clipped to W as today's are clipped to the
    table ranges; interior regions are bounded by their cavity, not by W.
    W restricts OUR
    placements to space the eye images; TAMPURA's placements may land out of
    view; disclosed.  BF# restates audit #67's premise (observed_clear_regions
    None means "the camera images the whole workspace") as valid inside W
    only.  Our scenes keep every default.

D9  PHYSICS SET-UP IN THEIR WORLD, PRE-REGISTERED AND COMPLETE.  Inside
    execute_fn, before main() and before the first physics step, in this
    order: (1) setGravity(0, 0, -9.81) on the executing client (our value,
    boxel_env.py:511; their world has none); (2) setTimeStep 1/240 (our
    SIM_TIMESTEP, robot_utils.py); (3) for every task body, mass AND
    localInertiaDiagonal from declared class masses (cited to the YCB
    dataset) AND lateralFriction 1.0 (the ObjectSpec default every object in
    our scenes carries, boxel_env.py:91, 674-677) through one changeDynamics
    call, unconditionally (their -1 is not a usable mass; their 0.5 friction
    is PyBullet's default, never set by them); (4) our gripper set-up applied
    to the robot we drive: finger-pad lateral friction 1.2 and fingers opened
    to 0.04 (boxel_env.py:810-812, 824-828), so the pad contact runs at the
    pad 1.2 against object 1.0 pairing our grasp is validated with (the
    archived 4.0 is forbidden).  Then main() performs (5) its own Phase-1
    settle of 50 steps (test_full_pipeline.py:562), which IS this step:
    execute_fn adds no settle of its own, and the counted no-op
    update_object_positions follows (G16); and (6) Phase 2's first render
    (:610).  Between (5) and (6), B3(c)'s after_settle hook runs the
    bridge's read-only check: assert the die is absent from the D4 eye's
    segmentation, and log the settle displacement of every task body, the
    die included (max XY and yaw drift, JSON pose to settled pose;
    pre-registered disclosure threshold 1 cm).  The hook is in G2's
    write-only set and runs under G3 and G14 with no allowance (it moves and
    steps nothing).  If the die is visible, the episode is flagged
    scene_changed_by_settle and reported in its own row of the per-seed
    table, never re-seated or re-rolled.  Each set-up item is a G1/G14
    allowlist entry with one BF#; nothing else of theirs is altered;
    campaign.json records gravity, timestep, frictions and masses.  The
    B1(c) probe uses this identical call sequence (steps (1) to (4), then
    exactly 50 steps).  TAMPURA's arm runs as shipped: no gravity, cups 1 cm
    above the table, and in layouts 1747173591 and 1747173593 (15 of 20
    seeds on the campaign map of BF#8; 12 on the laptop map) the die inside
    its cup at about 4 cm, picked from mid-air; the paper states this.  On
    our arm, in the same 15 seeds, D9's gravity drops the die inside its cup
    during main()'s settle: in 1747173591 by 37.7 mm, 3.9 mm sideways, tilted
    21.2 deg and still moving at 0.069 m/s when the settle ends; in
    1747173593 by 39.8 mm, 10.3 mm sideways, tilted 49.8 deg (BF#4 (c),
    BF#12).  The paper states this too.

D10 BUDGETS: one wall cap of 900 s per episode on both arms, enforced by
    tools/_bridge_campaign.py (the same value as tampura_sweep.py's kill in
    the 2026-05-26 baseline and in B1(g)); our --max-plan-time bounded by the
    remaining episode budget; no
    action cap on our arm (their 20 steps are their step semantics; our
    actions are finer grained); a killed or crashed episode enters every
    time statistic at the cap value, flagged censored, on both arms.
    STATE AT GATE 1 (BF#14): the harness passes a constant --max-plan-time of
    900 s and nothing yet owns the episode cap, so today the 900 s process
    kill is the only bound.  Before B7, main() gains an episode-deadline
    keyword (default None, today's behaviour; BF#13's test) that clips each
    plan() call to the remaining budget, and the cap-owning driver exists;
    the external kill stays as the backstop for non-planning stalls.

D11 CUP GRASPABILITY, pre-registered and scene-independent: for EVERY cup
    class in the scenes' category table (a..e), B1(b) measures the collision-
    mesh AABB width and the inverted top-face width, and B1(e) measures our
    detector's ESTIMATED pinch extent for that class on an empty table at
    the eye's viewing geometry (the sampler gates on the estimate,
    streams.py:819).  (i) If every class's largest estimated width fits
    GRASP_MAX_SPAN, the sampler runs unchanged.  (ii) If not, the top-face
    pinch refinement M6 (pinch extent from the top slice of the object's
    depth cloud, a general perception feature) is adopted BEFORE B7 with its
    own BF# and an A/B on our scenes.  (iii) If the top face does not fit
    either, the campaign is not runnable as specified (0/20 by construction
    would measure the gripper, not the method) and the user decides;
    TAMPURA's grasp library stays #P5.  MARGIN (pre-registered by BF#14,
    before B6 runs): B6 decides on the 5 cm cells of W at yaws 0, 30, 60
    and 90 deg, still on the empty table, and branch (i) holds only if every
    class's largest estimate stays at least 2 mm below GRASP_MAX_SPAN (about
    the 2.6 mm spread B1(e) measured over 9 cells); otherwise branch (ii).
    New exit reason no_grasp_in_span (the
    stream offered no grasp for a hollow object the plan needed) keeps such
    failures attributable.

D12 INTERPRETER FOR ARM O: its OWN venv, never their venv and never one that
    arm T uses.  Arm O imports tampura (Policy, TampuraEnv, structs), and
    that import pulls in torch, gymnasium, stable_baselines3 and tensorboard
    (P4.2).  Candidate (A), preferred: a clone of their venv (Python 3.11,
    numpy 1.24.4, their exact packages) plus our pinned dependencies, so the
    RolloutHistory pickle loads in their venv (G6).  Candidate (B): wsl_env
    plus their full import set at their versions, trimesh, scipy and imageio
    pinned to theirs and pyyaml added; under (B) a numpy 2.x pickle cannot
    load in their venv, so G6's pickle check runs in arm O's venv and the
    deviation is disclosed.  B1(j) runs five tests per candidate, in order:
    'import tampura' and their packages' full import set; all three layouts
    built under the candidate and under their venv with identical per-body
    AABBs and identical t=0 segmentation id sets (create_ycb offsets
    geometry by trimesh's centre of mass, panda_env_utils.py:688-691; their
    code has NumPy-2-sensitive calls such as pb_utils.py:1781); a G6 pickle
    round trip into their venv; a smoke run of our holding scene; and the
    c31cbdd A/A of B3's acceptance.  (A) is used if it passes all five;
    otherwise (B) if it passes all but the pickle round trip; otherwise the
    user decides.  campaign.json records the choice and a pip-freeze hash of
    both venvs; G13 asserts arm T's freeze unchanged before and after every
    run.  DECIDED 2026-10-27 (BF#7): candidate (A), armO-venvA, passed all
    five tests.

        constructor's failure path (today close() disconnects both,
        boxel_env.py:541-545, 1898-1907, and main() always ends with
        env.close(), test_full_pipeline.py:2362), and a B3 test asserts the
        adopted client is still connected after main() returns.  The caller
        is responsible for D9's set-up, which this path does not perform.
    (c) main() gains keyword arguments with byte-identical defaults: env
        (injected; default builds BoxelTestEnv as today), target_name
        (default None: today's Phase-4 choice, including its random.choice,
        so the global RNG stream of our scenes is unchanged; G10 pins its
        category), oracle_phases (default True; False skips the :576 check
        and Phase 4), domain_file (default today's
        pddl/domain_pddlstream.pddl; the only way M1's generated domain
        reaches the planner), after_settle (default None; a read-only
        callable run once between the Phase-1 settle and Phase 2's first
        render, D9), stop_before_planning (default False; returns right
        after the first plan-twin sync, used only by B2's deliverable and
        asserted False in B7 and B8) and gui_hold_s (default 4.0, today's
        window hold at :2353-2358; the bridge passes 0).  There is no goal
        argument (D7 (i)).  BoxelTestEnv gains a shadow-raycast client
        (default None: today's default-client rays, so PAPER_AUDIT item (h)
        is unchanged on our scenes) passed at all three cast sites; when it
        is set, the plan twin is synced to the OBJECT boxes known at that
        moment, with every boxel-less body parked, BEFORE each cast,
        including the initial generate_boxels, which today runs before the
        first sync_to_plan_client (test_full_pipeline.py:610, 1321).  The
        bridge builds the args namespace itself; no settings-object refactor.
    (d) Loop and executor parameters with today's behaviour as default: the
        pick contact floor (D8), the reach constants, and D7 (i) to (vii) as
        one list: the goal configuration, its home joints and the goal main()
        builds from it; the init facts (Config q_home) (Boxel home)
        (config_for_boxel q_home home); the held body's metadata on readback
        configurations and the cargo re-solve of every move made while
        holding; the at-home evaluator; the q_readback_k renaming; loop
        termination by the goal test; the home_not_reached exit; the final
        hold reading HOME_HOLD_STEP_CAP from task_spec (all default off).
    (e) physicsClientId threading only if B1(d) demands it.
    (f) The arm-O venv per D12.
    Class: refactor.  ACCEPTANCE (pre-registered, under P4.6's MACHINE
    rule): first an A/A run of c31cbdd against itself in the arm-O venv on
    our holding scene, seeds 0-19 and 100-119, gives the noise flip count;
    then the A/B of B3 against c31cbdd on the same seeds.  Phase-4 target
    choices must be identical (they are decided before any wall-clock
    search).  Outcome flips are accepted up to the A/A count; each flipped
    seed gets ONE pre-registered re-run on both arms, and a flip that
    reproduces fails.  Plan-count differences follow the same rule;
    timed-out plan() calls are logged as the noise source.  Tests: D7 (iv)'s
    two tests (the post-pick move re-solved with the die as cargo, and a
    corrective move to q_home planned while holding after an injected
    at-home miss); the after_settle hook neither steps nor moves anything;
    with the shadow-raycast client set, no ray reaches the executing client;
    the adopted client survives main(); stop_before_planning returns before
    the first plan() call.  B5, M6 and M7 reuse this acceptance, with its
    plan-count clause replaced by BF#13's pre-registered distributional
    test (three runs per arm).  Lands on tampura-bridge first,
    cherry-picked to main-paper-icaps-2027 once green.
    Effort 5 to 6 days.

B4  [IMPLEMENTED AS M8 (samplers plan Q-E, BF#33), ACCEPTANCE OPEN]  MODEL
    TWEAK M1, NOW ONE CANDIDATE SET OF M8.  M1's single declared q_clear is
    the general sense-configuration set of M8 C1 (the current

    the domain reaches the planner only through main(domain_file) and
    policy.py's GateConfig, and G10 regenerates and compares it
    (bridge_gates.py domain, and G10r on the run's domain row).
    EXECUTOR CHANGE, kept from M1: handle_sense_action unpacks the first two
    parameters of the three-parameter sense; the ledger logs the third as
    cfg:<name>; G5 reads the region at index 1 (tested).  Our scenes adopt
    it with M8 (Q-F, BF#34), not with a declared clearance configuration.
    Deliverable (unchanged in substance): with M8 on, every sense the
    planner emits is preceded by the arm being at a configuration in its

B5  [OPEN]  MODEL TWEAK M2, INTERIOR REGIONS (D6), AND M7 UNLESS THE USER
    REJECTS IT (Q10).  BoxelData gains region_kind in {lateral, interior};
    boxel_env.generate_boxels and the registration paths in execution.py
    (register_new_detections, the sense discovery and rediscovery path,

    cavity, with the D6 status rule; interior_boxel_ids is a separate list
    exempt from the rediscovery cleanup.  ONE CARVE SET on every
    registration path for every new interior fragment: all OBJECT boxels
    except the caster's plus all existing SHADOW fragments, interiors
    included.  M7 (P4.5) gives lateral fragments the same carve set on the
    rediscovery path and makes the rediscovery cleanup keep every superseded
    lateral fragment unless its grid renders empty, as retire_lost_objects
    does (execution.py:2105-2118); it is a switch, default off on our scenes,
    on in the bridge.  Without M7 the lateral overlap volume and the
    unrendered drops are logged per episode and disclosed (G5, correction
    (6)).  The post-place carve casts none and logs the destination's cavity
    footprint.  Class: model tweak.  Deliverable: (1) a synthetic test on
    OUR table (a hollow cup URDF over a cube, cup class declared hollow):
    the planner relocates the cup to a cell outside the corridor and the
    cube is found, either by the planned sense of the interior region or by
    the post-place integrity render (logged as discovery); the interior
    region's status changes only through a render of its grid or the logged
    "observed before placement" record (G5); (2) on find_dice seeds 0 and 1,
    the first render yields an interior region for every registered cup,
    with no planning; (3) a synthetic rediscovery on OUR table with M7 on
    keeps an occluded superseded fragment and removes an observed-empty one;
    (4) no change on scenes without a hollow class and with M7 off (B3
    acceptance).  Abort: any path that removes an interior region from the
    belief or marks it not_here without a render of its grid or D6's logged
    belief query; any "already moved" flag.  Effort 3 to 5 days, M7
    included.

CALENDAR (pre-registered).  Efforts: B0 3, B1 2-3, B2 5-7, B3 5-6, B4 1-2, B5
3-5 (M7 included), B6 1-2, B7 3, B8 4: 27 to 35 agent-days, plus 2 if M6 is
needed (up to 37).  The four review gates add about 4 days: 31 to 39 days of
serial work (up to 41 with M6), against the 38 calendar days from 2026-10-26
through 2026-12-02, counted inclusive and with every day a working day.  The
upper end overruns that window by 1 day, by 3 with M6.  Milestones, assuming
the upper half of each range: B0 + B1 part 1 by 2026-10-30; B3 + B1 part 2
by 2026-11-06; B2 + review gate 1 by 2026-11-14; B4 to B6 + review gate 2 by
2026-11-24; B7 + review gate 3 by 2026-11-28; B8 + review gate 4 by
2026-12-03.  The schedule has no slack and slipping is the expected case.
LATEST B8 START: ten days before the paper's submission deadline (the exact
December date is to be filled in from the call for papers, Q8).  CUT ORDER if
time runs short, touching nothing in the gates, the protocol or the
disclosures: (1) B9; (2) the optional view-blocking placement count (P4.5
ledger); (3) CB#125's docstring fix.  OUTCOMES.  If B7 meets its GO criterion,
B8 runs and is reported whatever it shows, also after a milestone slip if it
still starts before the latest B8 start.  If B7 cannot meet it in time, the
