names the blocking BF#, reports B7's per-episode outcomes, and keeps the
indirect comparison only under the corrected wording of P4.5, not as evidence
of parity.  B9 is not a fallback: it tests the planner alone, a different
claim, and needs a user decision.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
P4.5  THE MODEL TWEAKS, THE CLAIM LEDGER AND THE WORDING
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
M1  ARM CLEARANCE BEFORE SENSING (conditional, D5).
    BEFORE: (:action sense :parameters (?o ?region) :precondition (and (Obj
    ?o) (Boxel ?region) (handempty) (view_clear ?region) (not
    (obj_at_boxel_KIF ?o ?region)) (boxel_fits ?o ?region)) ...).
    AFTER (exact delta that tools/gen_bridge_domain.py applies and G10
    checks): predicates gain (sense_config ?q); sense's parameters become
    (?o ?region ?q) and its precondition adds (Config ?q) (at_config ?q)
    (sense_config ?q); effects unchanged.  sense_config is a static init fact
    true only of q_clear.  The executor accepts the extra parameter and
    ignores it for the observation (B4).
    POD JUSTIFICATION: the precondition Pre(Y) of the observation states that
    the robot occludes exactly what it occluded in the t=0 render from which
    the regions were cast (and W excludes that area), as handempty already
    states for the held object; the observation itself is unchanged
    (deterministic render of the region's grid from the fixed eye).
    CLAIM EFFECT: methods.tex:170's "sense calls no streams because the camera
    is fixed" stays true; its sentence that view_clear is the only geometric
    precondition and the sense listing at methods.tex:141-148 are already
    stale (handempty and boxel_fits, domain_pddlstream.pddl:174, 183) and
    become more so; the paper adds that in the find_dice runs sensing
    requires the arm at a declared clearance pose, measured necessary for this
    eye placement (BF#11 (h)), at the cost of a planned move per sense.

M2  INTERIOR REGIONS OF HOLLOW CLASSES (D6).
    BEFORE: hidden regions are lateral occlusion shadows only; an OBJECT boxel
    is solid (audit #67), so its footprint is removed from hidden space.
    AFTER: for a category declared hollow, the solidity assumption is relaxed:
    its measured cavity rejoins the hidden space as one region of kind
    interior, cast by the object at registration with the D6 status rule,
    cleared only by relocating the object and observing the region's grid.
    Categories not declared hollow cast nothing (all thesis scenes).
    PARTITION STATEMENT: for a hollow class the OBJECT boxel denotes the shell
    (the box minus its interior region) for InBoxel, and K(not InBoxel(target,
    shell)) rests on the observed outer surface and the solid walls; the
    interior region is carved with B5's one carve set on every registration
    path, so audit #68's at-most-one-SHADOW-per-point invariant holds for
    interiors; for lateral fragments on the rediscovery path it holds only
    with M7 (P4.2); the Boxel set is a partition of the workspace except that
    an OBJECT box and its interior region share volume.  methods.tex:84 and
    :97 need this qualification in the paper.
    POD JUSTIFICATION: a belief region with a Know-If literal, cleared only by
    an observation of its grid; discovery by relocation plus sensing; the
    thesis's hiddenness definition (methods.tex:138, every corner's line of
    sight blocked) covers it.  M2 is a single-level, declared-cavity region;
    the recursive partitioning of concave geometry that conclusion.tex:41-45
    describes stays future work.
    CLAIM EFFECT: results.tex:352 (TAMPURA keeps occlusion sub-symbolic, we
    name it) is unchanged, with the qualification that in find_dice the
    interior region exists because of a declared class property, not because
    the camera cast it; its view-blocking and clearance are camera-derived as
    for lateral shadows.
    PARITY SENTENCE (paper): "TAMPURA derives hollowness from the category's
    concave mesh for every known object; we declare it per class with cavity
    dimensions measured from the same mesh, because our perception yields
    boxes, not meshes; both take the category label from the scene."

M3  GOAL CONJUNCT at_config(q_home) AND D7's LOOP (D7, switched on by the
    bridge; BF#14 reclassifies D7-on from refactor to this model tweak): a
    goal form plus the home pseudo-boxel init facts, not a domain change; the
    goal formula is the same for both systems and the evaluation is stricter
    on ours (D3, D7).
    BEFORE: goal (holding T); q starts as the home configuration by
    assumption (methods.tex:198); after each action q is named after the
    planned target whatever the residual (methods.tex:211); the loop exits of
    methods.tex:188; the Boxels partition the workspace with one Know-If
    fluent per object and Boxel (methods.tex:84).
    AFTER: the goal gains at_config(q_home); the home pseudo-boxel (no
    volume, no Know-If literal, excluded from every init enumeration,
    asserted); the initial readback judged by TAMPURA's test and named
    q_readback_0 when it fails; after a move to q_home the readback is named
    by that test, otherwise by a fresh q_readback_k; the new exit
    home_not_reached; the final hold; the cargo re-solve of every move made
    while holding (plans still certify such moves with the cargo at the
    approach offset or none, the re-solve applying the pick's offset, BF#14
    RG1.25).
    CLAIM EFFECT: the paper says that in the find_dice runs lines 198 and 211
    of Algorithm 1 (and the loop condition at 199, through the goal), the
    exit list of methods.tex:188 and the partition sentence of methods.tex:84
    carry these qualifications; none on the thesis scenes, where D7 is off.

M4  SCORING (D2, D3): success for both systems = TAMPURA's reward on
    TAMPURA's belief through TAMPURA's observation channel; our belief inputs
    come from physical readback through the store-symbol lookup; theirs from
    their kinematic executor.  The paper states both.  SUPERSEDED
    2026-10-29 by BF#35: one success criterion for both arms (Q17).

M5  HEADLINE SENTENCES.  BEFORE: abstract.tex:18 (comparable end-to-end cost
    about 144 s vs 166 s and reliability 65.6 % vs the >= 63 % implied, "on
    the closest analogous hidden-object task"); results.tex:93 (the end-to-end
    time definition) and :108 (TAMPURA as an external reference with the
    per-step 57 s); results.tex:295 ("marginally easier goal", "the two run on
    different environments, so it benchmarks neither system"); results.tex:300
    (figure caption numbers); results.tex:304-312 ("rough parity end-to-end",
    "comparable in reliability", "a marginally easier goal"); results.tex:
    336-345 ("different environments ... benchmark neither system", "the
    closest analogous task"); results.tex:354 ("sufficient ... at comparable
    end-to-end cost and reliability, and simpler to model"); conclusion.tex:10
    ("comparable end-to-end cost and reliability").
    AFTER (template, blanks filled only from campaign.json): "On TAMPURA's
    Find Die, seed for seed over its 20 released episodes and three layouts,
    our pipeline (a fixed camera at TAMPURA's home wrist-camera pose,
    observing after every action) reached the goal in X/20 [Wilson CI]
    against Y/20 [CI] for TAMPURA (wrist camera, observing on look) (paired
    McNemar p = ...), at a median loop time of ... vs ..."  results.tex:93 and
    :108 are rewritten to define the same-machine loop time and TAMPURA as a
    measured arm.
    RULE: the parity adjectives are deleted now in every listed sentence and
    come back only if the intervals support them; a worse result is written as
    such; "simpler to model" is dropped or qualified by the task-knowledge
    disclosure (ours: hollow classes with cavity sizes, die and cup class
    sizes and masses, target class, the eye pose, W and, if M1, a clearance
    pose; theirs: category meshes, saved grasp library, look-conf sampler,
    target class).

M6  TOP-FACE PINCH WIDTH (conditional, D11 branch (ii)): BEFORE the pinch
    extent is the estimated AABB width; AFTER it is the extent of the top
    slice of the object's depth cloud.  A general perception feature with the
    B3 acceptance on our scenes; CLAIM EFFECT: none on the thesis scenes
    (cubes have equal top and AABB widths), recorded either way.

M7  REDISCOVERY KEEPS UNOBSERVED FRAGMENTS (B5; a switch, default ON on our
    scenes and in the bridge by the user's Q10 answer, BF#15).
    BEFORE: when a sense rediscovers a registered body, its old fragments are
    removed from the registry and the belief without a render
    (execution.py:2871-2884); the area becomes free space and the planner
    receives the not-here literal for it without an observation
    (pddlstream_planner.py:675-681); the recast is carved against OBJECT
    boxels only (:2960-2980).
    AFTER: a superseded fragment is tested with the sense slices against the
    rediscovery render; an observed-empty one is marked not_here and
    removed, and one that is still occluded is kept as an unknown region
    without a caster and left to the sense action, exactly as
    retire_lost_objects treats a lost caster's fragments (execution.py:
    2105-2118); the recast carves against all OBJECT boxels except the
    caster's and all existing fragments, as register_new_detections does
    (:2278-2279).
    POD JUSTIFICATION: a K-literal changes only through an observation of its
    region; M7 removes a belief write that had none.
    CLAIM EFFECT: no thesis sentence describes the rediscovery cleanup; the
    observed-space premise of free space (audit #67) is restored on this
    path.  Whether the main pipeline adopts M7 for the thesis scenes is a
    separate BF# with its own A/B; until then the thesis numbers stand as
    produced and correction (6) applies to them.

M8  POSE-GATED PICK, VIEW-CLEAR SENSE, WRIST-CAMERA OBSERVE (samplers plan
    A-C; switch --m8, default ON on our scenes and in the bridge by Q-F,
    BF#34; BF#29, BF#31, BF#33).
    BEFORE: pick needs no pose knowledge; a registered object's pose is
    trusted however it was seen; sense has no configuration and runs where
    the arm happens to stand (BF#26: the arm hid the region); the robot
    observes only with the fixed eye.
    AFTER: every pose writer sets pose_radius (formula from a complete
    detection, PLACE_POSE_TOL for a commanded pose, inf for an inferred
    or disturbed one); pick requires (obj_pose_known o), emitted iff
    pose_radius <= the believed commit margin; place, place_hiding and
    stack give it; sense takes a configuration from its region's
    sense_view set; a new observe(o, q) renders the wrist camera (their
    camera_frame mount, D4 intrinsics) at a look configuration and
    re-poses registered objects only.  The domain is generated (B4).
    Calibrations on our scenes and a die-class body, never a TAMPURA
    layout: YAW_NOISE eye 9.475 deg, wrist 0.202 deg; PLACE_POSE_TOL
    3.609 mm (95th percentiles, BF#29).
    POD JUSTIFICATION: a pick commits on a pose only within what the
    gripper tolerates; when the belief cannot guarantee it, the planner
    must plan the observation that can (a K-literal set by observation).
    CLAIM EFFECT: changes the system under test on both pipelines; thesis
    numbers before it stand as produced.  The comparison with TAMPURA is
    not widened: their find_dice look action already images the wrist
    camera away from home (BF#28 (g)).  Accepted only by BF#13's test.
M9  OBJECT-FITTED GRASP (samplers plan D; switch --m9, default ON by Q-F;
    BF#30, Q-H (a) in BF#33).
    BEFORE: grasp candidates and the pinch from the registry AABB at yaw 0
    or pi/2.
    AFTER: a minimum-area rectangle of the top face gives yaw and
    extents; grasps at the object's yaw, aim at the fused rectangle's
    centre, the pinch sized on the wider of the rectangle and the AABB
    along the pads (Q-H (a)).  Without a valid yaw, the AABB sampler.
    POD JUSTIFICATION: the grasp is sampled from the believed geometry.
    CLAIM EFFECT: as M8.
M10 A TOUCHING FAILURE MAKES THE POSE UNKNOWN (samplers plan E; switch
    --m10, default ON by Q-F; BF#31).
    BEFORE: a failed pick retries in the action at the same belief.
    AFTER: pad contacts are polled through the align and descent; a
    failed pick that touched the target skips the in-action retry, and
    every touched registered body gets pose_radius inf (with M8 the next
    pick needs an observe).
    POD JUSTIFICATION: a contact is evidence the belief is stale.
    CLAIM EFFECT: as M8.

CLAIM LEDGER.
  MEASURED by B8 (only if B7 meets its GO criterion): results.tex:93, :295,
  :300, :304-312 per-episode time and reliability (replaced by same-task,
  same-machine, same-seed numbers with intervals); "marginally easier goal"
  (deleted: same goal formula, evaluation stricter on our side, disclosed);
  :354 "sufficient to reach the goal" (measured on find_dice only: one task,
  three layouts, 20 seeds, worded with that scope); :336-345 and :389-404
  "different environments", "not the same problem", "a shared scene file
  would strengthen" (resolved; the hardware sentence names the campaign CPU).
  STAYS ARCHITECTURAL: :352 symbolic vs sub-symbolic occlusion and view-blind
  placement (unmeasured unless a view-blocking placement count is added to
  both arms from logs; optional, first in the cut order after B9); :295 and
  :108 per-step 11 s vs 57 s (dropped: step semantics differ; their per-step
  time includes 200 effects_fn samples); :354 "simpler to model"
  (qualitative, see M5); related-work statements on LAO* and the learned MDP
  (source-verified, unchanged); all semantic-vs-uniform partition claims
  (ours only, untouched).  M1, M2, M3, M6 and M7 are used in find_dice only and
  the sentence that introduces them says so (conclusion.tex:27-33, 41-45 name
  sensing configurations and concave interiors as future work).

CORRECTIONS THE PAPER NEEDS REGARDLESS.
  (1) The 3-strike give-up.  methods.tex:168 ("marked empty without ever
  being directly observed ... it is unsound") and results.tex:464-468 ("marks
  it as not containing the target", the run report distinguishes the two) are
  accurate: the code parks the fragment (BeliefState unresolved,
  execution.py:3061-3079) and withholds it from the planner with the same
  Know-If literal as an observed-empty fragment (test_full_pipeline.py:
  1217-1218; pddlstream_planner.py:638-640).  Two sentences need correcting:
  results.tex:469-470 ("the evaluation counts the give-up case as a
  failure") becomes "an episode that ends with only given-up regions left and
  no target is a failure; a give-up followed by finding the target elsewhere
  is a success" (test_full_pipeline.py:1260-1271), and methods.tex:168's
  "re-grounding the view-blocking facts is left to future work" is reconciled
  with the F2 blocks_view_at re-keying (test_full_pipeline.py:1053-1055).
  (2) The post-action lift is 0.10 m.  (3) SymK is a Fast Downward top-k
  fork, so "the same Fast Downward-based planner" means the FD lineage.
  (4) Our domain uses derived predicates, so "no deductive axioms" must read
  "no LW1-style knowledge axioms over K-literals".  (5) The sense listing at
  methods.tex:141-148 omits handempty and boxel_fits.  (6) Today's
  rediscovery cleanup removes a body's old fragments without a render and
  hands the area to the planner as free space with the not-here literal
  (execution.py:2871-2884; pddlstream_planner.py:675-681).  The paper states
  this for the thesis numbers, with the number of episodes in which it fired
  from the F9 rediscovery counter (test_full_pipeline.py:1087-1097) where
  the logs carry it, until the main pipeline adopts M7.  This finding is new:
  the #P1 F20 review follow-up (fixes in ba924bd) made the cleanup remove the
  fragments from the belief as well, but no observation was added; P4.13
  (round 3, R3.4) is its audit record.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
P4.6  EVAL PROTOCOL (pre-registered; any later change is itself a BF# entry)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
ARMS.  T = TAMPURA (tampura cdddf14, tampura_environments 4167c35, find_dice.yml
as snapshotted in the 2026-05-26 JSON, their venv), run through
tools/run_tampura_timed.py with tampura_policy_timed, a subclass in OUR code
that overrides get_action only (super().get_action inside perf_counter, the
span written to our side log) and inherits rollout (G13).  The step plus
update share of step k is time_deltas[k] - time_deltas[k-1] minus the
get_action span of step k.  B1(g)'s as-shipped block is arm T's reproduction
reference; pre-registered agreement: the timed block's per-seed outcomes may
differ from B1(g) only on seeds that also flipped between the 2026-05-26 block
and B1(g); otherwise both blocks are reported and neither is chosen.  O = our
pipeline through the bridge (B2-B6) at one recorded commit, run from an
immutable snapshot directory (the CB#113 pattern), in the D12 venv.
PYTHONHASHSEED is pinned to the same value for B1(g), both T repetitions and
O.
SEEDS AND SCENES.  --global-seed 0..19 on both arms; the loaded scene file is
logged per episode and must equal the pinned map; results are reported per
layout and pooled; the paper says there are three layouts.  No seed is added,
dropped, re-rolled or re-run outside the repetitions and the stalled-episode
rule below.
SUCCESS.  rewards[-1] == 1.0 from each arm's RolloutHistory (G12).  Secondary
columns, never substituted: O holding but not at home; O at home within 2e-3
rad (from the logged readback); O _verify_holding; optionally T successes
whose executing arm is not at DEFAULT_ARM_POS (read-only, after the episode).
Per-seed rows also carry scene_changed_by_settle (B1(e) found every cup
fully in frame, so the planned in-frame column is dropped; provisional until
the corrected-inertia re-measure of BF#12 (4)).  CRASHED EPISODES
(pre-registered by BF#14, before any campaign run): an arm O episode whose
bridge_episode.json carries 'error' is a crashed episode, exactly like an
arm T process that dies without a RolloutHistory: it fails, enters every
time statistic censored at the cap and is flagged.  Arm O's outcome is one
field that combines rewards[-1], the error and the G21 counts; the campaign
report never takes success from rewards[-1] alone.  GATE-FAILED EPISODES (pre-registered): an episode
with any per-episode gate count above 0 (G21's list: G3, G4, G5, G6, G8,
G10, G11, G14, G15, G16, G18, G19's seed hygiene, G20) counts as a failure
of its arm, keeps its row and is flagged with the gate's name; it is never
excluded and never re-run.  PER-RUN GATES: a G13 violation (tree,
registrations, pip freeze) or a G19 scene-map mismatch stops the campaign.
HARNESS STOP RULE: three gate-failed episodes of one arm with the same gate
also stop the campaign.  After any stop the partial table is reported with
the stop reason, and a run after the fix is a new campaign with its own BF#,
reported beside the stopped one.
STATISTICS.  Primary evidence: the paired per-seed table of repetition 1
(seed, scene file, T outcome, O outcome, both times).  Primary test: McNemar's
exact test on the discordant seeds; secondary: Fisher's exact test, two-
sided; Wilson 95 % intervals on every rate (11/20 is 0.34 to 0.74).  At n = 20
per arm two independent rates need a gap of roughly 7 seeds to reach
p < 0.05 by Fisher (the round-1 validity critique's estimate: 11/20 vs 17/20
gives about 0.08), so the headline is descriptive with intervals.  Repetition
2 is the stability check (per-seed flips reported) and is never pooled with
repetition 1.  Per-layout rates (8, 7, 5 seeds) are reported without tests.
The +/-1-2 seed flip rate of identical code is the noise floor, not a
significance threshold.
TIME.  (t1) loop time = time_deltas[-1] on both arms; T's contains no
physical simulation time (headless teleport, no gravity), O's contains stepped
physics; both labelled.  (t2) process wall from each driver.  (t3) time to
first reward 1.0 for successes.  (t4) actions to goal at macro level (pick,
place, sense, look; each move folded into the action it serves, the final move
home into the goal) with O's raw dispatched count beside it.  (t5) planning
share on both arms: T's summed get_action spans; O's summed plan() spans plus
first-render and boxelization time.  CLOCK BOUNDARIES (disclosed): T's
initial perception and belief-world build run inside their initialize(),
before the rollout clock starts; O's analogues (D9, the plan-twin build,
the first render, boxelization) and the gate hooks, the ledger and
world_eye run on O's clock.  This works against O; the report gives O's
set-up share as its own column so a reader can subtract it.  ANYTIME FIGURE: cumulative success
fraction against elapsed loop time up to 900 s for both arms (T: the time at
solve_step; O: the time of the final action), so a reader can apply any
budget.  All times as mean +/- std and median with n, over all episodes
(censored at the cap, flagged) and success-only.
MODE AND BUDGETS.  Headless on both arms; GUI runs are for watching only and
are never scored.
900 s wall per episode on both; T's 20 steps as shipped; O has no action cap.
MACHINE (binds B1(g), every A/A and A/B acceptance run of B3, B5, M6 and M7,
B7's headless episodes and B8).  Serial; in B8 interleaved per seed (T k, O
k, T k, O k ...); idle machine; host kept awake; before each episode
`pgrep -af` with the pattern
  test_full_pipeline|run_planner|run_bridge_on_finddice|run_tampura_timed|tampura_sweep|_bridge_campaign
shows no process other than the driver itself; CB#113 paused for the whole
window.  tools/_bridge_campaign.py owns the 900 s cap on both arms in B8.
Stalled-episode rule, written down now: an episode whose wall clock exceeds
1.5x the cap (host sleep) is dropped and re-run once, flagged.
DISCLOSURES IN THE TABLE NOTES: containment vs lateral shadows and the hollow
class declaration (M2, parity sentence); the eye at their home wrist pose,
fixed, observing after every action, vs their moving wrist camera observing
on look from 20 to 30 cm (also in the headline, M5); visibility needs 6
localisable pixels on our side and 1 seg pixel on theirs (perception.py:320 vs
env.py:64-73); the clearance precondition if M1; kinematic gravity-free
execution vs physical execution with gravity (D9); their perfect poses vs our
depth estimates; their saved grasp library vs our top-down contact grasp (and
M6 if adopted); at-home strictness (D3, D7) and the readback naming rule
beside it (M3); masses, the settle and its
campaign maximum displacement, their cups floating 1 cm and their die at about
4 cm inside the cup in 15 of 20 seeds on the campaign map (D9); on our arm,
in those seeds, the die's fall inside its cup during the settle, its tilt
(21.2 or 49.8 deg) and its residual motion (D9); W, a rectangle inside the
eye's unoccluded view, restricts our placements, theirs are unrestricted
(D8); on our arm moves made while holding are re-solved with the held body
as cargo (D7 (iv)), which the thesis runs did not do; every lateral shadow
on our arm is cast against the belief-posed plan twin (registered bodies at
their estimates, unregistered ones parked), while the thesis runs cast
against the simulated world (R6, item (h)); the task
knowledge each system was given (M5); three layouts; every cup fully in
frame for this eye (BF#11 (e); provisional until the corrected-inertia
re-measure of BF#12 (4)).
OUTPUT.  eval_results/tampura_bridge_<date>/{T,O}/rep<k>.json in the 2026-05-26
JSON's per-seed shape plus scene_file, and for O planning_time_s, plan_count,
exit_reason, parked_count, settle_max_disp, scene_changed_by_settle; copied
RolloutHistory pickles and console logs; campaign.json with both commits, the
config snapshot, problems/ hashes, the task_spec hash, the D9 values, the D12
venv choice and both pip-freeze hashes, CPU, WSL kernel, PYTHONHASHSEED.
Never WSL /tmp; previous campaigns are never overwritten.
COMMANDS.  T as-shipped block (B1(g)): wsl -d Ubuntu -e bash -lc 'source
/root/tampura-work/.venv/bin/activate && cd /root/tampura-work/
tampura_environments && PYTHONHASHSEED=<pinned> python -u /root/tampura-work/
tampura_sweep.py --seeds 0-19 --out runs/<name>.json'.  T GUI: bash
/root/tampura-work/tampura_run_gui.sh N 1.  O: the B2 command with --vis 0
(headless) or --vis 1 (GUI).  The interleaved driver is
tools/_bridge_campaign.py (B8); it always passes --save-dir to the T driver
(whose default save_dir now resolves into our tools/) and never passes
--vis to it (their parser reads --vis as type=bool, so '--vis 0' means GUI).

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
P4.7  RECORD FORMAT
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Every decision, fix, measurement and abort during #P4 is a BF#N entry in
P4.14, in this shape:
  BF#N [OPEN|DONE|REJECTED|ABORTED]  <title>  (package Bk, date)
  WHAT: one paragraph.  EVIDENCE: file:line, run directory, numbers.
  BEFORE / AFTER (for any model, constant or harness change).
  CLAIM EFFECT: which thesis sentence(s) change and how (or "none").
  HONESTY CLASS: translation | model tweak | configuration | measurement |
  infrastructure | refactor | FORBIDDEN (then ABORTED with the reason).
  DECISION yyyy-mm-dd (who): the choice and the alternatives rejected.
  RESULT: outcome lines once run.
Commits: one per BF# entry where code changes, one-line subjects, on
tampura-bridge.  The bridge wiki page is updated per BF# that changes
architecture or the domain.  Agent review reports go under
archive/tampura_bridge_plan_2026-10-25/reviews/.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
P4.8  OUT OF SCOPE, AND WHAT STAYS OPEN FOR LW1 / 3D WUMPUS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
OUT: any planner other than PDDLStream + FD as configured today (--planner
ff-astar2, search_sample_ratio 0.25, unit costs off, sense gate off, corridor
test cell+object, fixed for the campaign); TAMPURA's grasp library and
controllers (#P5; B9 is the only sanctioned contact with their controllers);
the real robot (#P6); LW1 (#P9); editing their tree; probabilistic weighting
of regions; an action cap on our arm.
KEPT OPEN: belief as literals over named regions (interior regions are more
names); deterministic observations as a function of the true world through
the render; the planned observable Y_R = "target in region R" with Pre(Y_R) =
view_clear(R) and handempty (and at_config(q_clear) if M1), with
boxel_fits(o, R) as a static applicability literal (emitted for every pair
under --sense-gate off).  A SECOND, PASSIVE CHANNEL exists: every render
evaluates every region's grid and every visible body with the render's own
visibility test as its precondition, not a planner literal (full-registry
sweep, execution.py:2185-2201; lost-caster retirement, :2105-2125;
register_new_detections, :2255-2285); it writes found, not_here (all
endpoints seen) or a new occluder, never a parked status; in LW1 terms these
are observables whose preconditions are not modelled in the domain and are
absorbed by replanning.  G5 enumerates both channels.  No probabilities, no
learned priors, no planner-external choosers.  KNOWN GAP (not opened by the
bridge): parked fragments are given the not_here literal in the planner
(P4.5 correction 1).  The LW1-clean alternative, withholding is_shadow or
boxel_fits for a parked fragment so it is unsensable rather than known, is a
main-pipeline candidate (BF# to file) that needs the B3 acceptance on our
scenes and is not done silently inside the bridge.  SECOND KNOWN GAP, closed
in the bridge by M7 unless the user rejects it: the rediscovery cleanup's
unobserved removal (P4.5 M7, correction (6)); in LW1 terms it is a write to a
K-literal with no observable behind it.  STANDING PREMISE, shared by the
thesis and the bridge: free space counts as observed space because the
workspace (W in the bridge) is imaged in full at t=0 and the world changes
only through actions that a render follows (audit #67).  A body displaced
into free space while the arm hides it from the eye would break it; lateral
shadows (no new shadow after a place, methods.tex:157) and interior regions
(D6's status rule) rest on it alike, and the paper states it once.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
P4.9  RISKS AND THE GATE THAT CATCHES EACH
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
R1  Cup width vs finger span decides ALL 20 seeds (every layout hides the die
    under a d or e cup).  Gate: D11 on estimated widths, decided in B6.
R2  The 1-cup layout makes the plan short; the temptation is to script it,
    or to steer the planner through its inputs.  Gate: G3, G10 (including
    the goal and target pin), G11; the plan still comes out of solve() from
    the belief.
R3  At-home within 1e-4 rad under position control with a payload.  Gate:
    B1(i) measures; D7 (i) to (vii) fix the goal, the init facts, the cargo
    re-solve, the evaluator scope, the renaming, the termination, the bound
    and the hold, all in B3(d) with two tests; HOME_HOLD_STEP_CAP follows a
    rule stated before B1(i) runs and is frozen in B6; no tolerance rule is
    ever chosen from outcomes.
R4  Default-client coupling.  Gate: B1(d), B3(e), G14.
R5  The hidden die is a real body in the executing world.  Gate: every
    planning-time collision check and every shadow raycast runs on the
    belief-posed plan twin (B3(c)); G2, G8(b)(c).
R6  The shadow caster raycasts the true world (PAPER_AUDIT item (h)): rays
    stop at the first body hit, so an unregistered die would clamp a
    fragment.  Gate: B3(c)'s shadow-raycast client, set to the plan twin by
    the bridge and synced before every cast, the initial one included; G2
    and G8(b) trap rayTest and rayTestBatch on the executing client.  Our
    scenes keep the default until a separate BF# decides with the B3
    acceptance; the bridge is stricter than the thesis pipeline here, which
    is disclosed.
R7  The implementer "helps" with a fallback (retry, nudge, re-aim, park,
    sweep).  Gate: G3's motion-layer hook (MultiDof calls included); G1 and
    G14 on velocity, force, collision-filter, constraint and body-creation
    calls; G6's zero count of stepSimulation outside main(); G21.  A new
    retry policy beyond today's dispatcher bounds is FORBIDDEN unless it
    lands in the main pipeline with the B3 acceptance and a BF#.  The new
    bounds are home_not_reached and HOME_HOLD_STEP_CAP (D7), both
    pre-registered here; the cap's value comes only from D7 (vii)'s rule.
    M8's observe bound is pre-registered here too (samplers plan C2, BF#33,
    main pipeline, accepted with M8 by B3's BF#13 test): an observe that
    sees its object but not completely within the margin is a strike and
    removes that (look configuration, object) pair; the object is parked
    (no look facts) after 3 strikes or once no pair is left; strikes,
    removed pairs and parking reset when the object is re-registered or
    re-posed (box moved > 1 mm).  An observe that does not see the object
    takes the lost path, no strike.
R8  Constants or rules fitted to the released layouts or to outcomes.  Gate:
    G9; B1 decisions run on an empty table; B6 before B7; task_spec hash in
    campaign.json.
R9  Scene drift from the physics settle, including the die settling into
    view.  Gate: D9 order, assert and scene_changed_by_settle row; no
    re-seat, no re-roll.
R10 Machine contention, host sleep.  Gate: P4.6 MACHINE.
R11 The 20-step vs wall-cap asymmetry invites post hoc re-weighting.  Gate:
    D10 pre-registered; the anytime figure shows every budget.
R12 Session budget: this plan's first agent run was cut by the usage limit on
    2026-10-25; agent passes write durable per-agent files.
R13 Schedule.  Gate: the calendar, the process-only GO criterion and the
    pre-registered outcomes (B7 cannot suppress an unfavourable result).
R14 Arm occlusion of the fixed eye parks regions.  Gate: D5's M1 rule.
R15 Zero gravity in their world would make our physical execution a global
    weld.  Gate: D9 step (1), B1(c) assert, G14 on setGravity.
R16 Two interpreters building different scenes, or arm O's dependencies
    leaking into arm T's venv.  Gate: D12's five tests, separate venvs, G13's
    freeze hash.
R17 A gate trip handled after seeing which arm and seed tripped.  Gate: P4.6
    GATE-FAILED EPISODES and HARNESS STOP RULE, pre-registered.
R18 The executor's own IK on the executing robot mistaken for, or used to
    hide, a teleport.  Gate: G14's net-zero exemption for solve_ik only,
    inside a dispatcher context.

P4.10 OPEN QUESTIONS FOR THE USER (the plan proposes an answer to each)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Q1  D1/D2: our whole pipeline inside their world through a one-action schema;
    the planner-only variant stays B9, optional.
Q2  D4: a fixed eye at the pose of their wrist camera at DEFAULT_ARM_POS (not
    the physical wrist camera); M1 decided by B1(h), adoption expected.
Q3  D6: hollow declared per category with mesh-measured cavities, not cast
    for every object.
Q4  D9: gravity, timestep, masses, object friction 1.0 (our scenes' value),
    our gripper's pad friction and opening set in their world before the
    first step; main()'s own settle, logged and disclosed.
Q5  D10/P4.6: 900 s cap, headless, two repetitions, no action cap on ours,
    paired analysis with McNemar.
Q6  B3: a mechanical main-pipeline refactor (RobotModel by name, an
    adopt-client constructor with plan-client-only mirrors and a close()
    that spares the adopted client, seven keyword arguments on main() and
    no goal argument, a shadow-raycast client, D7's loop change), every new
    behaviour default off.
Q7  The calendar has no slack; the process-only GO criterion and the
    reporting rule if B7 cannot meet it in time.
Q8  The exact submission date, to fix the latest B8 start.  ANSWERED
    2026-10-26: 2026-12-04, so the latest B8 start is 2026-11-24 (BF#1).
Q9  D11 branch (iii): if no cup class fits the gripper even by its top face,
    the campaign is not runnable as specified; the user decides then.
Q10 M7: in the bridge, the rediscovery cleanup keeps every superseded
    fragment that does not render empty (a switch, default off on our
    scenes).  If rejected, today's unobserved removal stays, counted per
    episode and disclosed (G5, correction (6)).  ANSWERED 2026-10-28
    (user): option (c), M7 everywhere: the main pipeline adopts it with
    default ON for the thesis scenes too (A/B with BF#13's test), and
    CB#113 is re-run after it lands (BF#15).
Q11 D12: arm O in its own venv, preferably a clone of theirs plus our pinned
    dependencies, never in theirs.
Q12 D7 (vii): if B1(i) shows their 1e-4 at-home test unreachable with the
    die payload, the at-home conjunct fails by construction; the test stays
    strict and the user decides before B7.  (Does not arise: BF#11 (i).)
Q13 B3's acceptance (BF#10): no outcome flip reproduced, but three
    plan-count differences reproduced exactly (seeds 100, 105, 119), so the
    rule as written fails B3; diagnostic runs show c31cbdd itself producing
    each of those plan counts.  Proposed: accept B3 on this evidence, and
    pre-register for the acceptances still to come (B5, M6, M7) a plan-count
    test the reference can pass, for example the per-seed plan-count
    distribution over three runs per arm compared seed by seed, fixed before
    those runs.  Alternative: keep the per-seed rule and investigate further
    before B2 lands.  ANSWERED 2026-10-27 (user): accept B3 on the
    diagnostic evidence, and pre-register a distributional plan-count test
    for the B5, M6 and M7 acceptances before those runs (BF#13).
Q14 GF10, the die loss on the move home (BF#11: 91 of 96 probe trials; BF#14
    RG1.1).  Proposed, fixed before the probe runs: an empty-table probe
    extending B1(i), one factor at a time (payload class: the die, each cup
    class and a 100 g control; squeeze force; a transport speed scale;
    solver iterations or substeps; the pick from the table at B6's floor
    instead of a box created at the EE), logging where a lost body lands.
    Rule: adopt the first remedy, in that order, that keeps a die-class and
    a cup-class body between the pads at >= 0.5 N in >= 95 % of the 96
    trials; it lands as a default-off main-pipeline parameter with the B3
    acceptance and BF#13's test, or as a disclosed D9 amendment of engine
    parameters.  Masses and frictions stay as declared (G9); no retry (R7).
    If nothing qualifies, the user decides before B7: run as specified and
    attribute the failures to the gripper, or B9.  ANSWERED 2026-10-28
    (user): the rule as proposed (BF#15).  AMENDED 2026-10-29, before the
    probe runs (samplers plan Q-D, BF#34): since M9 and the aperture rule
    ship ON, B7's die grip is the M9 grip; the probe's baseline is the
    accepted M8-M10 configuration, it runs after M9's BF#13 acceptance,
    and a remedy qualifies only with M9 and the aperture rule ON.  Nothing
    in the samplers plan is a remedy for GF10 (seed 0 with M8-M10 on still
    loses the die on the move home, BF#33).  RUN 2026-10-30 (BF#38): the
    0.5 mm squeeze qualifies (die 93 of 96); default off until accepted.
Q15PDDLStream's stream statistics (BF#14 RG1.12): every solve() loads and
    rewrites statistics/py3/boxel-streams.pkl in the working directory, so
    arm O episodes and acceptance arms depend on the runs before them.
    Proposed: a fresh, empty statistics directory per episode, archived with
    the run, for the bridge and the acceptance harness alike, and the paper
    says the thesis runs used an accumulated file.  Alternative: LOAD and
    SAVE off.  ANSWERED 2026-10-28 (user): neither for now.  The
    accumulated file stays; every episode records its position in the run
    sequence and the statistics file it started from (size and sha256, or
    none).  Whether the history changes performance is decided by an
    experiment (fresh against accumulated) after the bridge and the
    migration (BF#15).
Q16 Local smokes and probes (BF#12 (5), BF#11) ran in the laptop's arm T
    venv, against D12 and the Q11 answer (no install, nothing written; the
    campaign's arm T venv is the cluster's and its freeze is unchanged).
    Proposed: record it as a deviation and build a local arm O venv (a
    clone of the local arm T package set) for smokes and probes from now on.
    ANSWERED 2026-10-28 (user): as proposed (BF#15).
Q17 One success criterion for both arms (user decision 2026-10-29, BF#35):
    TAMPURA's die rides by a pose reset to the gripper every waypoint with
    no physics, and their reward reads their belief; ours is friction held
    and scored from pad contact.  Proposed: (a) the bridge carries a held
    body the way their environment does after our verified grasp, holding
    scored from that grasp, at-home from the joint readback; or (b) both
    physical, which changes their executor.  Plus: keep or drop our
    both-pads grasp check (theirs has none).  ANSWERED 2026-10-29
    (user): neither as written; our perception and planner on their
    controllers (B9), design in BF#36.
Q18 Finger rule scope under B9 (BF#36): our executor attaches only the
    body between the closed pads; proposed that the shared success test
    also checks both pads within 1 mm of the die for both arms, or no pad
    test at evaluation.  OPEN.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
P4.11 CRITIQUE LOG, ROUND 1 (draft 1, 2026-10-25; four adversarial critics,
      files reviews/critique_r1_{cheat,pod,validity,feasibility}.json)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
Every critic returned "revise".  Blockers: 4 (three critics independently found
the same scoring defect).  No finding was refuted outright; partial
acceptances say what was not taken and why.

CHEAT DETECTOR
C1  [blocker] D3 wrote grasp_body as a PyBullet body id; their reward unifies
    holding with is-target over STORE SYMBOLS (env.py:306-321, 362, 394), so
    arm O could never score 1.0, and G6's negative-only tests would have
    certified the defect.  ACCEPTED: D3 rewritten (store-symbol lookup as in
    env.py:70-73, grasp symbol through store.add_typed); G6 runs positive
    controls first.
C2  [major] The wrist camera "at q_home" would render from a cached pose while
    the arm is elsewhere (nine non-sense render sites).  ACCEPTED IN PART: the
    camera is decided now (D4) and G20 checks every render.  The critic's
    preferred option, the physical wrist camera with non-sense renders
    suppressed away from home, is REJECTED: the planning-time census needs a
    render the physical camera could only take after an unplanned move home
    (CB#120(g)); a fixed eye at the home wrist pose is the thesis's sensor
    model unchanged.
C3  [major] G8 contradicted the main pipeline's declared identity map, and
    update_object_positions fills ObjectInfo with true poses.  ACCEPTED: G8 is
    now the identity-map boundary with runtime hooks; update_object_positions
    is a no-op on the adapter and counted by G16.
C4  [major] main() runs the Phase-4 oracle split and picks the target with
    random.choice (test_full_pipeline.py:692, 728-733).  ACCEPTED: B3(c) adds
    target_name and oracle_phases keyword arguments with byte-identical
    defaults; the B3 A/B checks identical target choices.
C5  [major] G11 would not catch a hand-built init with a direct solve(), and
    plan() returns a new list.  ACCEPTED: G11 rewritten (no pddlstream import
    in the bridge, a solve-caller hook, id equality on plan()'s list).
C6  [major] G14 wrapped the BulletClient instance; our code calls the pybullet
    module.  ACCEPTED: module-level wrap dispatching on client and body.
C7  [major] The at-home tolerance rule was deferred to B7, where the only
    implementation is writing the constant (CB#123).  ACCEPTED: D7 decides now
    (their exact test, executor final hold, home_not_reached bound, secondary
    columns computed from the logged readback); B7 measures only.
C8  [major] Interior regions would re-spawn under every relocated cup.

BF#2 [DONE]  B0: GATES, RUNTIME HOOKS AND THE RUN LEDGER  (B0, 2026-10-26)
  WHAT: The gates exist as runnable checks in two places.  The static and
  ledger side is tools/bridge_gates.py (standard library only): the import
  graph, G1 and G2 by AST scan against the reviewed allowlist
  tools/bridge_gates_allowlist.txt, G7, G10's domain hashes, G11(i), G13
  (their trees, git status, pip freeze, T-driver diff), G17, and a ledger
  reader for G3, G4, G5, G11, G15, G19 and G21.  The runtime side is
  tampura_bridge/gate_hooks.py, because the harness must import it and tools/
  is not a package: install(GateConfig) wraps the pybullet module for G3's
  motor hook, G6's step count, G8(b)'s queries and rays, G14's state writes
  and G20's camera, rebinds robot_utils.solve_ik (G14's net-zero exemption),
  move_robot_smooth (G3) and both collision checks (task-body poses
  unchanged) in every loaded module of this repository, and wraps
  PDDLStreamPlanner.plan (G10, including the goal and target pin) and the
  solve() it calls (G11(ii)).  Every hook counts, writes a ledger row and
  raises; gate_record() writes the counts (G21).  The G6 controls are seven
  pytest cases in tampura_bridge/tests/test_g6_controls.py that fail until B2
  and B7.  The ledger is <run_dir>/ledger.jsonl, opened by RunLogger, so it
  serves our scenes too: one row per plan() call and result, per dispatched
  step and how it ended, per runtime move re-solve, per commanded place
  target, per sense outcome, and per belief change of a SHADOW fragment.
  FINDINGS AND DEVIATIONS FROM THE PLAN TEXT, each decided here:
  (a) BulletClient injects its client id only into builtins
      (pybullet_utils.bullet_client: inspect.isbuiltin), so the plan's "one
      wrap covers both call styles" was wrong as stated: a Python wrapper on
      the module would receive no client id from a BulletClient and act on
      client 0.  install() gives BulletClient.__getattr__ the same injection
      for the wrapped functions; forwarding is otherwise unchanged.  This is
      pybullet_utils' class, not TAMPURA's; B1(d) confirms which client class
      their executing world uses.  Tested.
  (b) G3's early-end triggers.  The dispatcher has 17 exits; each now names
      one of: sense_outcome, integrity_replan, motion_resolve_failed,
      pick_failure, place_failure, stack_failure (the plan's "IK failure"
      and "give-up"; give-ups are decided inside these paths), empty_hand
      and stale_precondition (the step's precondition no longer holds in the
      belief).  The last two are existing dispatcher bounds, not new retries.
      The dispatcher has no deadline exit; the wall-clock bound is the outer
      loop's.  An exit without a name is written as 'unlabelled' and fails.
  (c) G4 compares the executor's commanded target with the planned boxel's
      registry centre at 1e-6 m (in our dispatcher it is that centre); the
      released body's position is not measured here.
  (d) G5 is enforced at the data layer, not only at call sites: BeliefState
      (mark_sensed, mark_unresolved, remove_shadow, add_shadow) and
      BoxelRegistry.remove_boxel for SHADOW boxels write the row; call sites
      name the channel (planned_sense_render on handle_sense_action,
      sweep_render on sweep_all_fragments, lost_caster_render on
      retire_lost_objects, strike_park at both parks, superseded_unrendered at
      the rediscovery cleanup).  A change made where no channel was named
      fails, so a new unlabelled site cannot pass silently.
  (e) G6 counts executing-client steps: steps inside a dispatched step reset
      the count, steps outside it after the first dispatched step accumulate
      and become G6 violations at gate_record; main()'s settle before the
      first dispatch is counted separately (it is D9 step (5)).
  (f) G8(b): an unfiltered getContactPoints (no bodyA) on the executing
      client is a violation.
  (g) The allowlist.  The graph has 22 modules, rooted at test_full_pipeline.py
      as a declared root (D1 runs its main()) until B2's drivers exist.  40 G1
      and 35 G2 hits, each reviewed by hand: plan-client writes and the plan-
      twin mirror; the three solve_ik resets (G14 exemption); print-only
      ground-truth diagnostics and the scoring-only verifiers, world_eye and
      telemetry (write-only); and our own scene construction, the oracle
      paths, BoxelTestEnv.reset, the Phase-4 oracle read and the overlay
      bodies (unreachable on the bridge path through B3(b), the G16
      overrides, oracle_phases=False and draw_boxel_overlays=False; asserted
      at run time by the hooks, not taken on trust).  No hit is a new honesty
      problem.  Two need action before review gate 1: the shadow caster
      (shadow_calculator.py:160) runs on the default client, i.e. on their
      executing world, until B3(c), so G8(b) would fail every bridge episode
      before then; and world_eye and telemetry must run inside
      gate_hooks.write_only in bridge runs, or be off.
  (h) Numbering: B1's record, called BF#2 in P4.4, takes the next free number.
  BEFORE / AFTER: no gates, no ledger / the files above.  Main-pipeline
  changes are logging only: run_logger.py (ledger, dispatcher context,
  provenance), belief.py and boxel_data.py (provenance rows), execution.py
  (channel labels), test_full_pipeline.py (plan and step rows, the 17
  labelled exits, move_resolve, place_target, sense_result).
  EVIDENCE: archive/tampura_bridge_plan_2026-10-25/bf/BF2_b0/: gates_and_tests.txt
  (bridge_gates.py all exit 0; 33 tests pass: 19 hook tests including G10's

      (B1, 2026-10-27)
  WHAT: TAMPURA picks a seed's scene with random.choice(os.listdir(problems))
  (env.py:801-811), so the map from seed to scene depends on the order in
  which the filesystem lists the directory, which P4.2 had flagged ("os.listdir
  order dependent").  Measured: the laptop's ext4 lists [1747173590,
  1747173591, 1747173593]; the cluster's NFS lists [1747173593, 1747173591,
  1747173590].  Each seed draws the same index on both, so on the cluster
  seeds 1, 2, 3, 4, 8, 14, 15, 18 load 1747173593 (not 1747173590) and
  seeds 5, 6, 10, 17, 19 load 1747173590 (not 1747173593); the seeds of
  1747173591 (0, 7, 9, 11, 12, 13, 16) are unchanged.  Seen first in BF#7's
  layout signatures (the die's pose identifies the layout), confirmed by
  listing both directories and by B1(g)'s console (seed 1: "Loading scene
  from 1747173593.826745.json").  The pre-registered map cannot be restored
  by re-creating their directory: in a scratch directory on the same NFS,
  the six creation orders of the three real file names list as [93, 91, 90]
  three times, [91, 93, 90] twice and [90, 93, 91] once, never [90, 91, 93].
  G19, as pinned, would stop every cluster campaign on its first episode of
  a changed seed; it caught this before any campaign episode.
  OPTIONS.  (A) The cluster's own map becomes the campaign map: both arms
  read the same directory on the same NFS, so seed for seed holds between
  them; G19's SCENE_MAP is re-pinned from B1(g)'s consoles and still asserted

  5 (1747173590) instead of 8 / 7 / 5 for 1747173590 / 91 / 93.  Their code,
  data and tree stay as they are.  (B) Keep the pre-registered map by
  running both arms from a copy of their tree on a filesystem whose order we
  control (e.g. a tmpfs copy per job), which adds a step that is ours, not
  theirs, to every episode of both arms.  Not considered: re-rolling or
  choosing seeds (forbidden, H4), or editing their scene loader (G13).

  1747173593 (four cups, the die under an e cup, BF#4's cup beyond our span)

  (1) One active RobotModel per process, not a model argument on every
      function: the pipeline drives one robot, its plan twin loads the

      at-home test, else q_readback_0.  D7 (v) names only moves; without

      xy, and _grasp_ee_to_obj_z on the held object's estimated box, the

      today's re-solve copies and which puts the cargo at the approach
      height, 10 cm below the EE instead of about 2 cm.
  (4) D7 (iv) keeps plan_motion's semantics: a configuration that ignores
      a body is a pick or place configuration, so a cargo move relaxes the
      gripper at its endpoints and ignores the supports, as every move
      made while holding does today.

      sense slices (perception.sense_ray_slices over the declared cavity)
      tested on the render (first_surface_interceptors; out of view counts
      as blocked): the worst blocked fraction is 1.0 (a_cups_ud at
      (0.375, -0.105)), and 42 to 44 of the 96 cells exceed 0.05 in every
      class.  M1 is ADOPTED by the pre-registered rule, as the plan
      expected.  The 5 cm grid stands for "every cell of W": a 1 cm grid
      would mean 12000 renders for the same verdict.
  (i) At-home convergence: D9's gravity, timestep, pad friction and opening
      on the empty table; from the post-place lift pose of the die class
      (EE 0.135 m) over the 96 cells to DEFAULT_ARM_POS through
      move_robot_smooth (one velocity-bounded segment with the endpoint
      settle), then hold_at_config with the 2400-step probe cap, empty
      handed and with a die-class box (17.6 x 17.3 x 17.8 mm, 5.2 g) closed
      on by close_gripper_until_contact.  Today's settle never passes
      TAMPURA's test (largest error 1.98e-3 rad, the settle's own 2e-3
      stop); the hold passes in every trial within 26 steps (largest
      residual 1.76e-4 rad, inside rtol + atol).  The payload stayed between
      the pads in only 5 of its 96 trials (see the FINDING); those 5 passed
      within 22 to 23 steps, the others ran effectively empty handed.  So
      HOME_HOLD_STEP_CAP = 2 x 26 = 52 by D7 (vii)'s rule on the measured
      maximum, with the payload measured in 5 trials only, and Q12 does not
      arise; B6 freezes it.
  FINDING (for B7): the die-class box was held by both pads once the grasp
  had settled (7 to 10 N pad force) in all 96 trials, but left the pads on
  the move home in 91 and lay on the table afterwards (the first run of (i),
  probe_i_first.json, showed the same 91 losses; the second added the
  held-state checkpoints).  With BF#10's finding (our cube rolled out of the
  pads on a cargo move to REST_POSES) our friction grasp does not carry a
  light body through a long move to the home configuration in these runs.
  B7 meets this on every episode it reaches; any remedy is a main-pipeline
  change with the B3 acceptance and its own BF#, never a bridge-side helper
  or retry (R7).
  BEFORE / AFTER: P4.2's assumptions / measured as above; task_spec now
  holds the eye (final, from their code) and W (provisional until B6).
  CLAIM EFFECT: none yet.  Disclosures: the cups are fully in frame (the
  planned "partly out of frame" note is dropped); M1 is adopted.
  HONESTY CLASS: measurement.  G9: every number comes from the URDF, the
  meshes, their source constants or a probe on the empty table; the
  layouts feed nothing.
  DECISION 2026-10-27 (executor): M1 adopted and HOME_HOLD_STEP_CAP 52, by
  the pre-registered rules; W, the reach envelope and D11's branch are
  B6's to freeze from these numbers.
  RESULT: archive/tampura_bridge_plan_2026-10-25/bf/BF11_b1p2/ (one JSON and
  one console per item; probe_i_first.* is the first run of (i)).
  CORRECTIONS (BF#14, review gate 1): HOME_HOLD_STEP_CAP 52 rests on the
  empty-hand trials for 91 of the 96 cells: the payload stayed between the
  pads in 5 trials only, so D7 (vii)'s "with the die-class payload" clause
  was not met.  The cap is re-measured with a payload that stays in the
  pads, and with the pass's persistence (N steps held after the first
  pass, worst error recorded), before B6 freezes it.  The die loss is no

BF#13 [DONE, pre-registered]  THE PLAN-COUNT TEST OF THE B5, M6 AND M7
      ACCEPTANCES  (protocol, 2026-10-27)
  WHAT: By the user's decision on Q13, the acceptances still to come (B5,
  M6, M7) replace the per-seed plan-count clause of B3's acceptance with a
  distributional test, fixed here before any of their runs and implemented
  in tools/acceptance_plan_counts.py and tools/cluster/acceptance_dist.sbatch.
  RUNS: the reference (the commit the change applies to) and the candidate
  (the change) each run our holding scene on seeds 0-19 and 100-119 three
  times, interleaved per seed (reference, then candidate) in every
  repetition, on one exclusive node (cn-110) with one harness file, in
  armO-venvA, PYTHONHASHSEED 0 (P4.6 MACHINE).
  PLAN-COUNT CLAUSE: a seed enters when all six of its runs reached the
  planner (a scene the pre-flight rejects has no plan count on either arm
  and leaves; a seed with any other missing count is listed and left out).
  With R_s and C_s the reference's and the candidate's three plan counts on
  seed s, T = sum over the seeds of (mean C_s - mean R_s).  Null hypothesis:
  the change leaves each seed's plan-count distribution unchanged, so the
  six counts of a seed are exchangeable between the arms; T is recomputed
  under 20000 random three-to-three splits of every seed's six counts
  (random.Random(0)) and p is the share of draws with |T*| >= |T|.  The
  clause PASSES when p >= 0.05.
  UNCHANGED: the outcome clause (flips between the first repetitions of the
  arms accepted up to the A/A's count, 3 by BF#7; repetition 2 is each
  flipped seed's one re-run on both arms; a flip that reproduces fails)
  and the identical Phase-4 target clause, now over all six runs.
  CHECK (synthetic counts only, nothing from any real arm, so the rule is
  not fitted to data it will judge): with the reference's distribution
  reproduced the test passes (p 0.33); with one more plan on half the seeds
  it fails (p 0.007).
  BEFORE / AFTER: per-seed exact reproduction of a plan-count difference
  fails the acceptance / the aggregate exchangeability test above, for B5,
  M6 and M7.
  CLAIM EFFECT: none.
  HONESTY CLASS: configuration (protocol).
  DECISION 2026-10-27 (user, Q13; the test's form fixed by the executor the
  same day, before any B5, M6 or M7 run).
  RESULT: n/a until those acceptances run.

BF#35 [OPEN, decision]  ONE ENVIRONMENT, ONE SUCCESS CRITERION; HOW TAMPURA
      CARRIES AND SCORES THE DIE  (code reading + probes, 2026-10-29)
  DECISION (user, 2026-10-29): the comparison is one to one.  The planners
  differ; the environment and the success criterion are the same for both
  arms.  Supersedes M4's asymmetric scoring (our belief inputs from
  physical readback, theirs from their kinematic executor).
  EVIDENCE, THEIR CARRY (tampura_environments/tampura_environments/):
  pick_execute_fn sets state.grasp (find_dice/env.py:396); from then on
  SceneState.apply_sequence (panda_utils/panda_env_utils.py) calls
  enforce_grasp() on every waypoint, which is Grasp.create_attachment(...)
  .assign() -> Attachment.assign (panda_utils/pb_utils.py:2656) ->
  set_pose -> resetBasePositionAndOrientation (pb_utils.py:944).  The arm
  moves by resetJointState (step_curve, pb_utils.py:1605); headless pick
  and place teleport to the path end (teleport = not has_gui).  Physics
  steps only for sim_steps, default 0.  MEASURED on their unmodified
  run_planner.py, seed 0 (reward 1.0; tools/_probe_tampura_carry.py counts
  calls at BulletClient.__getattr__; archive/tampura_bridge_plan_2026-10-25/
  bf/calib/tampura_carry_s0.json): the carry home made 0 stepSimulation and
  0 motor calls, 5187 resetJointState and 741 die pose resets; the die's
  offset in the tool frame stayed (3.81, -0.62, 1.32) mm to 0.001 mm while
  it rose 0.38 m; robot-die contacts at home: 0.  Their die cannot slip.
  EVIDENCE, THEIR SUCCESS TEST: reward = holding(target) and at-home on
  their abstract BELIEF (find_dice/env.py:993-1004, policy.py rollout).
  holding comes from the observation's grasp, which pick_execute_fn sets
  whenever grasp IK and the motion plan succeed (no contact check);
  at-home is np.allclose(belief conf, DEFAULT_ARM_POS, 1e-4), and
  home_execute_fn (env.py:563-580) builds its observation with
  conf=DEFAULT_ARM_POS BEFORE planning and returns it when planning fails,
  so their belief can read at-home with the arm never moved.  Whether this
  path occurs in their 20 seeds is not checked.
  EVIDENCE, OUR CARRY (tools/_probe_grip_transport.py, die-class box on our
  table, M9 on, yaws 0/15/30/45, carry to the start configuration; files
  bf/calib/grip_transport*.json): held at home 0/4 at today's 60 N and
  3 mm squeeze; 70 N identical (the cap does not bind, pad force 11-35 N);
  6 mm and a full close worse; 200 solver iterations (pad force 50-100 N),
  frictionAnchor, both, spinning friction 0.002-0.02 and a carry at 1/2
  and 1/4 joint speed: 1 of 48 held; squeeze 0.5 mm: 4/4, 1 mm: 1/4.  At
  loss the fingers sit at the hold target: the position overdrive drives
  the pads into the 5 g body until it is expelled.  NOT the Q14 probe
  (not its 96 trials, payloads or order); informal, picks no remedy.
  OPEN QUESTION Q17 (user): how the shared criterion is realised.
  Proposed: (a) the bridge carries a held body the way their environment
  does, a pose reset to the gripper each step after OUR physically
  verified grasp, and scoring reads holding from that grasp instead of pad
  contact; at-home stays the joint readback (equal to theirs or stricter,
  the conf quirk disclosed).  (b) Both physical: needs a change to their
  executor, so not "their system as is".  Sub-question either way: our
  grasp must pass a both-pads contact check, theirs passes on IK and a
  motion plan; same criterion means deciding whether to keep that check.
  CLAIM EFFECT: no number before Q17 is answered; M4's sentence "The paper
  states both" is replaced by one shared criterion.  GF10 (Q14) stays the
  remedy track for our own scenes, where the carry stays physical.
  HONESTY CLASS: evaluation protocol.

BF#36 [PARKED 2026-10-30 by BF#37]  Q17 ANSWERED: B9, OUR PERCEPTION AND PLANNER
      ON THEIR CONTROLLERS; ONE WORLD-STATE SUCCESS TEST  (code reading,
      2026-10-30)
  DECISION (user, 2026-10-29): realise BF#35 by running our perception
  (depth camera, boxelization, M8-M10 pose estimates) and our planner on
  TAMPURA's execute layer, their files unedited (B9, now the main arm O
  route, no longer optional).  No oracle anywhere outside evaluation.
  THEIR EXECUTE LAYER (panda_utils/panda_env_utils.py):
  SceneState.apply_sequence(commands, teleport, sim_steps=0) moves the arm
  and fingers by resetJointState through GroupTrajectory.iterate and calls
  enforce_grasp() at every waypoint while state.grasp is set;
  pick_execute(state, grasp, motion_plan, pre_grasp) runs the motion plan,
  the pre grasp approach, a finger close from OPEN_GRIPPER_POS to
  grasp.closed_position in GRIPPER_RES steps, sets state.grasp and
  retreats; place_execute mirrors it and clears state.grasp.  A Grasp is
  (body, grasp = the body's pose in the tool tip frame, pregrasp,
  closed_position).  No physics step happens anywhere in it.  Their
  find_dice pick_execute_fn (env.py:359-396) plans IK and motion in the
  BELIEF's sim world and only executes in the executing world.
  THE SEAM.  Our dispatcher moves the arm with move_robot_smooth along the
  planner's waypoints (test_full_pipeline.py:1828) and ends a move home
  with hold_at_config (:1844); execute_pick, execute_place and
  execute_stack (execution.py:908, 1401, 1574) approach, close, verify
  and release physically.  Under B9 a bridge module installs, for arm O
  only, their execute layer behind those primitives:
    (1) arm motion: the planner's waypoint path, interpolated as today,
        runs as one GroupTrajectory through state.apply_sequence with
        their teleport rule (teleport = not has_gui), on the SceneState
        their rollout passes to execute_fn;
    (2) finger close: GRIPPER_RES steps through apply_sequence until both
        pads touch one body geometrically (getClosestPoints <= 0, their
        close_until_collision rule moved from planning to execution);
    (3) attach: if that body is a task body, state.grasp = Grasp(body,
        its pose in the tool tip frame READ FROM THE EXECUTING WORLD at
        closure, closed_position = the measured width), so the body never
        jumps; otherwise no grasp and our verification sees an empty hand;
    (4) open: the reverse finger trajectory and state.grasp = None;
    (5) no physics step after D9's settle: hold_at_config, settle loops
        and the release drop observation issue none, as in theirs;
    (6) every force based check (pick contact floor, _verify_holding's
        0.5 N, the drop check) becomes the geometric both pads test of
        (2), since resetJointState fingers produce no contact force.
  THE THREE TRAPS, RESOLVED.  (i) Motion: their plan_motion collision
  checks the world it is given, so it is never called; our motion planner
  plans on the plan twin from boxels as today and only its path reaches
  apply_sequence.  A kinematic path passes through anything it was not
  planned around, in both arms; the evaluator logs, read only, every
  waypoint at which the robot or the held body penetrates another body in
  the executing world (one count, both arms).  (ii) Attach: their executor
  attaches the body the planner names even with the hand centimetres away;
  ours attaches only the body between the closed pads (3).  (iii)
  Identity: the attached body is the one physically pinched, never a
  lookup from our planner's symbol to their body id.
  THE SHARED SUCCESS TEST (both arms, the same code, read only, after the
  rollout, on the executing world's final SceneState; their files
  unedited): success iff state.grasp is set, state.grasp.body is the die,
  both pads are within 1 mm of it, and the 7 joint readbacks match
  DEFAULT_ARM_POS under their np.allclose(rtol=atol=1e-4).  Their own
  reward is reported next to it for both arms (it reads the belief and
  carries the home conf quirk of BF#35).  The pad test is the finger rule
  applied to both arms at evaluation; only our executor also applies it
  at attach, because our executor, unlike theirs, must find the body.
  WHAT IT READS FROM THE WORLD: (3) reads the pinched body's true pose at
  closure, the executor's own state (theirs reads the belief pose for the
  same transform).  The planner never receives it; a new gate phase
  'executor' scopes these reads and G2's ledger proves no planner side
  read sees them.  Proposed, needs the G rule written before code.
  CLAIM EFFECT: arm O tests perception plus planning on their execution
  layer, not D1's whole pipeline with physical execution; D1's physical
  result stays reported separately for our own scenes.  GF10 (die loss)
  cannot occur in find_dice under B9 and stays the remedy track for our
  scenes (Q14).  M4 stays superseded.
  OPEN QUESTION Q18 (user): the finger rule scope, as proposed above
  (evaluation for both arms, attach for ours only), or attach for ours
  only and no pad test at evaluation (their reward's convention).
  PLAN (each step ends with a BF#, serial runs only):
    S1  audit, read only: every stepSimulation, force read and finger
        command in one find_dice episode of arm O (call sites with
        counts, from a probe like _probe_tampura_carry.py on our side).
    S2  tampura_bridge/their_executor.py: (1) to (6), installed by a
        bridge switch --executor theirs, default ours until S5 passes.
    S3  tools/shared_success.py: the shared test and the penetration
        count, run on both arms' final states.
    S4  the 'executor' gate phase and its G rule; existing gates pass.
    S5  seed 0 headless and in the GUI (watch.ps1 gains -Executor),
        then seeds 0 to 2 on both arms locally; cluster only on approval.
  EFFORT: 3 to 4 days.  HONESTY CLASS: evaluation protocol and claim
  scope.

BF#43 [DONE]  PUSH_SNAPSHOT.PS1 NEVER UNPACKED A SNAPSHOT, AND ITS ARCHIVES
      HELD CRLF TEXT FILES  (found 2026-10-30 by the B8 smoke)
  WHAT: (1) The script pipes its unpack commands into "ssh cn-04 bash -s".
  PowerShell ends a string piped into a native command with CRLF, so bash
  read "fi\r", never closed the if and stopped with "line 7: syntax error:
  unexpected end of file" after every upload (the script's .Replace of CRs
  acts inside the string, not on that trailing newline); the 115 MB archive
  sat in $W/inbox and sbatch found no $W/sb_<sha>.  The remote side now
  strips every CR (tr -d '\r') before bash, the archive is gzip-tested, and
  the script stops on a failed scp or unpack instead of printing the sbatch
  lines.  (2) git archive on this laptop (core.autocrlf=true, no
  .gitattributes) writes every LF text blob as CRLF: 4566 of the 6801 files
  of sb_d8b51d4, every .sbatch and .sh among them (Slurm: "Batch script
  contains DOS line breaks"), the pddl/ files too.  The archive is now made
  with core.autocrlf=false and holds the commit's blobs byte for byte.
  (3) The uploaded sb_d8b51d4 was converted in place on cn-04 (sed on the
  4566 files, read-only again afterwards) and verified: a scratch git index
  over the directory writes tree fde84285e05bcfb092c9d6620a87829d929cdd02,
  the tree of commit d8b51d4; the 22 files that still hold CRs hold lone
  CRs, which git never converts.  BF#40's smoke runs on that directory.
  EVIDENCE: tools/cluster/push_snapshot.ps1; the laptop reproduction (the
  same here-string piped into wsl bash -n fails the same way, cat -A shows
  "fi^M"); git ls-files --eol (no index blob holds CRLF or mixed endings,
  so the in-place conversion is exact).
  CLAIM EFFECT: none; nothing of B8 had run.  HONESTY CLASS: infrastructure.

  that script's note promised did not exist; it does now),
  SemanticBoxels-prune-runs daily 12:20, SemanticBoxels-prune-cluster
  Mondays 12:25.

  prune_work.ps1 from the laptop, at the end of every b8_campaign.sbatch

  archive of an unpacked snapshot or lib goes at once, a results directory
  older than two days becomes one tar.gz, job logs older than two days are
  gzipped, a snapshot no queued or running job of mine references
  (scontrol's Command) goes after fourteen days, anything older than
  ninety days goes; then the cap: while inbox + snapshots + results + logs
  exceed 1 GiB the oldest unreferenced snapshot goes, then the oldest

BF#51 [OPEN]  FINDING: CLOSING THE GUI WINDOW MID-EPISODE LEAVES THE BRIDGE
      PROCESS SPINNING AT FULL CPU  (2026-10-30)
  WHAT: Hani's watch.ps1 run of 16:42 (seed 0, GUI) had its PyBullet
  window closed during the first pick ("Picking dice from dice..." is the
  run log's last line, then "X connection to :0 broken (explicit kill or
  server shutdown)" at 16:43:31).  The python process did not exit: at
  18:00 it was still alive under /init (its terminal gone), its main
  thread at 99 % CPU with no kernel wait channel, 1 h 20 min of CPU
  burnt, nothing written since 16:43:31.  That is PyBullet's GUI client
  after its graphics thread dies: the main thread busy-waits for a
  command status that never comes, so no Python code runs and nothing of
  ours can notice.  Killed by hand at 18:01 (kill, then kill -9).  Hani:
  "all gui runs are closed. is it an orphan run?"  Consequence: a closed
  GUI run keeps a core busy for ever and blocks the next pipeline run
  (the pddlstream temp/ rule), unseen.
  FIX CANDIDATES: (a) tools/watch.ps1 ends the python when the window
  goes: a watchdog that kills the process once its console_debug.log
  carries "X connection to :0 broken" (or once the X window is gone) and
  says so; (b) end GUI runs with Ctrl+C in the terminal, never by closing
  the window (a note in watch.ps1's header).  Not fixed; (a) is small.
  CLAIM EFFECT: none (GUI runs are never scored).  HONESTY CLASS:
  infrastructure (tooling).

  --search-initial-complexity (default 3), pddlstream solve() keyword
  arguments (pddlstream_planner.search_options_from, planner.
  search_options; tests/test_search_options.py).  WHY: the first call's
  cost is pddlstream's attempt loop (refinement.iterative_plan_streams).
  pddlstream weighs a stream result's effort into the optimistic search
  only with effort_weight set (scheduling/plan_streams.add_stream_efforts
  returns at once without it), so the search is blind to which optimistic
  objects it already refined and every attempt may pick a fresh unrefined
  alternative: seed 10's first call made 13 attempts, 11 s of search,
  each a FastDownward run on a problem of 341 to 1664 results.  With the
  weight an unrefined result costs opt_index x DEFAULT_SEARCH_OVERHEAD
  (1e2) more than a refined one and the second attempt takes the refined
  chain.  The starting complexity: every pick needs a grasp, a kin
  solution and a motion, three stream levels, so levels 0 to 2 never find
  a plan (and with the fold's real facts level 2 made five futile
  attempts, 4.4 s).

BF#59 [DONE, decisions open]  THE DIE-SLIP CAMPAIGN: SIX FIXES AS SWITCHES,
      EVERY ONE AND A PRE-REGISTERED SET ON THE CLUSTER, 3 REPETITIONS OF
      SEEDS 0-19, TAMPURA BESIDE  (user, 2026-10-31 to 2026-11-01)
  WHAT: Hani (2026-10-31): "tackle the problem of the slipping die once and
  for all ... the grasp samplers really just need to be that good. we make
  suggestions, review those, then implement and review implementations and
  then run eval ... utilize git, worktrees and clusters to test all
  improvements and fixes in parallel ... we are doing this scientifically,
  so at the end make a table of all the changes and if they actually
  performed ... suggest the best changes that should stay in and test a
  version with all of those in; all tests on the same seeds; TAMPURA on
  those seeds; report time; scene variance via replicated instances".
  THE PROBLEM: BF#53's per-fix eval lost the die in 21 % of its carries,
  always in the move home after a verified pick (the Panda's friction
  pinch of the 17.8 mm, 5.2 g die).
  THE WORKFLOW (archive/tampura_bridge_plan_2026-10-25/bf/BF59_slip/, the
  campaign folder: BRIEF.md, STATE.md the live state, proposals/, reviews/,
  probes/, eval/): (1) four Opus agents diagnosed, each from one angle
  with its own probes (grasp_geometry, hold_physics, carry_motion,
  loss_dynamics; proposals/<angle>.md); (2) four Opus reviewers reviewed
  them (reviews/<angle>.md); (3) the orchestrator cut the list to seven
  candidates, one mechanism per switch; (4) six were implemented by Opus
  agents in sibling worktrees sb-slip-<name> on branches slip/<name>, each
  with unit tests and tools/slip_check.sh (tests, bridge tests, static
  gates, one locked episode); (5) the orchestrator reviewed every
  implementation (reviews/implrev_<name>.md, all accepted; the seventh,
  pad_rolling, was dropped as a fallback not needed); (6) all six merged
  on slip/campaign (cff9bc5 = tampura-bridge 562db41 + the six), the
  all-on, all-off and injected-loss checks green; (7) the eval on the
  cluster; (8) this table.  Every fix is a switch of the registry
  slip_fixes.py (register/configure/on; `--slip-fixes a,b` of run_logger
  and the bridge driver; a name nobody registered refuses the run),
  default OFF; the physics switches bind through execute_fn's configure
  call before d9_setup; tests/test_slip_<name>.py per switch.
  THE DIAGNOSIS (the proposals and reviews, with the probes' numbers): the
  die rotates out of a near-face pinch during the swing home: the 0.5 mm
  position hold's force collapses to 0 N once the fingers stop inside the
  close's tolerance; the hold depends on the yaw between pads and faces
  (exact face 79-88 % held, 1-25 deg off 46-51 %, edge pinch 80-94 %) and
  the eye's 9.5 deg yaw noise cannot certify a face pinch; every carry
  home spins the wrist (joint 7) 168 to 242 deg because the world-frame
  yaw wrap picks the "smallest rotation" in the world, not on the arm, and
  a die that sits off its face turns out of the pinch; the waypoint
  junctions decelerate at 60 to 97 m/s^2; a 10 N force-limited hold needs
  200 solver iterations (at PyBullet's default 50 a convergence artefact
  expels the 5 g die) and coupled fingers (PyBullet ignores the URDF's
  mimic tag); the per-carry outcome is non-deterministic (one seed: held,
  lost, lost); the slow tail is a cup loop, not the die (the overhead
  refresh after a move overwrites the wrist observe's pose radius, 0.4 mm
  to 5.5 mm, the pick's commit check refuses, up to 90 plans until the
  cap).
  THE SIX SWITCHES: solver_iters: 200 contact-solver iterations per step
  on the executing world (boxel_env, d9_setup, re-applied after reset).
  force_hold: after the close, a force-limited position hold 4 mm inside
  the measured width at 10 N per finger, the fingers coupled by a
  JOINT_GEAR constraint (ratio -1, erp 0.1, 50 N); requires solver_iters
  (robot_utils).  pose_radius_keep: a consistent later detection keeps
  min(r_new, r_old + centre shift) and the wrist yaw over the eye yaw
  (pose_belief.record_detection).  grasp_wrist_home: the grasp sampler
  takes, of a top-down grasp yaw and its pad-swapped twin (yaw + pi), the
  one whose joint 7 is nearer home (tie band 5 deg, the joint limit
  2.8973 rad respected; pose_belief, streams).  carry_trapezoid: one
  acceleration-bounded schedule per move made while holding, collinear
  pairs merged, a rest at every direction change (test_full_pipeline's
  move action, execution).  carry_hold_check: after every move made while
  holding, both pads must touch the held body or the move fails as an
  empty hand and is replanned, the finger width growth logged (execution).
  THE EVAL (tools/slip_eval.py, tools/cluster/slip_eval.sbatch and
  slip_t.sbatch; snapshot sb_cff9bc5, code only, BF#56): arms = sets of
  switches on; base in every job; seeds 0-19 (TAMPURA's seeds, BF#9),
  which load only THREE scenes on the cluster (1747173590: seeds 5, 6, 10,
  17, 19; 1747173591: 0, 7, 9, 11, 12, 13, 16; 1747173593: 1, 2, 3, 4, 8,
  14, 15, 18), so every number below is 3 scenes x 3 repetitions x their
  seeds; 3 repetitions of every seed per arm (60 episodes) because the
  per-carry outcome is a near coin flip; 600 s cap per episode; carries =
  verified die picks (the curated "firm grip" line), losses = the lift
  loss, the carry loss and the goal check's physical loss (finger-pad
  contact incomplete or a graze), each counted once from the pipeline's
  run log (the harness double-counted twice before the eval, 74cffac and
  562db41, tests/test_slip_eval.py).  Five jobs on five idle rleap_cpu
  nodes, submitted 21:08 on 2026-10-31: A 5006486 cn-101 (base,
  solver_iters, solver_iters+force_hold; 5 h 18 min), B 5006487 cn-102
  (base, pose_radius_keep, pose_radius_keep+grasp_wrist_home; 5 h 40), C
  5006488 cn-103 (base, pose_radius_keep+grasp_wrist_home+carry_trapezoid,
  carry_hold_check; 6 h 38), D 5006489 cn-105 (base and the PRE-REGISTERED
  combined set, stated in STATE.md at 21:00 before any result:
  solver_iters+force_hold+pose_radius_keep+grasp_wrist_home; 2 h 49), T

  same seeds, _bridge_campaign.py --arms T; 2 h 50).  The records (one
  JSON line per episode), summaries, static gates and job logs are in
  eval/<job>/ (tracked); eval/slip_table.py builds eval/TABLE.md from
  them (Fisher's exact test two-sided, Clopper-Pearson intervals, paired
  times); a Sonnet agent recomputed every number independently from the
  records (623 cells, no discrepancy; its two readings were adopted: the
  planning and execution medians over finished episodes only, since a
  timed-out episode carries partial counters, and the p-values as
  descriptive, since the episodes share three scenes and the same seeds).
  THE TABLE (success of 60 episodes; T = timeouts at the cap; losses per

  FINDINGS: (a) solver_iters ALONE is harmful: with today's 0.5 mm

  69 refusals, 93 plans, 600 s.  The centre-shift term is the mechanism:
  under pose_radius_keep a cup whose eye and wrist centres disagree by

  3 mm disagreement between looks was noted at the implementation as the
