BF#60 [OPEN, FOR LATER]  ENTROPY SEARCH FOR THE SEARCH ORDER: NOT FOR THIS
      PAPER  (user decision, 2026-10-31 13:02)
  WHAT: Hani asked where entropy search could apply in this project.  The
  answer: the choice of which shadow fragment to search next, which
  nothing makes today.  Every sense costs 1 and optimistically asserts
  the target, so the plan goes to the fragment cheapest to reach (the
  sense comment in pddl/domain_pddlstream.pddl: the planner "cannot
  reason about search ORDER"), and every empty sense costs a full
  replan.  The shadow belief holds no probabilities, and nothing in the
  code computes an entropy or an information gain.
  SKETCH: (1) BeliefState (belief.py) keeps a probability p_i per
  fragment instead of a status, with a declared prior: uniform over the
  spots where a target-class body rests fully hidden.  streams.py's
  test_target_can_hide_in_shadow already samples those spots on a 1 cm
  grid and only reports whether one exists; counting them gives the
  weight (for the cups, task_spec.HOLLOW_CAVITY).  TAMPURA's belief, a
  grid of candidate voxels, is the nearest match to that prior.  A sense
  that sees a fraction v_i of a fragment and finds nothing leaves
  p_i (1 - v_i) / (1 - p_i v_i).  (2) The value of a sense enters as a
  cost inside the model, never as a reordering of a plan: PDDLStream
  takes per-grounding cost functions, (increase (total-cost) (SenseCost
  ?region)) with a :function stream (pddlstream_lib
  examples/advanced/multi_cost.py); the exchange rate between actions
  and probability is the one new constant, declared, not tuned.  (3) The
  3-strike park, #P9's unsound bounded give-up, becomes a residual-mass
  rule: the episode ends when the mass left is below a declared epsilon,
  which is then the stated risk of giving up on a target that is there;
  an unresolved fragment keeps its mass, and re-sensing it from the same
  view has zero expected gain.
  ENTROPY OR PROBABILITY: with a perfect sensor and no costs, entropy
  search and most-likely-first pick the same fragment.  With costs, bits
  per action is the wrong score for find-and-hold: of a 50 % fragment at
  4 actions and a 5 % one at 1, bits per action picks the 5 % one (0.29
  against 0.25), the optimal order for searching boxes with fixed costs
  (highest p/c first) the 50 % one.  So rank by p/c; entropy counts where
  a look sees only part of a fragment.  Two cases are on record: F12's
  withdrawn sense_partial (the belief could not record a partial look,
  so the planner repeated one that could teach it nothing until three
  strikes parked every fragment, seed 13) and TAMPURA's own loop (P4.2:
  "a look that reveals nothing is re-selected to step 20").
  LOWER VALUE: ranking ObserveViews' candidates by information gain (the
  wrist camera's yaw noise is already 0.2 deg); Entropy Search as
  Bayesian optimisation of the search settings (it reopens BF#57, could
  tune only on our own scenes, never on TAMPURA's 20 seeds, and competes
  with the B8 re-runs for the cluster).
  WHY LATER: find_dice cannot show it.  Its 20 seeds load three saved
  scenes (P4.2's map): one with a single cup, two with four cups and the
  die where the scene file puts it, so the order would change in two
  scenes only (the cavities do differ, 40 to 57 mm).  The test would be
  random-pairs, where the hidden target moves with the seed (empty senses
  and replans per episode).  It also changes the system under test while
  the B8 re-runs and CB#113 are pending, five weeks before the
  2026-12-04 deadline.
  DECISION 2026-10-31 (user): "log it but state that we concluded it is
  not for now, well look at it later, but not for this paper".  Not in
  the ICAPS 2027 paper; when it is picked up, a default-off switch like
  M8 to M10, on its own branch.
  CLAIM EFFECT: none for this paper.  HONESTY CLASS: to be decided then
  (a model tweak if built).

================================================================================
#P5. [OPEN T2]  CONSIDER ADOPTING TAMPURA'S CONTROLLERS
================================================================================
Supervisor suggestion: their controllers may simply work better.  What they are
(panda_utils/panda_env_utils.py, primitives.py): kinematic joint-interpolation
drivers (pick_execute/place_execute) over BiRRT motion plans (bundled
motion_planners package: functionally the same RRT-Connect + shortcut smoothing
we implement in streams/streams_boxel), plus the qualitatively different part:
get_grasp samples from a grasp LIBRARY (saved per-mesh JSON grasps, antipodal
mesh sampling via trimesh, or axis-aligned top grasps) validated by
close-until-collision at planning time.  Ours: single fixed top-down offset, but
with genuine runtime finger-contact verification (more honest, far less
expressive).  DECISION TO MAKE (with #P1's sampler work): adopt their grasp
library as the pose sampler (fits "use their controllers" AND "good pose
sampler"), keep our runtime contact verification, keep one motion planner
(theirs and ours are redundant: pick one).  Their kinematic executors conflict
with #P1's push toward real physics: adopting their grasp SAMPLING while keeping
our physical execution is the coherent combination.  Note their Panda differs:
EE frame panda_grasptarget link 14, fingers [12,13] (ours 11/[9,10]); the bridge
already resolves this by name (tampura_robot.build_robot_model).

================================================================================
#P9. [OPEN, STRETCH]  FULL POD PLANNING: LW1 + PDDLSTREAM, 3D WUMPUS
================================================================================
Far stretch, in principle interesting: replace the relaxation
(plan-execute-observe-replan over an optimistic determinization) with a genuine
POD planner: compile PDDLStream's sampled groundings into LW1 (Bonet & Geffner),
gaining branching contingent plans and deductive axioms.  Then a problem that
actually NEEDS it, e.g. a continuous/3D Wumpus-style domain.
WHY IT IS JUSTIFIED (the thesis's own disclosed gap, raised 3x by Till):
TH#238/#239/#248 (simplified POD, lacks deductive axioms, does not scale without
them); TH#307 (formal statement: we solve a single optimistic determinization
and never certify goal-belief containment b_G subset S_G as CLG/LW1 would);
TH#328 (pessimistic sensing would require exactly the LW1 axiom machinery we
lack; text reverted from thesis but the reasoning stands).  The unsound
3-strike bounded give-up (thesis §5 limitation) is the visible symptom.
EXISTING SPEC: CB#105 (Wumpus-World baseline: cell-belief search, no shadow
geometry) is the natural first rung and doubles as an ablation of the semantic
partition.  Treat #P9 as post-deadline research unless #P1-#P4 finish early;
for the December paper it is future work, at most a positioning paragraph.
